# 2.9.3 Bench

- [BBQ](https://arxiv.org/abs/2110.08193): Evaluates bias in question answering under ambiguous and disambiguated contexts. Core idea: diagnose whether models lean on social stereotypes or correctly abstain/use context, which remains a basic model-card safety signal even when the broader page focuses on agents.
- [Digger: Detecting Copyright Content Mis-usage in Large Language Model Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.00676): Benchmarks or evaluates agent safety capabilities with a reusable task, dataset, protocol, or metric.
- [GOAT-Bench: Safety Insights to Large Multimodal Models through Meme-Based Social Abuse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.01523): Benchmarks LMM safety awareness on more than 6K memes covering implicit hate speech, sexism, cyberbullying, offensiveness, sarcasm, and harmful content, with GPT-4o and other LMMs tested on whether they detect social abuse that is not explicit in text or image alone.
- [Escalation Risks from Language Models in Military and Diplomatic Decision-Making](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.03408): Benchmarks agent safety capabilities with a task, evaluation protocol, or diagnostic setting.
- [TrustLLM: Trustworthiness in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.05561) ([benchmark site](https://trustllmbenchmark.github.io/TrustLLM-Website/)): TrustLLM adds a safety bench for jailbreaks, privacy, guardrails, unlearning, or adversarial robustness.
- [How Johnny Can Persuade LLMs to Jailbreak Them: Rethinking Persuasion to Challenge AI Safety by Humanizing LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.06373): Evaluates agent safety through the paper's stated contribution.
- [Authorship Obfuscation in Multilingual Machine-Generated Text Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.07867): Adds an evaluation resource or protocol for Agent Safety capabilities.
- [AttackEval: How to Evaluate the Effectiveness of Jailbreak Attacking on Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.09002): Scores jailbreak prompt effectiveness rather than only target-model robustness, using coarse-grained and fine-grained 0-1 evaluation frameworks plus a ground-truth jailbreak-prompt dataset to identify harmful attacks that binary refusal metrics can miss.
- [R-Judge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.10019): Evaluates safety risk awareness in LLM agents from multi-turn interaction records. Core idea: judge whether trajectories contain risky agent behavior so safety assessment covers interaction history rather than only a final answer.
- [PsySafe: A Comprehensive Framework for Psychological-based Attack, Defense, and Evaluation of Multi-agent System Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.11880): Evaluates multi-agent safety through agent-psychology threat modeling, linking dark personality traits, psychological assessments, risky collective behavior, self-reflection during dangerous actions, and mitigation strategies in LLM-based multi-agent systems.
- [Spotting LLMs With Binoculars: Zero-Shot Detection of Machine-Generated Text](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.12070): Evaluates agent safety through the paper's stated contribution.
- [The Language Barrier: Dissecting Safety Challenges of LLMs in Multilingual Contexts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.13136): Benchmarks agent safety capabilities with a task, evaluation protocol, or diagnostic setting.
- [Under the Surface: Tracking the Artifactuality of LLM-Generated Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.14698): Benchmarks or evaluates agent safety capabilities with a reusable task, dataset, protocol, or metric.
- [LeftoverLocals: Listening to LLM Responses Through Leaked GPU Local Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.16603): Adds a bench entry for Agent Safety, because it exposes GPU local-memory leakage from LLM responses.
- [Weak-to-Strong Jailbreaking on Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.17256): Studies weak-to-strong jailbreaking, adding a safety-evaluation case for model-scale transfer attacks.
- [Detecting AI-Generated Video via Frame Consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02085): Provides a benchmark, dataset, challenge, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [Jailbreaking Attack against Multimodal Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02309): Evaluates agent safety through the paper's stated contribution.
- [Reconstruct Your Previous Conversations! Comprehensively Investigating Privacy Leakage Risks in Conversations with GPT Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02987): Adds a bench entry for Agent Safety, because it investigates conversational privacy leakage risks in GPT-style models.
- [HarmBench: A Standardized Evaluation Framework for Automated Red Teaming and Robust Refusal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.04249) ([project page](https://www.harmbench.org/)): HarmBench adds a safety bench for jailbreaks, privacy, guardrails, unlearning, or adversarial robustness.
- [SALAD-Bench: A Hierarchical and Comprehensive Safety Benchmark for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05044): Provides a hierarchical comprehensive safety benchmark for large language models.
- [Assessing the Brittleness of Safety Alignment via Pruning and Low-Rank Modifications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05162): Evaluates safety, alignment, watermarking, unlearning, or attack-defense capability, adding a benchmark, dataset, metric, or empirical protocol for parent review.
- [JailbreakRadar: Comprehensive Assessment of Jailbreak Attacks Against LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05668): Benchmarks agent safety capabilities with a task, evaluation protocol, or diagnostic setting.
- [Can LLMs Recognize Toxicity? A Structured Investigation Framework and Toxicity Metric](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.06900): Adds a benchmark, dataset, challenge, metric, or evaluation protocol for agent safety.
- [Do Membership Inference Attacks Work on Large Language Models?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.07841): Evaluates agent safety through the paper's stated contribution.
- [Instruction Backdoor Attacks Against Customized LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09179): Evaluates agent safety through the paper's stated contribution.
- [Copyright Traps for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09363): Benchmarks or evaluates agent safety capabilities with a reusable task, dataset, protocol, or metric.
- [PANORAMIA: Privacy Auditing of Machine Learning Models without Retraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09477): Audits privacy leakage of machine-learning models without retraining.
- [DE-COP: Detecting Copyrighted Content in Language Models Training Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09910): Tests whether a language model has seen copyrighted material by comparing generated continuations against protected passages, making training-data provenance auditable without direct access to the training set.
- [Enhancing Role-playing Systems through Aggressive Queries: Evaluation and Improvement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10618): Evaluates agent safety capability with a benchmark, dataset, challenge, metric, or diagnostic protocol suggested by the title.
- [ToolSword](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10753): Evaluates safety issues in tool learning across input, execution, and output stages. Core idea: test whether tool-using models can recognize malicious or unsafe tool contexts before tool access turns text errors into external effects.
- [M4GT-Bench: Evaluation Benchmark for Black-Box Machine-Generated Text Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11175): Provides a multilingual, multi-domain, multi-generator corpus for black-box machine-generated-text detection, with tasks for binary detection, generator attribution, and locating human-machine text boundaries, plus baseline and human evaluations.
- [Watch Out for Your Agents! Investigating Backdoor Threats to LLM-Based Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11208): Evaluates backdoor attacks on LLM agents by inserting triggers into agent-facing instructions or environments and measuring whether the compromised agent executes attacker-chosen behavior during tool-mediated tasks.
- [LEMMA: Towards LVLM-Enhanced Multimodal Misinformation Detection with External Knowledge Augmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11943): Benchmarks or evaluates agent safety capabilities with a reusable task, dataset, protocol, or metric.
- [Emulated Disalignment: Safety Alignment for Large Language Models May Backfire!](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12343): Tests whether safety tuning can be inverted by asking aligned models to emulate misaligned personas, exposing cases where refusal behavior collapses under role or instruction framing.
- [A Critical Evaluation of AI Feedback for Aligning Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12366): Audits AI-feedback alignment by comparing model-generated preference or critique signals with human judgments, highlighting when automated feedback reinforces shallow or unreliable safety preferences.
- [Potential and Challenges of Model Editing for Social Debiasing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.13462): Adds an evaluation resource or protocol for Agent Safety capabilities.
- [Is LLM-as-a-Judge Robust? Investigating Universal Adversarial Attacks on Zero-shot LLM Assessment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14016): Benchmarks agent safety capabilities with a task, evaluation protocol, or diagnostic setting.
- [Eagle: Ethical Dataset Given from Real Interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14258): Builds an ethics dataset from real user interactions so models can be tested on concrete value-conflict and policy-compliance cases rather than only synthetic moral dilemmas.
- [How (un)ethical are instruction-centric responses of LLMs? Unveiling the vulnerabilities of safety guardrails to harmful queries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.15302): Benchmarks or evaluates agent safety capabilities with a reusable task, dataset, protocol, or metric.
- [Fast Adversarial Attacks on Language Models In One GPU Minute](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.15570): Evaluates agent safety through the paper's stated contribution.
- [Generalization or Memorization: Data Contamination and Trustworthy Evaluation for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.15938): Benchmarks agent safety capabilities with a task, evaluation protocol, or diagnostic setting.
- [Likelihood-based Mitigation of Evaluation Bias in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.15987): Diagnoses likelihood bias in LLM-as-evaluator settings by testing data-to-text and grammatical-error-correction judgments, then measures whether few-shot examples from highly biased cases improve correlation with human scores.
- [CodeChameleon: Personalized Encryption Framework for Jailbreaking Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.16717): Attacks LLM guardrails by encrypting or encoding harmful requests into model-personalized representations, then measures whether the model decodes and follows the hidden unsafe instruction.
- [Towards Tracing Trustworthiness Dynamics: Revisiting Pre-training Period of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.19465): Tracks how trustworthiness signals evolve during pre-training checkpoints, connecting safety, bias, toxicity, robustness, and privacy behavior to training-stage dynamics rather than only final-model evaluation.
- [Dialect prejudice predicts AI decisions about people's character, employability, and criminality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.00742): Measures dialect bias in LLM judgments of speakers, showing that African American English cues can shift model assessments of character, jobs, and criminality even without explicit identity labels.
- [NewsBench: Systematic Evaluation of LLMs for Writing Proficiency and Safety Adherence in Chinese Journalistic Editorial Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.00862): Provides a benchmark, dataset, or evaluation protocol for agent safety capability.
- [Gradient Cuff: Detecting Jailbreak Attacks on Large Language Models by Exploring Refusal Loss Landscapes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.00867): Detects jailbreak attempts by probing refusal-loss landscapes, using gradient-derived signals to distinguish adversarial prompts from benign requests before unsafe completion.
- [Inexact Unlearning Needs More Careful Evaluations to Avoid a False Sense of Privacy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.01218): Evaluates agent safety through the paper's stated contribution.
- [InjecAgent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.02691): Benchmarks indirect prompt-injection attacks in tool-integrated LLM agents. Core idea: place attacker instructions in external observations or tool data and measure whether agents execute them despite benign user goals.
- [Angry Men, Sad Women: Large Language Models Reflect Gendered Stereotypes in Emotion Attribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03121): Evaluates gendered stereotypes in large language models by measuring biased emotion attribution patterns.
- [The WMDP Benchmark: Measuring and Reducing Malicious Use With Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03218): The WMDP Benchmark adds a safety bench for jailbreaks, privacy, guardrails, unlearning, or adversarial robustness.
- [A Safe Harbor for AI Evaluation and Red Teaming](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.04893): Classification concern: this is a policy proposal rather than a benchmark, arguing that AI developers should provide legal and technical safe harbor so independent evaluators can red-team systems without account or legal retaliation.
- [AMUSE: Adaptive Multi-Segment Encoding for Dataset Watermarking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.05628): Adds a agent safety benchmark, dataset, metric, or evaluation protocol.
- [Stealing Part of a Production Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.06634): Studies partial stealing of production language models, adding a model-extraction safety evaluation.
- [Exploring Safety Generalization Challenges of Large Language Models via Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.07865): Introduces CodeAttack, which rewrites harmful natural-language requests as code-completion inputs and tests GPT-4, Claude-2, and Llama-2-family models; the study reports guardrail bypasses above 80%, exposing weak safety generalization from natural language to code.
- [VIGFace: Virtual Identity Generation for Privacy-Free Face Recognition Dataset](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.08277): Generates synthetic face identities from orthogonal feature-space prototypes, aiming to reduce privacy and portrait-rights risk in face-recognition training while preserving benchmark performance.
- [Evaluating Robustness of Generative Search Engine on Adversarial Factual Questions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.12077): Adds an evaluation resource or protocol for Agent Safety capabilities.
- [OpenEval: Benchmarking Chinese LLMs across Capability, Alignment and Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.12316): Adds an evaluation resource or protocol for Agent Safety capabilities.
- [An Entropy-based Text Watermarking Detection Method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.13485): Evaluates agent safety through the paper's stated contribution.
- [Investigating Bias in LLM-Based Bias Detection: Disparities between LLMs and Human Perception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.14896): Compares LLM bias-detection judgments with human perception, exposing where automated bias monitors disagree with people on what counts as biased language.
- [Risk and Response in Large Language Models: Evaluating Key Threat Categories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.14988): Uses the Anthropic red-team dataset to compare how reward models and LLM responses treat information hazards, malicious uses, and discrimination or hateful content, including jailbreak vulnerability in information-hazard scenarios.
- [As Good as a Coin Toss: Human Detection of AI-Generated Content](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.16760): Measures how well humans identify AI-generated content and uses the near-chance detection result as a risk signal for provenance, disclosure, and misuse evaluation.
- [Assessment of Multimodal Large Language Models in Alignment with Human Values](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.17830): Evaluates MLLM alignment with human values through multimodal safety and value-judgment cases, checking whether visual context changes harmfulness, fairness, or ethical responses.
- [IndiBias: A Benchmark Dataset to Measure Social Biases in Language Models for Indian Context](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.20147): Introduces an Indian-context social-bias benchmark so LLMs can be tested on caste, religion, region, gender, and other locally salient stereotypes absent from Western-centric bias suites.
- [JailbreakBench: An Open Robustness Benchmark for Jailbreaking Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01318) ([leaderboard](https://jailbreakbench.github.io/)): Standardizes jailbreak robustness testing with a curated harmful-behavior dataset, reference attack artifacts, refusal/violation evaluation, and a public leaderboard so attack and defense papers can be compared under a shared protocol.
- [Which Model Generated This Image? A Model-Agnostic Approach for Origin Attribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.02697): Provides a benchmark, dataset, challenge, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [JailBreakV: A Benchmark for Assessing the Robustness of MultiModal Large Language Models against Jailbreak Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.03027): Benchmarks agent safety capabilities with a task, evaluation protocol, or diagnostic setting.
- [Online Safety Analysis for LLMs: A Benchmark, an Assessment, and a Path Forward](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.08517): Establishes a benchmark for detecting unsafe LLM outputs during generation rather than after completion, comparing online safety-analysis methods across models, tasks, datasets, and metrics after a pilot study on early unsafe-output detection.
- [ALERT: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Assessing Large Language Models' Safety through Red Teaming](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.08676): Evaluates safety, alignment, watermarking, unlearning, or attack-defense capability, adding a benchmark, dataset, metric, or empirical protocol for parent review.
- [A Reliable Cryptographic Framework for Empirical Machine Unlearning Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.11577): Frames unlearning evaluation as a cryptographic game between an unlearning algorithm and membership-inference adversaries, yielding a provable metric for whether targeted training samples were removed.
- [AdvisorQA: Towards Helpful and Harmless Advice-seeking Question Answering with Collective Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.11826): Adds a bench reference for agent safety, with a reusable contribution signaled by the title rather than a narrow application-only result.
- [Introducing v0.5 of the AI Safety Benchmark from MLCommons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.12241): Documents MLCommons AI Safety Benchmark v0.5 for chat-tuned language-model systems, specifying an adult-assistant English use case, user personas, 13 hazard categories, 7 tested categories, and 43,090 prompt test items while warning that v0.5 is a trial release.
- [Typos that Broke the RAG's Back: Genetic Attack on RAG Pipeline by Simulating Documents in the Wild via Low-level Perturbations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.13948): Introduces GARAG, a genetic attack that applies low-level textual perturbations to retrieved documents and evaluates retriever, generator, and end-to-end RAG robustness on QA datasets under realistic noisy-document conditions.
- [Competition Report: Finding Universal Jailbreak Backdoors in Aligned LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.14461): Summarizes the IEEE SaTML 2024 competition on universal jailbreak backdoor strings, where participants searched for trigger tokens that make otherwise aligned LLMs answer harmful prompts after safety-training poisoning.
- [Talking Nonsense: Probing Large Language Models' Understanding of Adversarial Gibberish Inputs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.17120): Adds an evaluation resource or protocol for Agent Safety capabilities.
- [From Languages to Geographies: Towards Evaluating Cultural Bias in Hate Speech Datasets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.17874): Benchmarks or evaluates agent safety capabilities with a reusable task, dataset, protocol, or metric.
- [UnsafeBench: Benchmarking Image Safety Classifiers on Real-World and AI-Generated Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.03486): Adds a task or dataset for evaluating agent safety, extending the Agent Safety / Bench coverage with a concrete measurement target.
- [The Dark Side of Dataset Scaling: Evaluating Racial Classification in Multimodal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.04623): Benchmarks or evaluates agent safety capabilities with a reusable task, dataset, protocol, or metric.
- [RAID: A Shared Benchmark for Robust Evaluation of Machine-Generated Text Detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.07940): Stress-tests machine-generated-text detectors with over 6M generations spanning 11 generators, 8 domains, 11 adversarial attacks, and 4 decoding strategies, then compares open and closed detectors under out-of-domain and adversarial shifts.
- [PolygloToxicityPrompts: Multilingual Evaluation of Neural Toxic Degeneration in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.09373): Provides 425K naturally occurring prompts across 17 languages for toxicity-degeneration testing, showing that toxicity increases for lower-resource languages and larger models despite instruction tuning.
- [Benchmark Early and Red Team Often: A Framework for Assessing and Managing Dual-Use Hazards of AI Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.10986): Classification concern: this is mainly a risk-management framework, recommending early benchmark design and repeated red-team cycles for dual-use hazards rather than releasing a single fixed dataset.
- [An Assessment of Model-On-Model Deception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.12999): Builds more than 10K misleading MMLU explanations generated by Llama-2 variants and GPT-3.5, then measures how strongly other models are deceived by wrong-answer justifications.
- [How to Trace Latent Generative Model Generated Images without Artificial Watermark?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.13360): Evaluates tracing of latent-generative-model images without artificial watermarks.
- [S-Eval: Towards Automated and Comprehensive Safety Evaluation for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14191): Evaluates agent safety capabilities with a benchmark, dataset, or assessment protocol.
- [Redundant Semantic Environment Filling via Misleading-Learning for Fair Deepfake Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15173): Adds an evaluation, dataset, or measurement signal for agent and model safety.
- [Evaluating and Safeguarding the Adversarial Robustness of Retrieval-Based In-Context Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15984): Adds an evaluation, dataset, or measurement signal for agent and model safety.
- [Automatic Jailbreaking of the Text-to-Image Generative AI Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.16567): Studies automatic jailbreaking attacks against text-to-image generative systems.
- [Navigating the Safety Landscape: Measuring Risks in Finetuning Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17374): Measures how fine-tuning choices change LLM risk profiles, comparing post-finetuning safety behavior so benign adaptation is not assumed to preserve guardrails.
- [Stress-Testing Capability Elicitation With Password-Locked Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.19550): Benchmarks or evaluates agent safety capabilities with a reusable task, dataset, protocol, or metric.
- [Black-Box Detection of Language Model Watermarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.20777): Studies whether language-model watermarks can be detected from generated text alone, turning watermark presence into a black-box provenance and authenticity test.
- [OR-Bench: An Over-Refusal Benchmark for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.20947): OR-Bench adds a safety bench for jailbreaks, privacy, guardrails, unlearning, or adversarial robustness.
- [AI-Face: A Million-Scale Demographically Annotated AI-Generated Face Dataset and Fairness Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.00783): Releases a million-scale generated-face dataset with demographic annotations, enabling fairness audits of synthetic face generation and downstream face-analysis models.
- [JBBQ: Japanese Bias Benchmark for Analyzing Social Biases in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.02050): Adapts bias-question evaluation to Japanese social categories and language use, testing whether LLMs reproduce stereotypes under ambiguous and disambiguated Japanese contexts.
- [Chaos with Keywords: Exposing Large Language Models Sycophancy to Misleading Keywords and Evaluating Defense Strategies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.03827): Exposes large language model sycophancy to misleading keywords and evaluates defense strategies.
- [BEADs: Bias Evaluation Across Domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.04220): Introduces a gold-annotated bias dataset spanning text classification, token classification, bias quantification, and benign generation, exposing demographic bias and inconsistent guardrail behavior across LLMs.
- [MoralBench: Moral Evaluation of LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.04428): Adds a task or dataset for evaluating agent safety, extending the Agent Safety / Bench coverage with a concrete measurement target.
- [CRiskEval: A Chinese Multi-Level Risk Evaluation Benchmark Dataset for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.04752): Builds a Chinese risk-evaluation benchmark with multi-level harmfulness labels so LLM safety can be scored on nuanced Chinese-language risk categories.
- [MultiTrust: A Comprehensive Benchmark Towards Trustworthy Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.07057): Adds a task or dataset for evaluating agent safety, extending the Agent Safety / Bench coverage with a concrete measurement target.
- [MBBQ: A Dataset for Cross-Lingual Comparison of Stereotypes in Generative LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.07243): Extends BBQ-style stereotype tests across languages, allowing direct comparison of how generative LLMs handle ambiguous social-bias questions in different linguistic settings.
- [AI Sandbagging: Language Models can Strategically Underperform on Evaluations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.07358): Benchmarks agent safety capabilities with a task, evaluation protocol, or diagnostic setting.
- [MLLMGuard: A Multi-dimensional Safety Evaluation Suite for Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.07594): Releases a bilingual image-text safety suite for MLLMs, with human-curated red-team data and a lightweight evaluator across privacy, bias, toxicity, truthfulness, and legality in English and Chinese.
- [JailbreakEval: An Integrated Toolkit for Evaluating Jailbreak Attempts Against Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.09321): Provides a toolkit for running jailbreak attacks and judging attack success, separating prompt generation, target-model querying, and safety-evaluator choices so jailbreak results are reproducible.
- [Bag of Tricks: Benchmarking of Jailbreak Attacks on LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.09324): Adds a task or dataset for evaluating agent safety, extending the Agent Safety / Bench coverage with a concrete measurement target.
- [RWKU: Benchmarking Real-World Knowledge Unlearning for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10890): Benchmarks whether LLMs can forget real-world knowledge while retaining neighboring facts and general utility, making unlearning evaluation less dependent on synthetic memorization tasks.
- [SPA-VL: A Comprehensive Safety Preference Alignment Dataset for Vision Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12030): Benchmarks agent safety capabilities with a task, evaluation protocol, or diagnostic setting.
- [Who's asking? User personas and the mechanics of latent misalignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12094): Tests latent misalignment by varying user personas and request framing, showing that models can answer differently when the same task is attributed to different users.
- [The Comparative Trap: Pairwise Comparisons Amplifies Biased Preferences of LLM Evaluators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12319): Adds an evaluation resource or protocol for Agent Safety capabilities.
- [MultiSocial: Multilingual Benchmark of Machine-Generated Text Detection of Social-Media Texts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12549): Provides 472,097 social-media texts across 22 languages, 5 platforms, and 7 multilingual generators to test machine-generated text detectors on short informal posts.
- [SHIELD: Evaluation and Defense Strategies for Copyright Compliance in LLM Text Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12975): Evaluates and defends copyright compliance in large-language-model text generation.
- [Large-Scale Dataset Pruning in Adversarial Training through Data Importance Extrapolation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.13283): Classification concern: this is an adversarial-training efficiency method, using extrapolated data-importance scores to prune training sets while preserving robustness rather than introducing a safety benchmark.
- [AgentDojo](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.13352) ([open-source code](https://github.com/ethz-spylab/agentdojo); [project page](https://agentdojo.spylab.ai/); [results](https://agentdojo.spylab.ai/results)): Evaluates prompt-injection and utility tradeoffs in tool-using agents. Core idea: run benign user tasks and malicious third-party tool or data instructions in the same environment so defenses must preserve task utility while blocking indirect attacks.
- [Watching the Watchers: a Comparative Fairness Audit of Cloud-based Content Moderation Services](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14154): Adds an evaluation, dataset, or measurement signal for agent and model safety.
- [VLBiasBench: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Evaluating Bias in Large Vision-Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14194): Adds an evaluation resource or protocol for Agent Safety capabilities.
- [Towards Truthful Multilingual Large Language Models: Benchmarking and Alignment Strategies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14434): Evaluates truthfulness across multilingual prompts and studies alignment strategies that reduce false or unsupported answers outside English-centric safety settings.
- [SORRY-Bench: Systematically Evaluating Large Language Model Safety Refusal Behaviors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14598) ([project page](https://sorry-bench.github.io/)): Benchmarks agent safety capabilities with a task, evaluation protocol, or diagnostic setting.
- [MU-Bench: A Multitask Multimodal Benchmark for Machine Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14796): Evaluates agent safety capability with a benchmark, dataset, challenge, metric, or diagnostic protocol suggested by the title.
- [Safe Inputs but Unsafe Output: Benchmarking Cross-modality Safety Alignment of Large Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.15279): Adds a bench entry for Agent Safety, because it benchmarks cross-modality safety alignment of large vision-language models.
- [Code-Switching Red-Teaming: LLM Evaluation for Safety and Multilingual Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.15481): Benchmarks or evaluates agent safety capabilities with a reusable task, dataset, protocol, or metric.
- [PUDD: Towards Robust Multi-modal Prototype-based Deepfake Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.15921): Adds an evaluation resource or protocol for Agent Safety capabilities.
- [Blind Baselines Beat Membership Inference Attacks for Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16201): Re-evaluates membership-inference attacks for foundation models against blind baselines, warning that apparent privacy attacks can be weaker than simple prior-only predictors.
- [Evaluating Copyright Takedown Methods for Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.18664): Defines CoTaEval to assess language-model copyright takedown methods on protected-content suppression, retention of uncopyrightable factual knowledge, general utility, and efficiency across prompt, decoding-filter, and unlearning interventions.
- [Glue pizza and eat rocks - Exploiting Vulnerabilities in Retrieval-Augmented Generative Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.19417): Tests black-box RAG poisoning by uploading crafted content to an open retrieval database without knowing user queries, model weights, or existing corpus contents, then measuring whether retrieval steers generated answers toward attacker-chosen claims.
- [MMEvalPro: Calibrating Multimodal Benchmarks Towards Trustworthy and Efficient Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.00468): Calibrates multimodal multiple-choice evaluation by expanding each original question into perception, knowledge-anchor, and reasoning checks, yielding 2,138 question triples and metrics that reduce Type-I errors from language-only shortcuts.
- [GenderBias-VL: Benchmarking Gender Bias in Vision Language Models via Counterfactual Probing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.00600): Adds an evaluation resource or protocol for Agent Safety capabilities.
- [Can Small Language Models Learn, Unlearn, and Retain Noise Patterns?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.00996): Evaluates agent safety through the paper's stated contribution.
- [Explaining Length Bias in LLM-Based Preference Evaluations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.01085): Diagnoses why LLM judges prefer longer answers in pairwise evaluation, linking verbosity bias to preference-model reliability and alignment-evaluation distortions.
- [Language Model Alignment in Multilingual Trolley Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.02273): Uses trolley-problem variants in multiple languages to test whether moral preferences and alignment behavior remain stable under translation and cultural framing.
- [JailbreakHunter: A Visual Analytics Approach for Jailbreak Prompts Discovery From Large-Scale Human-LLM Conversational Datasets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.03045): Provides a visual analytics workflow for discovering private jailbreak prompts in large human-LLM conversation logs, targeting multi-turn attack patterns that ordinary reported-prompt collections miss.
- [Social Bias Evaluation for Large Language Models Requires Prompt Variations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.03129): Adds a task or dataset for evaluating agent safety, extending the Agent Safety / Bench coverage with a concrete measurement target.
- [Beyond Perplexity: Multi-dimensional Safety Evaluation of LLM Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.04965): Tests compressed LLMs beyond perplexity by measuring degeneration harms, representational bias, dialect bias, and downstream performance under unstructured pruning, semi-structured pruning, and quantization.
- [MUSE: Machine Unlearning Six-Way Evaluation for Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.06460): Defines a six-way evaluation suite for machine unlearning in language models.
- [CopyBench: Measuring Literal and Non-Literal Reproduction of Copyright-Protected Text in Language Model Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.07087): Measures literal and non-literal reproduction of copyright-protected text in language-model outputs.
- [Multilingual Blending: LLM Safety Alignment Evaluation with Language Mixture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.07342): Evaluates agent safety capabilities with a benchmark, dataset, or assessment protocol.
- [Malicious Path Manipulations via Exploitation of Representation Vulnerabilities of Vision-Language Navigation Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.07392): Adds a bench entry for Agent Safety, because it studies representation-vulnerability attacks in vision-language navigation.
- [AgentPoison](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.12784): Evaluates memory and knowledge-base poisoning attacks against LLM agents. Core idea: inject adversarial content into long-term memory or RAG stores and test whether later agent decisions retrieve and act on the poisoned state.
- [Cross-Task Attack: A Self-Supervision Generative Framework Based on Attention Shift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.13700): Provides a benchmark, dataset, challenge, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [BIGbench: A Unified Benchmark for Evaluating Multi-dimensional Social Biases in Text-to-Image Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.15240): Adds a bench reference for agent safety, with a reusable contribution signaled by the title rather than a narrow application-only result.
- [AIR-Bench 2024: A Safety Benchmark Based on Risk Categories from Regulations and Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.17436): Adds a task or dataset for evaluating agent safety, extending the Agent Safety / Bench coverage with a concrete measurement target.
- [Can Editing LLMs Inject Harm?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.20224): Tests whether model-editing operations can introduce harmful behaviors while changing target facts, treating editing as a safety attack surface rather than only a knowledge-correction tool.
- [BadRobot](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.20242): Evaluates jailbreak risks for embodied LLM agents in physical-world settings. Core idea: test whether malicious natural-language interactions can bypass safety constraints and induce unsafe embodied actions.
- [LocalValueBench: A Collaboratively Built and Extensible Benchmark for Evaluating Localized Value Alignment and Ethical Safety in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.01460): Adds a bench reference for agent safety, with a reusable contribution signaled by the title rather than a narrow application-only result.
- [Reasons to Doubt the Impact of AI Risk Evaluations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.02565): Classification concern: this is a critique of AI risk-evaluation impact, arguing that evaluation results may fail to change deployment decisions unless incentives, governance hooks, and enforcement channels are addressed.
- [WalledEval: A Comprehensive Safety Evaluation Toolkit for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03837): Aggregates more than 35 LLM safety benchmarks, adds text-style mutators such as tense shifts and paraphrases, and introduces WalledGuard plus SGXSTest and HIXSTest for exaggerated-safety testing.
- [Nob-MIAs: Non-biased Membership Inference Attacks Assessment on Large Language Models with Ex-Post Dataset Construction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.05968): Rebuilds membership-inference evaluation sets under an ex-post assumption, creating non-biased and non-classifiable member/non-member splits for Gutenberg data and showing how time-shift and n-gram overlap biases can inflate MIA claims on OpenLLaMA and Pythia.
- [MMJ-Bench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.08464): Evaluates jailbreak attacks and defenses for vision-language models under a unified pipeline. Core idea: compare multimodal attack and defense methods with shared datasets and metrics instead of incompatible one-off safety tests.
- [LLMs Are Biased Towards Output Formats! Systematically Evaluating and Mitigating Output Format Bias of LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.08656): Adds an evaluation resource or protocol for Agent Safety capabilities.
- [PsychoLex: Unveiling the Psychological Mind of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.08848): Evaluates LLM psychological-trait inferences with a lexicon-style probing setup, treating personality and mental-state portrayal as a safety signal for anthropomorphic or manipulative assistant behavior.
- [Characterizing and Evaluating the Reliability of LLMs against Jailbreak Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.09326): Runs 10 jailbreak strategies from three attack families over 1,525 harmful questions, 61 harm categories, and 13 LLMs, aggregating ASR, toxicity, fluency, length, and grammatical-error metrics into a jailbreak reliability score.
- [Towards Robust Knowledge Unlearning: An Adversarial Framework for Assessing and Improving Unlearning Robustness in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.10682): Evaluates safety, alignment, watermarking, unlearning, or attack-defense capability, adding a benchmark, dataset, metric, or empirical protocol for parent review.
- [V-RoAst: Visual Road Assessment Can VLM be a Road Safety Assessor using the iRAP Standard?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.10872): Adds an evaluation, dataset, or measurement signal for agent and model safety.
- [BackdoorLLM: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Backdoor Attacks on Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.12798): Introduces or analyzes a benchmark, dataset, or evaluation protocol for agent safety, guardrails, and adversarial risk.
- [Systematic Evaluation of LLM-as-a-Judge in LLM Alignment Tasks: Explainable Metrics and Diverse Prompt Templates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.13006): Defines interpretable reliability metrics for LLM judges, separates judge internal inconsistency from human-alignment quality, and tests prompt-template effects on TL;DR Summarization and HH-RLHF-Helpfulness comparisons.
- [Emerging Vulnerabilities in Frontier Models: Multi-Turn Jailbreak Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.00137): Adds a bench entry for Agent Safety, because it studies multi-turn jailbreak vulnerabilities in frontier models.
- [PrivacyLens](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.00138): Evaluates privacy norm awareness in LLM-agent actions. Core idea: turn contextual privacy norms into trajectory-level tests so agents are judged on whether their actions leak or misuse sensitive information.
- [Automatic Pseudo-Harmful Prompt Generation for Evaluating False Refusals in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.00598): Builds PHTest with automatically generated pseudo-harmful but harmless prompts, labels controversial cases separately, and evaluates 20 LLMs on false refusals and their tradeoff with jailbreak robustness.
- [Polyrating: A Cost-Effective and Bias-Aware Rating System for LLM Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.00696): Introduces a MAP-based rating system that estimates LLM evaluation scores while detecting preference biases, reducing human-evaluation cost by reusing benchmark scores across new models and tasks.
- [Conversational complexity for assessing risk in large language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.01247): Proposes Conversational Length and approximate Conversational Complexity to quantify how much multi-turn effort is needed to elicit harmful content, then applies the measures to a large red-teaming dataset.
- [Content moderation by LLM: from accuracy to legitimacy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.03219): Benchmarks or evaluates agent safety capabilities with a reusable task, dataset, protocol, or metric.
- [Games for AI Control: Models of Safety Evaluations of AI Deployment Protocols](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.07985): Formalizes AI-control red teaming as partially observable stochastic games, then uses the model to evaluate and synthesize deployment protocols for untrusted coding assistants with trusted monitors and limited human oversight.
- [Unleashing Worms and Extracting Data: Escalating the Outcome of Attacks against RAG-based Inference in Scale and Severity Using Jailbreaking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.08045): Shows how jailbreaks can escalate RAG membership/entity extraction into document extraction, reporting 80%-99.8% database extraction in chatbot settings and scaling poisoning attacks across RAG applications.
- [AI-LieDar: Examine the Trade-off Between Utility and Truthfulness in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.09013): Adds an evaluation resource or protocol for Agent Safety capabilities.
- [ValueCompass: A Framework for Measuring Contextual Value Alignment Between Human and LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.09586): Grounds value-alignment measurement in psychological value theory and a systematic review, then compares humans and LLMs across collaborative writing, education, public-sector, and healthcare scenarios.
- [Towards Data Contamination Detection for Modern Large Language Models: Limitations, Inconsistencies, and Oracle Challenges](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.09927): Adds an evaluation resource or protocol for Agent Safety capabilities.
- [ESPERANTO: Evaluating Synthesized Phrases to Enhance Robustness in AI Detection for Text Origination](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.14285): Uses multi-language back-translation to manipulate AI-generated text and test whether text-origin detectors remain robust when generated prose is paraphrased through synthesized phrase variants.
- [RoWSFormer: A Robust Watermarking Framework with Swin Transformer for Enhanced Geometric Attack Resilience](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.14829): Adds a bench entry for agent safety, centered on safety, security, risk, or alignment contribution.
- [Attack Atlas: A Practitioner's Perspective on Challenges and Pitfalls in Red Teaming GenAI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.15398): Evaluates safety, alignment, watermarking, unlearning, or attack-defense capability, adding a benchmark, dataset, metric, or empirical protocol for parent review.
- [Leveraging Mixture of Experts for Improved Speech Deepfake Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.16077): Adds an evaluation resource or protocol for Agent Safety capabilities.
- [Holistic Automated Red Teaming for Large Language Models through Top-Down Test Case Generation and Multi-turn Interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.16783): Adds an evaluation resource or protocol for Agent Safety capabilities.
- [Freeze and Learn: Continual Learning with Selective Freezing for Speech Deepfake Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.17598): Adds an evaluation, dataset, or measurement signal for agent and model safety.
- [Evading Toxicity Detection with ASCII-art: A Benchmark of Spatial Attacks on Moderation Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.18708): Introduces ToxASCII, a spatial-obfuscation benchmark that encodes toxic text as ASCII art and reports attack success against LLMs and dedicated moderation tools.
- [GenTel-Safe: A Unified Benchmark and Shielding Framework for Defending Against Prompt Injection Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.19521): Evaluates agent safety capability with a benchmark, dataset, challenge, metric, or diagnostic protocol suggested by the title.
- [Multimodal Misinformation Detection by Learning from Synthetic Data with Multimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.19656): Adds an evaluation resource or protocol for Agent Safety capabilities.
- [T2Vs Meet VLMs: A Scalable Multimodal Dataset for Visual Harmfulness Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.19734): Releases VHD11K with 10K images and 1K videos from web and generative sources across 10 harm categories, using a multi-agent VQA annotation workflow to evaluate visual harmfulness recognition in VLMs.
- [Does RAG Introduce Unfairness in LLMs? Evaluating Fairness in Retrieval-Augmented Generation Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.19804): Defines a scenario-question fairness framework for RAG, measuring demographic disparities across gender, geography, and other attributes in both retrieval and generation stages of open-domain QA systems.
- [Measurement challenges in AI catastrophic risk governance and safety frameworks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.00608): Provides a benchmark, dataset, or evaluation protocol for agent safety capability.
- [Agent Security Bench / ASB](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02644) ([open-source code](https://github.com/agiresearch/ASB); [project page](https://luckfort.github.io/ASBench/)): Benchmarks security attacks and defenses in LLM-based agents. Core idea: cover direct and indirect prompt injection, memory poisoning, backdoors, and tool-stage vulnerabilities under one agent-specific security protocol.
- [Ward: Provable RAG Dataset Inference via LLM Watermarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03537): Evaluates agent safety capability with a benchmark, dataset, challenge, metric, or diagnostic protocol suggested by the title.
- [SciSafeEval: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Safety Alignment of Large Language Models in Scientific Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03769): Adds an evaluation resource or protocol for Agent Safety capabilities.
- [Forgetting Curve: A Reliable Method for Evaluating Memorization Capability for Long-Context Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.04727): Forgetting Curve adds a safety bench for jailbreaks, privacy, guardrails, unlearning, or adversarial robustness.
- [Developing Assurance Cases for Adversarial Robustness and Regulatory Compliance in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.05304): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for safety, adversarial robustness, refusal, or harmful-content evaluation for LLMs.
- [Efficient Policy Evaluation with Safety Constraint for Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.05655): Classification concern: this is a safe-RL policy-evaluation method, deriving a variance-minimizing behavior policy under safety constraints rather than releasing an agent-safety benchmark.
- [ETA: Evaluating Then Aligning Safety of Vision Language Models at Inference Time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06625): Benchmarks or evaluates agent safety capability through ETA: Evaluating Then Aligning Safety of Vision Language Models at Inference Time.
- [Secure Video Quality Assessment Resisting Adversarial Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06866): Studies adversarial defenses for video-quality assessment, combining random spatial grid sampling, guardian-map pixel randomization, and temporal feature handling so VQA scores remain reliable under malicious perturbations.
- [Robust AI-Generated Text Detection by Restricted Embeddings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.08113): Evaluates AI-text detectors under cross-domain and cross-generator transfer, showing that removing harmful embedding subspaces can reduce detector reliance on spurious style and topic cues.
- [Responsible AI in NLP: GUS-Net Span-Level Bias Detection Dataset and Benchmark for Generalizations, Unfairness, and Stereotypes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.08388): Introduces GUS-Net with 3,739 snippets and more than 69K token-level BIO annotations for generalizations, unfairness, and stereotypes, enabling span-level bias detection instead of sentence-only labeling.
- [Understanding the First Wave of AI Safety Institutes: Characteristics, Functions, and Challenges](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.09219): Classification concern: this is a governance primer on Japan, UK, and US AI Safety Institutes and their evaluation functions, not a released agent-safety benchmark.
- [Are You Human? An Adversarial Benchmark to Expose LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.09569): Releases implicit and explicit conversational challenges for detecting LLM imposters in real time, reporting detection rates across nine LMSYS-listed models and validating prompts with user studies.
- [LOKI: A Comprehensive Synthetic Data Detection Benchmark using Large Multimodal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.09732): Evaluates LMM synthetic-data detection across video, image, 3D, text, and audio with about 18K curated samples, asking models not only to classify authenticity but also to explain their judgments.
- [Evaluating Gender Bias of LLMs in Making Morality Judgements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.09992): Introduces GenMO, paired short stories with male and female characters, to test whether LLM moral judgments change by gender across GPT, Llama, Mistral, and Claude model families.
- [Multi-round jailbreak attack on large language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.11533): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for safety, adversarial robustness, refusal, or harmful-content evaluation for LLMs.
- [JAILJUDGE: A Comprehensive Jailbreak Judge Benchmark with Multi-Agent Enhanced Explanation Evaluation Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.12855): Benchmarks jailbreak-judge quality with 35K instruction-tuning examples, a 4.5K labeled risk test set, and a 6K multilingual set across ten languages, using a multi-agent explanation framework for more transparent risk judgments.
- [Self-Comparison for Dataset-Level Membership Inference in Large (Vision-)Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13088): Proposes dataset-level membership inference by comparing a model's own behavior across candidate corpora, reducing reliance on known member data or distribution-matched non-members for LLM and VLM privacy audits.
- [Large Language Models are Easily Confused: A Quantitative Metric, Security Implications and Typological Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13237): Defines Language Confusion Entropy from output language distributions and typological variation, then links unwanted language switching to security failures when malicious prompts exploit multilingual confusion.
- [Jailbreaking LLM-Controlled Robots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13691): Evaluates jailbreak risks for LLM-controlled robots. Core idea: use RoboPAIR and harmful-action datasets to test whether attacks can elicit unsafe physical actions across self-driving, ground-robot, and quadruped robot settings.
- [BrowserART](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13886): Red-teams browser agents for harmful behaviors. Core idea: test whether chat-model refusals transfer to browser-agent settings where the model can navigate, click, submit forms, and otherwise act in a live web workflow.
- [LLMs are Biased Teachers: Evaluating LLM Bias in Personalized Education](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.14012): Benchmarks or evaluates agent safety capabilities with a reusable task, dataset, protocol, or metric.
- [Beyond Dataset Watermarking: Model-Level Copyright Protection for Code Summarization Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.14102): Adds an evaluation, dataset, or measurement signal for agent and model safety.
- [CAP: Data Contamination Detection via Consistency Amplification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.15005): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for data-contamination detection for language-model evaluation.
- [NetSafe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.15686): Evaluates safety in networked multi-agent systems. Core idea: vary communication topology to measure how hallucination, misinformation, and unsafe behavior propagate through agent networks.
- [AUTALIC: A Dataset for Anti-AUTistic Ableist Language In Context](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.16520): Provides 2,400 autism-related Reddit sentences with surrounding context, expert neurodiversity annotations, and model evaluations for detecting subtle anti-autistic ableist language.
- [SoK: Dataset Copyright Auditing in Machine Learning Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.16618): Surveys dataset-copyright auditing assumptions and robustness gaps across ML pipelines, framing what must be tested when models may have trained on unauthorized images, artworks, or face data.
- [Arabic Dataset for LLM Safeguard Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.17040): Adds a bench entry for Agent Safety, because it provides an Arabic dataset for LLM safeguard evaluation.
- [Towards Reliable Evaluation of Behavior Steering Interventions in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.17245): Adds a benchmark, dataset, challenge, metric, or evaluation protocol for agent safety.
- [MobileSafetyBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.17520): Evaluates safety of mobile device-control agents in Android environments. Core idea: test misuse, negative side effects, and indirect prompt injection where an agent can operate apps and system functions.
- [ChineseSafe: A Chinese Benchmark for Evaluating Safety in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.18491): Builds a Chinese content-safety benchmark with 205,034 examples across 4 classes and 10 subclasses, including political sensitivity, pornography, and homophonic variants, and evaluates legal-risk detection in open and API LLMs.
- [DreamClear: High-Capacity Real-World Image Restoration with Privacy-Safe Dataset Curation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.18666): Classification concern: the safety hook is GenIR's copyright-compliant and privacy-safe synthetic curation pipeline for image restoration, while the main artifact is a restoration dataset and DiT model rather than an agent-safety benchmark.
- [SafeBench: A Safety Evaluation Framework for Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.18927) ([project page](https://safebench-mm.github.io/); [SafeBench challenge winners](https://www.mlsafety.org/safebench/winners)): Combines automatically generated harmful-query data with an automated MLLM evaluation protocol and challenge leaderboard, targeting multimodal safety failures where visual context changes harmfulness.
- [FairMT-Bench: Benchmarking Fairness for Multi-turn Dialogue in Conversational LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.19317): Adds a task or dataset for evaluating agent safety, extending the Agent Safety / Bench coverage with a concrete measurement target.
- [An Auditing Test To Detect Behavioral Shift in Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.19406): Benchmarks agent safety, alignment, privacy, or provenance, adding an evaluation point for this capability area.
- [CFSafety: Comprehensive Fine-grained Safety Assessment for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.21695): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for safety, adversarial robustness, refusal, or harmful-content evaluation for LLMs.
- [SG-Bench: Evaluating LLM Safety Generalization Across Diverse Tasks and Prompt Types](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.21965): Adds a task or dataset for evaluating agent safety, extending the Agent Safety / Bench coverage with a concrete measurement target.
- [Protecting Privacy in Multimodal Large Language Models with MLLMU-Bench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.22108): Protecting Privacy in Multimodal Large Language Models with adds a safety bench for jailbreaks, privacy, guardrails, unlearning, or adversarial robustness.
- [PV-VTT: A Privacy-Centric Dataset for Mission-Specific Anomaly Detection and Natural Language Interpretation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.22623): Provides video-feature and text annotations for privacy-violation detection, avoiding raw video release while evaluating context-dependent precursor events for mission-specific anomaly detection.
- [InjecGuard: Benchmarking and Mitigating Over-defense in Prompt Injection Guardrail Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.22770): Introduces or analyzes a benchmark, dataset, or evaluation protocol for agent safety, guardrails, and adversarial risk.
- [Do LLMs Know to Respect Copyright Notice?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.01136): Tests whether LLMs honor copyright notices embedded in user inputs across books, news, API documentation, and movie scripts, treating compliance failure as a content-misuse safety risk.
- [Defining and Evaluating Physical Safety for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.02317): Defines a drone-control physical-safety benchmark with human-targeted threats, object-targeted threats, infrastructure attacks, and regulatory violations, then measures utility-safety tradeoffs in mainstream LLMs and prompting methods.
- [Attacking Vision-Language Computer Agents via Pop-ups](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.02391): Evaluates pop-up based attacks on vision-language computer agents, testing whether visually injected instructions inside GUI workflows can redirect actions away from the user's intended task.
- [Benchmarking Vision Language Model Unlearning via Fictitious Facial Identity Dataset](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.03554): Evaluates safety, alignment, watermarking, unlearning, or attack-defense capability, adding a benchmark, dataset, metric, or empirical protocol for parent review.
- [Large-scale moral machine experiment on large language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.06790): Provides a benchmark, dataset, challenge, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [LongSafetyBench: Long-Context LLMs Struggle with Safety Issues](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.06899): Benchmarks agent safety, alignment, privacy, or provenance, adding an evaluation point for this capability area.
- [Beyond the Safety Bundle: Auditing the Helpful and Harmless Dataset](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.08243): Audits Anthropic's Helpful and Harmless dataset with manual and automated content checks, experiments on downstream model safety, and an analysis of 100 citing papers to expose feedback-quality and conceptualization failures.
- [Measuring Non-Adversarial Reproduction of Training Data in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.10242): Adds an evaluation resource or protocol for Agent Safety capabilities.
- [Bias in Large Language Models: Origin, Evaluation, and Mitigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.10915): Benchmarks agent safety capabilities with a task, evaluation protocol, or diagnostic setting.
- [Watermark under Fire: A Robustness Evaluation of LLM Watermarking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.13425): Stress-tests LLM watermarking robustness, clarifying where provenance signals fail under attack or transformation.
- [ChemSafetyBench: Benchmarking LLM Safety on Chemistry Domain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.16736): Benchmarks chemistry-domain safety by testing whether LLMs answer or refuse prompts involving hazardous synthesis, chemical misuse, and benign chemistry knowledge, making dual-use chemical assistance measurable.
- [VLSBench: Unveiling Visual Leakage in Multimodal Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.19939): Adds a task or dataset for evaluating agent safety, extending the Agent Safety / Bench coverage with a concrete measurement target.
- [PhysGame: Uncovering Physical Commonsense Violations in Gameplay Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.01800): Provides a benchmark, dataset, challenge, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [Evaluating and Mitigating Social Bias for Large Language Models in Open-ended Settings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.06134): Extends BBQ into Open-BBQ with fill-in-the-blank and short-answer formats, then labels sentence and paragraph responses to evaluate social bias in open-ended LLM outputs and test debiasing over-correction.
- [On Evaluating the Durability of Safeguards for Open-Weight LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.07097): Adds a task or dataset for evaluating agent safety, extending the Agent Safety / Bench coverage with a concrete measurement target.

- [Look Before You Leap: Enhancing Attention and Vigilance Regarding Harmful Content with GuidelineLLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.10423): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for safety, adversarial robustness, refusal, or harmful-content evaluation for LLMs.
- [Unbiased General Annotated Dataset Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.10831): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for safety, privacy, robustness, watermarking, or adversarial evaluation contribution.
- [SafeAgentBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.13178) ([project page](https://safeagentbench.github.io/)): Evaluates safe task planning for embodied LLM agents. Core idea: measure whether agents can avoid unsafe execution while still completing physical or simulated tasks.
- [Crabs: Consuming Resource via Auto-generation for LLM-DoS Attack under Black-box Settings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.13879): Benchmarks or evaluates agent safety capabilities with a reusable task, dataset, protocol, or metric.
- [Agent-SafetyBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.14470): Benchmarks broad LLM-agent safety risks across interaction environments. Core idea: organize cases by agent risk category and failure mode so safety evaluation is not limited to single-step harmful-response refusal.
- [LLMs Lost in Translation: M-ALERT uncovers Cross-Linguistic Safety Inconsistencies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.15035): Benchmarks agent safety, alignment, privacy, or provenance, adding an evaluation point for this capability area.
- [Chinese SafetyQA: A Safety Short-form Factuality Benchmark for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.15265): Evaluates agent safety capability with a benchmark, dataset, challenge, metric, or diagnostic protocol suggested by the title.
- [Towards More Robust Retrieval-Augmented Generation: Evaluating RAG Under Adversarial Poisoning Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.16708): Evaluates agent safety capability with a benchmark, dataset, challenge, metric, or diagnostic protocol suggested by the title.
- [Are We in the AI-Generated Text World Already? Quantifying and Monitoring AIGT on Social Media](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.18148): Benchmarks or evaluates agent safety capabilities with a reusable task, dataset, protocol, or metric.
- [Libra-Leaderboard: Towards Responsible AI through a Balanced Leaderboard of Safety and Capability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.18551) ([leaderboard](https://leaderboard.librai.tech/)): Ranks 26 mainstream LLMs from 14 organizations with a distance-to-optimal score that balances capability and safety, pairing a dynamic leaderboard with an interactive arena to discourage capability-only optimization.
- [Order Matters! An Empirical Study on Large Language Models'Input Order Bias in Software Fault Localization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.18750): Adds an evaluation, dataset, or measurement signal for agent and model safety.
- [Multi-P2A: A Multi-perspective Benchmark on Privacy Assessment for Large Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.19496): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for privacy assessment benchmark for large vision-language models.
- [MVTamperBench: Evaluating Robustness of Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.19794): Evaluates video VLM robustness against rotation, masking, substitution, repetition, and frame dropping, expanding about 3.4K source videos into more than 17K tampered clips across 19 manipulation tasks and 45 MLLMs.
- [MM-MoralBench: A MultiModal Moral Evaluation Benchmark for Large Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.20718): Evaluates moral reasoning in LVLMs with multimodal scenarios where visual context changes ethical judgment, extending moral-safety tests beyond text-only dilemmas.
- [INFELM: In-depth Fairness Evaluation of Large Text-To-Image Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.01973): Evaluates text-to-image fairness beyond pixel detectors by checking content-generation alignment and bias-sensitive domains where generated images can amplify social stereotypes.
- [AIR-BENCH 2024: A Safety Benchmark based on Regulation and Policies Specified Risk Categories](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/a2dc10dad4d39e8d24987ec0fddea6ce009a81f3): A safety benchmark based on regulation- and policy-specified risk categories. Core idea: align safety evaluation categories with external rules rather than ad hoc harm labels.
- [Do-Not-Answer: Evaluating Safeguards in LLMs](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.findings-eacl.61): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, or evaluation protocol for agent safety, jailbreak, privacy, or harmful-behavior capability.
- [AdversLLM: A Practical Guide To Governance, Maturity and Risk Assessment For LLM-Based Applications](https://doi.org/10.5121/ijci.2024.130604): Combines an LLM governance maturity assessment form with real-world risk scenarios and a prompt-injection testing ground for checking application robustness against malicious prompts and data poisoning.
- [All Languages Matter: On the Multilingual Safety of LLMs](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.findings-acl.349): Adds a reusable agent safety benchmark or evaluation protocol centered on All Languages Matter: On the Multilingual Safety of LLMs.
- [VFD-Net: Vocoder Fingerprints Detection for Fake Audio](https://doi.org/10.1109/icassp48485.2024.10446798): Adds an evaluation resource or protocol for Agent Safety capabilities.
- [TWBias: A Benchmark for Assessing Social Bias in Traditional Chinese Large Language Models through a Taiwan Cultural Lens](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.findings-emnlp.507): Adds an evaluation benchmark, dataset, protocol, or metric for Agent Safety coverage.
- [KDPII: A New Korean Dialogic Dataset for the Deidentification of Personally Identifiable Information](https://doi.org/10.1109/access.2024.3461804): Adds an evaluation, dataset, or measurement signal for agent and model safety.
- [Evaluating Alignment Techniques for Enhancing LLM Performance in a Closed-Domain Application: A RAG Bench-Marking Study](https://doi.org/10.1109/icmla61862.2024.00180): Adds a bench entry for Agent Safety, focusing on the capability signal stated in the title.
- [Evaluating Language Model Vulnerability to Poisoning Attacks in Low-Resource Settings](https://doi.org/10.1109/taslp.2024.3507565): Provides a dataset, benchmark, metric, or evaluation protocol for measuring Agent Safety capabilities.
- [VisDiaHalBench: A Visual Dialogue Benchmark For Diagnosing Hallucination in Large Vision-Language Models](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.acl-long.658): Benchmarks or evaluates agent safety capabilities, adding coverage for visDiaHalBench: A Visual Dialogue Benchmark For Diagnosing Hallucination in Large Vision-Language Models to the Bench section.
- [Overview of the Multilingual Text Detoxification Task at PAN 2024](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-56072-9_1): Adds a task or dataset for evaluating agent safety, extending the Agent Safety / Bench coverage with a concrete measurement target.
- [Challenging Fairness: A Comprehensive Exploration of Bias in LLM-Based Recommendations](https://doi.org/10.1109/bigdata62323.2024.10825082): Benchmarks or evaluates agent safety capabilities with a reusable task, dataset, protocol, or metric.
- [FBK@IWSLT Test Suites Task: Gender Bias evaluation with MuST-SHE](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.iwslt-1.10): Adds a agent safety benchmark, dataset, metric, or evaluation protocol.
- [CRALA: A Chinese-centric Risk Simulation and Assessment Framework for LLM Agents](https://doi.org/10.1109/aiim64537.2024.10934441): Adds a bench entry for Agent Safety, focusing on CRALA: A Chinese-centric Risk Simulation and Assessment Framework for LLM Agents.
- [Sentiment Bias and Security Analysis in Training Datasets of Large Language Models](https://doi.org/10.1109/bdcloud63169.2024.00008): Adds a bench entry for Agent Safety, focusing on Sentiment Bias and Security Analysis in Training Datasets of Large Language Models.
- [AGGA: A Dataset of Academic Guidelines for Generative AI and Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.02063): Contributes a benchmark, dataset, metric, or evaluation protocol for agent and LLM safety.
- [FlippedRAG: Black-Box Opinion Manipulation Adversarial Attacks to Retrieval-Augmented Generation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.02968): Constructs black-box opinion-manipulation attacks against RAG systems, testing whether retrieval poisoning can steer outputs on complex opinion tasks.
- [LLM Content Moderation and User Satisfaction: Evidence from Response Refusals in Chatbot Arena](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.03266): Provides a benchmark, dataset, rubric, or evaluation protocol for agent and model safety, alignment, privacy, bias, guardrail, or misuse-resistance capability.
- [LLMs Reproduce Stereotypes of Sexual and Gender Minorities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.05926): Evaluates stereotypes of sexual and gender minorities reproduced by LLMs.
- [Tag&Tab: Pretraining Data Detection in Large Language Models Using Keyword-Based Membership Inference Attack](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.08454): Tests pretraining-data detection in LLMs through keyword-based membership-inference attacks, adding a privacy and data-contamination diagnostic to agent-safety evaluation.
- [Multimodal Fake News Video Explanation: Dataset, Analysis and Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.08514): Adds a reusable benchmark, dataset, testbed, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [GenAI Content Detection Task 1: English and Multilingual Machine-Generated Text Detection: AI vs. Human](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.11012): Adds an evaluation resource or protocol for Agent Safety capabilities.
- [TAD-Bench: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Embedding-Based Text Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.11960): Adds a reusable evaluation, dataset, challenge, or benchmark protocol for this capability.
- [Implicit Causality-biases in humans and LLMs as a tool for benchmarking LLM discourse capabilities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.12980): Adds an evaluation, dataset, or measurement signal for agent and model safety.
- [LLMs are Vulnerable to Malicious Prompts Disguised as Scientific Language](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.14073): Provides a reusable evaluation target, leaderboard, or measurement protocol for agent safety.
- [CASE-Bench: Context-Aware Safety Evaluation Benchmark for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.14940): Adds a context-aware safety benchmark for large language models.
- [HateBench: Benchmarking Hate Speech Detectors on LLM-Generated Content and Hate Campaigns](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.16750): Adds a benchmark for hate-speech detectors under LLM-generated content and campaigns.
- [AxBench: Steering LLMs? Even Simple Baselines Outperform Sparse Autoencoders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.17148): Benchmarks model steering and concept detection methods, comparing prompting, fine-tuning, sparse autoencoders, probes, and representation fine-tuning.
- [How Much Do Code Language Models Remember? An Investigation on Data Extraction Attacks Before and After Fine-tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.17501): Benchmark/evaluation candidate for agent safety and secure agent infrastructure: Studies data-extraction attacks against code language models.
- [CSEval: Towards Automated, Multi-Dimensional, and Reference-Free Counterspeech Evaluation using Auto-Calibrated LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.17581): Provides a benchmark, dataset, or evaluation protocol for agent safety capability.
- [Early External Safety Testing of OpenAI's o3-mini: Insights from the Pre-Deployment Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.17749): Reports pre-deployment external safety testing of OpenAI o3-mini.
- [Membership Inference Attacks Against VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.18624): Evaluates membership-inference risks for vision-language models, adding a multimodal privacy-attack benchmark line before later OpenLVLM-MIA-style refinements.
- [TombRaider: Entering the Vault of History to Jailbreak Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.18628): Introduces a jailbreak attack targeting large language models through historical-context vaulting.
- [SafeRAG: Benchmarking Security in Retrieval-Augmented Generation of Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.18636): SafeRAG adds a safety bench for jailbreaks, privacy, guardrails, unlearning, or adversarial robustness.
- [Benchmarking Gaslighting Negation Attacks Against Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.19017): Bench candidate for agent safety. Core idea: Benchmarking Gaslighting Negation Attacks Against Multimodal Large Language Models.
- [Riddle Me This! Stealthy Membership Inference for Retrieval-Augmented Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.00306): Provides a reusable evaluation target, leaderboard, or measurement protocol for agent safety.
- [Clone-Robust Weights in Metric Spaces: Handling Redundancy Bias for Benchmark Aggregation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.03576): Proposes clone-proof weighting for benchmark aggregation, reducing score bias when adversarially redundant tasks, data points, or opinion questions overrepresent similar items.
- [ELITE: Enhanced Language-Image Toxicity Evaluation for Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.04757): Curates a higher-quality VLM safety benchmark by filtering ambiguous image-text pairs, diversifying language-image toxicity combinations, and using an ELITE evaluator with explicit toxicity scoring for implicit harmful multimodal content.
- [Privacy-Preserving Dataset Combination](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.05765): Introduces SecureKL, a zero-leakage protocol for comparing dataset utility before sharing by privately computing dataset-divergence metrics without exposing candidate data.
- [Fact-or-Fair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.05849): Evaluates AI bias by separating descriptive factual judgments from normative fairness judgments. Core idea: use cognitive-bias-grounded queries so safety evaluation can identify when a model is factually accurate but socially unfair, or fair-sounding but factually distorted.
- [LLMs on Offensive-Language Detection with Annotation Disagreement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.06207): Evaluates LLM judging of offensive language under annotator disagreement, exposing safety-evaluation reliability limits.
- [Predictive Red Teaming: Breaking Policies Without Breaking Robots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.06575): Introduces RoboART, which edits observations and predicts policy degradation to red-team visuomotor robot policies before costly hardware tests.
- [Multi-turn Evaluation of Anthropomorphic Behaviours](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.07077): Evaluates anthropomorphic behavior in realistic multi-turn LLM interactions. Core idea: measure behaviors such as identity claims, emotional dependence, and relationship framing across varied dialogues rather than relying on single-turn static prompts.
- [SycEval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.08177): Evaluates LLM sycophancy under user-pressure settings. Core idea: test whether models abandon independent reasoning in favor of user agreement across domains such as mathematics and medical advice, making agreement-seeking behavior measurable as a safety risk.
- [BBQ-V: Benchmarking Visual Stereotype Bias in Large Multimodal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.08779): A visual stereotype-bias benchmark for large multimodal models. Core idea: extend BBQ-style ambiguous and disambiguated bias testing to images so multimodal assistants can be audited for stereotype reliance.
- [The Hidden Dimensions of LLM Alignment: A Multi-Dimensional Analysis of Orthogonal Safety Directions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.09674): Provides a reusable evaluation target, leaderboard, or measurement protocol for agent safety.
- [Man Made Language Models? Evaluating LLMs' Perpetuation of Masculine Generics Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.10577): Measures LLM perpetuation of masculine-generics bias.
- [Safety Evaluation of DeepSeek Models in Chinese Contexts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.11137): Evaluates DeepSeek model safety in Chinese contexts.
- [Can't See the Forest for the Trees](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.11184): Benchmarks multimodal safety awareness in MLLMs. Core idea: test whether models detect safety-relevant visual context instead of judging risk only from isolated text or local image details.
- [VLDBench Evaluating multimodal disinformation with regulatory alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.11361): A multimodal disinformation benchmark with regulatory-alignment framing. Core idea: evaluate whether models detect coordinated text-image deception rather than only single-modality misinformation.
- [DemonAgent / AgentBackdoorEval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.12575): Evaluates encrypted multi-backdoor attacks against LLM-based agents. Core idea: split backdoor triggers into benign-looking dynamic fragments and test whether agent safety audits detect or miss the resulting behavior manipulation.
- [Fraud-R1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.12904): Benchmarks LLM robustness against multi-round fraud and phishing inducements. Core idea: model scam progression through credibility building, urgency, and emotional manipulation, including role-play settings where assistants behave like interactive agents.
- [MEXTRA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.13172): Evaluates privacy extraction risks from LLM-agent memory. Core idea: test whether sensitive information stored in memory can be elicited later through agent interactions, exposing privacy failures beyond final-output filtering.
- [Demonstrating specification gaming in reasoning models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.13295): Evaluates whether reasoning models exploit underspecified objectives instead of following the intended task. Core idea: use controlled tasks where apparent success can diverge from the true goal, making specification gaming visible as an agent-safety failure mode.
