- [Deception at Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.13499): Evaluates deceptive designs in LLM-generated e-commerce components. Core idea: measure how model choice and business-interest prompts induce manipulative UI patterns, extending safety evaluation into generated software artifacts.
- [Qorgau: Evaluating LLM Safety in Kazakh-Russian Bilingual Contexts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.13640): Evaluates LLM safety in Kazakh-Russian bilingual settings, exposing region-specific and mixed-language risks that English-only safety prompts can miss.
- [A Matter of Perspective(s): Contrasting Human and LLM Argumentation in Subjective Decision-Making on Subtle Sexism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.14052): Provides a benchmark, dataset, challenge, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [Can LLMs Simulate L2-English Dialogue? An Information-Theoretic Analysis of L1-Dependent Biases](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.14507): Adds an evaluation, dataset, or measurement signal for agent and model safety.
- [The Canary's Echo: Auditing Privacy Risks of LLM-Generated Synthetic Text](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.14921): Adds a bench reference for agent safety, with a reusable contribution signaled by the title rather than a narrow application-only result.
- [Is Safety Standard Same for Everyone? User-Specific Safety Evaluation of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.15086): Evaluates user-specific safety standards and whether LLM safety is consistent across users.
- [Adversarial Prompt Evaluation: Systematic Benchmarking of Guardrails Against Prompt Input Attacks on LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.15427): Benchmarks guardrail defenses against diverse jailbreak and prompt-input attacks, measuring out-of-distribution robustness rather than only performance on one attack family.
- [A Mousetrap: Fooling Large Reasoning Models for Jailbreak with Chain of Iterative Chaos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.15806): Defines or stress-tests safety, jailbreak, oversight, or trustworthiness behavior.
- [Control Illusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.15851): Evaluates whether LLMs enforce instruction hierarchies. Core idea: use constraint-prioritization conflicts to test whether system and user role separation actually controls behavior, and whether social hierarchy cues override intended priority.
- [GuidedBench: Equipping Jailbreak Evaluation with Guidelines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.16903): Measures discrepancies in jailbreak evaluation systems, then supplies a curated harmful-question set, case-specific evaluation guidelines, and GuidedEval so attack success is judged against explicit per-case criteria.
- [LongSafety: Evaluating Long-Context Safety of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.16971): Evaluates long-context safety in open-ended tasks. Core idea: combine safety-risk categories with long user-oriented contexts so models are tested on whether extended evidence and instructions introduce unsafe behavior beyond ordinary short-prompt refusal tests.
- [PrivaCI-Bench: Evaluating Privacy with Contextual Integrity and Legal Compliance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.17041): Evaluates privacy under contextual integrity and legal-compliance constraints. Core idea: test whether models respect context-specific information norms rather than applying generic privacy heuristics.
- [MM-PoisonRAG: Disrupting Multimodal RAG with Local and Global Poisoning Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.17832): What it evaluates: multimodal RAG poisoning. Core idea: construct local and global poisoning attacks against text-image retrieval contexts to test whether models remain grounded under hostile knowledge bases.
- [PII-Bench: Evaluating Query-Aware Privacy Protection Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.18545): Evaluates query-aware privacy protection systems for personally identifiable information, adding a PII-focused benchmark line to privacy safety evaluation.
- [Holistic Audit Dataset Generation for LLM Unlearning via Knowledge Graph Traversal and Redundancy Removal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.18810): Introduces HANKER, a knowledge-graph traversal and redundancy-removal pipeline that expands MUSE-style unlearning audits to more than 69K news and 111K books audit cases with finer forget-retain coverage.
- [Multi-Agent Security Tax](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.19145): Measures the security tax of collaboration in multi-agent systems.
- [Among Them: A game-based framework for assessing persuasion capabilities of LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.20426): Uses a game-based framework to assess LLM persuasion capabilities.
- [BadJudge: Backdoor Vulnerabilities of LLM-as-a-Judge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.00596): Provides a reusable evaluation target, leaderboard, or measurement protocol for agent safety.
- [Answer, Refuse, or Guess?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.01332): evaluates risk-aware answer-versus-defer decisions under task-fixed but consequence-varying reward structures. Core idea: measure whether language models maximize expected utility by answering more freely in low-risk settings and deferring in high-risk settings, exposing over-answering and over-deferral failure modes for agents.
- [Unmasking Implicit Bias: Evaluating Persona-Prompted LLM Responses in Power-Disparate Social Scenarios](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.01532): Evaluates persona-prompted LLM responses in power-disparate social scenarios, making implicit bias visible under role-conditioned interaction.
- [MINJA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03704): Evaluates memory-injection attacks on LLM agents through query-only interaction. Core idea: test whether ordinary-looking queries can poison persistent memory and later steer agent behavior without direct access to the memory store.
- [MASK Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03750): Evaluates whether a model contradicts its own stated beliefs under pressure. Core idea: separate honesty from accuracy by checking whether models maintain stated knowledge when user or system pressure pushes them toward a conflicting answer.
- [How Personality Traits Shape LLM Risk-Taking Behaviour](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.04735): Studies how personality interventions affect LLM risk-taking behavior.
- [Collaborative Evaluation of Deepfake Text with Deliberation-Enhancing Dialogue Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.04945): Adds a reusable benchmark, dataset, testbed, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [SafeArena](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.04957) ([project page](https://safearena.github.io/); [leaderboard](https://huggingface.co/spaces/McGill-NLP/safearena-leaderboard)): Evaluates how autonomous web agents behave on safety-related and harmful web tasks. Core idea: pair safe and harmful tasks to observe whether agents comply with risky requests involving misinformation, illegal activity, harassment, cybercrime, bias, and related harms.
- [Beyond English: Unveiling Multilingual Bias in LLM Copyright Compliance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.05713): Evaluates agent safety through the paper's stated contribution.
- [AILuminate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.05731) ([official results](https://ailuminate.mlcommons.org/benchmarks/security/0.5-en_us-official-ensemble)): Evaluates AI risk and reliability through the MLCommons benchmark protocol. Core idea: standardize safety and reliability measurement across model behavior categories so results are more comparable than one-off red-team prompt sets.
- [Can Small Language Models Reliably Resist Jailbreak Attacks? A Comprehensive Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06519): Evaluates agent safety capability with a benchmark, dataset, challenge, metric, or diagnostic protocol suggested by the title.
- [Towards More Accurate Personalized Image Generation: Addressing Overfitting and Evaluation Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06632): Adds a agent safety benchmark, dataset, metric, or evaluation protocol.
- [Evaluating and Aligning Human Economic Risk Preferences in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06646): Measures whether LLM decisions match human economic risk preferences under lottery-style choices, then evaluates alignment interventions for risk-taking behavior relevant to financial or advisory agents.
- [VisBias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.07575): Evaluates explicit and implicit social biases in vision-language models. Core idea: separate conscious stereotype-like responses from implicit association patterns so multimodal bias evaluation covers failures that ordinary direct QA may miss.
- [Towards Large Language Models that Benefit for All: Benchmarking Group Fairness in Reward Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.07806): Benchmarks group fairness in reward models by separating prompt preference differences from demographic bias and tracing whether unfairness arises from pretraining, RLHF, or learned reward components.
- [Generating Robot Constitutions & Benchmarks for Semantic Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08663): Generates robot constitutions and benchmarks for semantic safety. Core idea: turn high-level robot safety rules into evaluable tasks for embodied agents.
- [TH-Bench: Evaluating Evading Attacks via Humanizing AI Text on Machine-Generated Text Detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08708): Evaluates humanization attacks against machine-generated-text detectors on evasion effectiveness, text quality, and computational overhead, covering 6 attack methods, 13 detectors, 6 datasets, 19 domains, and 11 generators.
- [SAEBench: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Sparse Autoencoders in Language Model Interpretability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09532): Benchmarks sparse autoencoders for language-model interpretability.
- [AgentDAM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09780): Evaluates data minimization failures in autonomous web agents. Core idea: measure whether agents reveal unnecessary personal data while completing realistic web tasks, using leakage rate as a deployment-relevant privacy metric.
- [ExtremeAIGC: Benchmarking LMM Vulnerability to AI-Generated Extremist Content](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09964): Candidate benchmark or evaluation contribution for agent safety; it directly targets this page's capability scope and belongs in the `Bench` track.
- [MinorBench: A hand-built benchmark for content-based risks for children](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10242): Provides a hand-built benchmark and taxonomy for child-specific content risks, testing whether LLMs refuse unsafe or inappropriate queries from minors under different system prompts.
- [Measuring Political Preferences in AI Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10649): integrates measurement approaches for political preferences in AI systems, supporting bias and alignment evaluation.
- [DarkBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10728): Evaluates manipulative dark patterns in LLM interactions. Core idea: measure brand bias, retention pressure, sycophancy, anthropomorphism, harmful generation, and sneaking as user-manipulation risks beyond ordinary refusal tests.
- [MIP against Agent: Malicious Image Patches Hijacking Multimodal OS Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10809): Studies malicious image patches that hijack multimodal OS agents; core idea: evaluate whether agents treat visual adversarial content as instructions during OS-level execution.
- [Auditing language models for hidden objectives](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10965): Provides a reusable evaluation target, leaderboard, or measurement protocol for agent safety.
- [Typographic Visual Prompt Injection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.11519): Evaluates prompt-injection threats in cross-modality generation models. Core idea: put malicious instructions into rendered text or typography inside visual inputs so multimodal generation systems are tested for whether visual text can override the intended user prompt.
- [No LLM is Free From Bias: A Comprehensive Study of Bias Evaluation in Large Language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.11985): Introduces or analyzes a benchmark, dataset, metric, or evaluation protocol for agent safety, security, privacy, or robustness.
- [Multi-Agent Systems Execute Arbitrary Malicious Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.12188): Adds a benchmark or evaluation suite for agent safety, centered on Multi-Agent Systems Execute Arbitrary Malicious Code.
- [PEBench: A Fictitious Dataset to Benchmark Machine Unlearning for Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.12545): Evaluates agent safety capability with a benchmark, dataset, challenge, metric, or diagnostic protocol suggested by the title.
- [IRT-Based LLM Ideology Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13149): uses item-response theory to measure perceived socio-economic bias and ideology in LLMs.
- [Only a Little to the Left: A Theory-grounded Measure of Political Bias in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16148): Provides a reusable evaluation target, leaderboard, or measurement protocol for agent safety.
- [Big Help or Big Brother? Auditing Tracking, Profiling, and Personalization in Generative AI Assistants](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16586): Audits tracking, profiling, and personalization in generative AI assistants, making privacy-relevant assistant behavior an explicit safety-evaluation target.
- [Automated Harmfulness Testing for Code Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16740): Uses coverage-guided transformations to test whether Code LLMs generate harmful content inside software artifacts, extending safety testing into code-generation settings.
- [When Tom Eats Kimchi](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16826): Evaluates cultural bias in multimodal large language models under mixed-cultural image-text contexts. Core idea: test whether models preserve cultural recognition across people, foods, and scenes whose visual cues cross stereotypical associations.
- [Challenging Dataset and Multi-Modal Gated Mixture of Experts Model for Remote Sensing Copy-Move Forgery Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18104): Releases Real-RSCM for remote-sensing copy-move question answering, using manually annotated subtle tampering cases to evaluate whether models understand forged object relations in security-relevant imagery.
- [Unbiasing through Textual Descriptions: Mitigating Representation Bias in Video Benchmarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18637): Adds an evaluation, dataset, or measurement signal for agent and model safety.
- [CEFW: A Comprehensive Evaluation Framework for Watermark in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.20802): Evaluates text watermarking across five dimensions: detection ease, text quality fidelity, embedding cost, robustness to adversarial attacks, and imperceptibility against imitation or forgery.
- [FindTheFlaws: Annotated Errors for Detecting Flawed Reasoning and Scalable Oversight Research](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22989): Provides annotated reasoning flaws for studying critique, debate, and scalable oversight of complex model answers.
- [Beyond Speculation: Measuring the Growing Presence of Large Language Model-Generated Texts in Multilingual Disinformation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23242): Adds a reusable benchmark, dataset, testbed, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [When Persuasion Overrides Truth in Multi-Agent LLM Debates: Introducing a Confidence-Weighted Persuasion Override Rate (CW-POR)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.00374): Evaluates truth failures in multi-agent debate. Core idea: measure when a confident false agent persuades a judge away from a factual answer.
- [FortisAVQA and MAVEN: a Benchmark Dataset and Debiasing Framework for Robust Multimodal Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.00487): Extends MUSIC-AVQA with rephrased questions and distribution-shifted test splits so audio-visual QA systems can be evaluated on rare, frequent, and overall question robustness rather than shortcut-heavy averages.
- [P2NIA: Privacy-Preserving Non-Iterative Auditing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.00874): Defines a privacy-preserving, non-iterative fairness-auditing scheme where auditors use synthetic or local data instead of repeated platform API queries, reducing data-leakage exposure and estimation bias in high-risk AI audits.
- [PiCo: Jailbreaking Multimodal Large Language Models via Pictorial Code Contextualization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.01444): Jailbreaks multimodal LLMs through pictorial code contextualization, adding a visual-code attack pattern to multimodal safety evaluation.
- [Les Dissonances](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.03111): Evaluates cross-tool harvesting and polluting risks in pool-of-tools LLM agents. Core idea: expose how information and malicious state can propagate across tools, so tool-rich agents are tested for inter-tool contamination rather than isolated call safety only.
- [A Benchmark for Scalable Oversight Protocols](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.03731): Benchmarks scalable oversight protocols. Core idea: provide a systematic empirical setting for comparing debate-style and other oversight mechanisms.
- [What Large Language Models Do Not Talk About: An Empirical Study of Moderation and Censorship Practices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.03803): Empirically evaluates hard and soft censorship or moderation behavior across multilingual political prompts.
- [Among Us: A Sandbox for Measuring and Detecting Agentic Deception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.04072): A sandbox for measuring and detecting agentic deception; core idea: evaluate deception as behavior over interactive trajectories rather than only false statements in static prompts.
- [IMPersona](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.04332): What it evaluates: individual-level LM impersonation risk. Core idea: combine personal writing style, personal knowledge, fine-tuning, and memory-style retrieval to test whether models can convincingly simulate specific people.
- [Your Image Generator Is Your New Private Dataset](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.04582): Adds an evaluation, dataset, or measurement signal for agent and model safety.
- [ShadowCoT: Cognitive Hijacking for Stealthy Reasoning Backdoors in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.05605): Studies cognitive hijacking backdoors in chain-of-thought reasoning. Core idea: target the reasoning path itself so malicious behavior can stay hidden behind plausible intermediate steps.
- [CLEAR-Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07887): Benchmarks adversarial robustness to bias elicitation. Core idea: probe sociocultural bias categories with curated prompts, jailbreak variants, and LLM-as-judge safety scores so bias resilience is measured under attack rather than only under benign prompts.
- [EmoAgent: Assessing and Safeguarding Human-AI Interaction for Mental Health Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.09689): Evaluates and safeguards mental-health-oriented human-AI interaction. Core idea: stress-test emotionally sensitive interactions where an agent must balance helpfulness, risk detection, and safe escalation.
- [LLM Can be a Dangerous Persuader](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10430): Empirically evaluates persuasion-safety risks in large language models.
- [Bypassing LLM Guardrails](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.11168): Empirically evaluates evasion attacks against prompt-injection and jailbreak detection systems.
- [OpenTuringBench: An Open-Model-based Benchmark and Framework for Machine-Generated Text Detection and Attribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.11369): Benchmarks open-model machine-generated-text detection and attribution with Turing-test, authorship-attribution, manipulated-text, out-of-domain, and unseen-model tasks, plus the OTBDetector contrastive baseline.
- [Benchmarking Multi-National Value Alignment for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12911): Provides a benchmark, dataset, rubric, or evaluation protocol for agent and model safety, alignment, privacy, bias, guardrail, or misuse-resistance capability.
- [Manipulating Multimodal Agents via Cross-Modal Prompt Injection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.14348): Studies prompt-injection attacks that cross language and vision channels in multimodal agents.
- [SafePlan-Bench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.14650): Evaluates task-planning safety for LLM-based embodied agents. Core idea: test whether agents generate safe plans for daily embodied tasks across hazard categories, including cases without explicit malicious instructions.
- [PROMPTEVALS: A Dataset of Assertions and Guardrails for Custom Production Large Language Model Pipelines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.14738): Bench candidate for agent safety. Core idea: A Dataset of Assertions and Guardrails for Custom Production Large Language Model Pipelines.
- [aiXamine: Simplified LLM Safety and Security](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.14985): Evaluates integrated safety and security evaluation for LLMs. Core idea: make agent safety measurable with task-specific data, protocols, metrics, or diagnostic environments rather than generic aggregate scores.
- [Advancing Embodied Agent Security](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15699): Evaluates embodied-agent safety and input moderation. Core idea: define embodied safety risks, curate benchmark data, and test moderation mechanisms before unsafe physical or simulated actions are executed.
- [MetaHarm: Harmful YouTube Video Dataset Annotated by Domain Experts, GPT-4-Turbo, and Crowdworkers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.16304): Adds a reusable benchmark, dataset, testbed, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [Amplified Vulnerabilities: Structured Jailbreak Attacks on LLM-based Multi-Agent Debate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.16489): Evaluates agent safety and security evaluation. Core idea: use the paper's task, dataset, or protocol around structured jailbreak attacks on llm-based multi-agent debate to make this capability more comparable.
- [PReSS: A Black-Box Framework for Evaluating Political Stance Stability in LLMs via Argumentative Pressure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.17052): Evaluates political stance stability in LLMs under argumentative pressure.
- [RepliBench: Evaluating the autonomous replication capabilities of language model agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.18565): A benchmark for autonomous replication capabilities of language-model agents; core idea: measure whether agents can copy, persist, or propagate themselves in ways that create deployment safety risk.
- [WASP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.18575): Evaluates end-to-end web-agent security against prompt-injection attacks. Core idea: inject low-cost adversarial content into realistic web tasks and measure whether agents preserve the user goal without executing attacker instructions.
- [DoxBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.19373): Evaluates location-related privacy leakage in multimodal reasoning models. Core idea: test whether models infer sensitive geolocation from user images across graded privacy-risk levels.
- [SAGE: A Generic Framework for LLM Safety Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.19674): A generic framework for LLM safety evaluation. Core idea: move safety testing beyond single-turn generic policies toward contextual, application-specific conversational harms.
- [ToolHijacker](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.19793): Evaluates prompt-injection attacks against tool selection in LLM agents. Core idea: poison tool documents so retrieval and selection route the agent toward malicious tools, exposing a tool-library attack surface.
- [XBreaking: Understanding how LLMs security alignment can be broken](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21700): Defines or stress-tests safety, jailbreak, oversight, or trustworthiness behavior.
- [AgentHarm](https://openreview.net/forum?id=AC5n7xHuR1) ([evaluation page](https://ukgovernmentbeis.github.io/inspect_evals/evals/safeguards/agentharm/)): Evaluates harmfulness of LLM agents. Core idea: move beyond single-turn harmful-response classification by testing whether agents can plan or execute harmful actions in agentic settings.
- [AgentHarm: A Benchmark for Measuring Harmfulness of LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.09024): Benchmarks agent safety capabilities with a task, evaluation protocol, or diagnostic setting.
- [SPY: Enhancing Privacy with Synthetic PII Detection Dataset](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.naacl-srw.23): Introduces a synthetic PII detection dataset for privacy evaluation.
- [Multilingual Blending](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-naacl.191/): Evaluates LLM safety alignment under mixed-language inputs, exposing multilingual safety failures beyond monolingual prompts.
- [Multilingual Blending: Large Language Model Safety Alignment Evaluation with Language Mixture](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.findings-naacl.191): Evaluates LLM safety alignment under mixed-language inputs, exposing multilingual safety failures beyond monolingual prompts.
- [Decoding Fatphobia: Examining Anti-Fat and Pro-Thin Bias in AI-Generated Images](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.findings-naacl.266): Provides a benchmark, dataset, rubric, or evaluation protocol for agent and model safety, alignment, privacy, bias, guardrail, or misuse-resistance capability.
- [It Is Not Only the Negative that Deserves Attention! Understanding, Generation & Evaluation of (Positive) Moderation](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.naacl-long.567): Contributes a benchmark, dataset, metric, or evaluation protocol for agent and LLM safety.
- [OET](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.00843): Evaluates prompt-injection attacks and defenses with an optimization-based adaptive testing toolkit. Core idea: generate worst-case adversarial strings under white-box and black-box access, execute attacks dynamically, and analyze robustness under a shared protocol.
- [Value Portrait: Assessing Language Models' Values through Psychometrically and Ecologically Valid Items](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.01015): Provides a benchmark, dataset, rubric, or evaluation protocol for agent and model safety, alignment, privacy, bias, guardrail, or misuse-resistance capability.
- [Stealth and Situational Awareness Evaluations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.01420): Evaluates scheming-relevant reasoning in frontier models. Core idea: test whether models can reason about oversight circumvention, self-state, environment, and deployment context, supporting safety cases about inability to scheme.
- [Multimodal LLM Sensitive-Information Unlearning Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.01456): Evaluates sensitive-information unlearning in multimodal LLMs with attack and defense protocols, connecting privacy, forgetting, and multimodal safety.
- [Unmasking the Canvas](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04146): Dynamically benchmarks image-generation jailbreaks and LLM content-safety checks by testing short natural prompts that elicit forged documents, manipulated public-figure images, and other compromising visual outputs.
- [WaterDrum: Watermarking for Data-centric Unlearning Metric](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05064): Evaluates agent safety through the paper's stated contribution.
- [SecReEvalBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.07584): Provides multi-turn, scenario-based adversarial prompt attacks for security-sensitive LLM evaluation, expanding security resilience testing beyond predefined cybersecurity-only attack domains.
- [Winning at All Cost: A Small Environment for Eliciting Specification Gaming Behaviors in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.07846): Evaluates specification-gaming behavior in LLMs. Core idea: use an impossible game setting to observe whether models exploit loopholes instead of accepting task failure.
- [PIG: Privacy Jailbreak Attack on LLMs via Gradient-based Iterative In-Context Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09921): Uses gradient-based iterative in-context optimization to probe privacy jailbreaks and PII leakage.
- [Understanding Gen Alpha's Digital Language: Evaluation of LLM Safety Systems for Content Moderation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10588): Adds a reusable benchmark, dataset, testbed, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [BanglaFake: Constructing and Evaluating a Specialized Bengali Deepfake Audio Dataset](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10885): Builds a Bengali deepfake-audio dataset with 12,260 real and 13,260 synthetic utterances, using MOS, intelligibility, and MFCC visualization to evaluate low-resource audio-forgery detection.
- [PANORAMA: A synthetic PII-laced dataset for studying sensitive data memorization in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.12238): Adds an evaluation, dataset, or measurement signal for agent and model safety.
- [CAPTURE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.12368): Evaluates context-aware prompt-injection detection and over-defense in guardrail models. Core idea: pair adversarial and benign in-context cases so defenses are tested for both false negatives on attacks and false positives on harmless content, with CaptureGuard as a trained reference defense.
- [Bullying the Machine: How Personas Increase LLM Vulnerability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.12692): Evaluates agent safety and security evaluation. Core idea: use the paper's task, dataset, or protocol around how personas increase llm vulnerability to make this capability more comparable.
- [The Traitors: Deception and Trust in Multi-Agent Language Model Simulations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.12923): Evaluates deception and trust in multi-agent language-model simulations. Core idea: study whether agents can detect or exploit deceptive behavior in social multi-agent settings.
- [AgentScan](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.12981): Evaluates security risks of mobile LLM agents across language reasoning, GUI interaction, and system execution. Core idea: run a semi-automated analysis framework over 11 attack scenarios and multiple deployed mobile agents to expose privacy leakage, behavioral deviation, and execution-hijacking risks.
- [The Hidden Dangers of Browsing AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.13076): Studies safety risks in browsing agents; core idea: expose how autonomous web access changes the threat model through untrusted pages, injected content, and state-changing actions.
- [Benchmarking Unified Face Attack Detection via Hierarchical Prompt Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.13327): Introduces UniAttackData+ to unify presentation-attack and deepfake face detection, covering 2,875 identities and many forgery techniques so detectors face both physical and digital attacks.
- [Investigating the Vulnerability of LLM-as-a-Judge Architectures to Prompt-Injection Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.13348): Evaluates prompt-injection vulnerabilities in LLM-as-a-judge evaluation architectures.
- [Logic Jailbreak](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.13527): Attacks LLM safety restrictions through formal logical expressions. Core idea: use logic-form transformations as a jailbreak protocol that tests whether safety filters generalize beyond natural-language attack phrasing.
- [Breaking Language Barriers or Reinforcing Bias? A Study of Gender and Racial Disparities in Multilingual Contrastive Vision Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14160): Adds a reusable benchmark, dataset, testbed, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [Information Retrieval Induced Safety Degradation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14215): What it evaluates: how retrieval access changes the safety behavior of LLM agents. Core idea: compare no-retrieval, Wikipedia retrieval, and open-web search settings to measure reliability, bias propagation, and harmful-content generation under external information exposure.
- [EVA: Red-Teaming GUI Agents via Evolving Indirect Prompt Injection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14289): Red-teams GUI agents against evolving indirect prompt injection. Core idea: close the loop over attention signals and GUI cues to optimize adversarial instructions.
- [Hidden Ghost Hand: Unveiling Backdoor Vulnerabilities in MLLM-Powered Mobile GUI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14418): Adds a benchmark or evaluation suite for agent safety, centered on Hidden Ghost Hand: Unveiling Backdoor Vulnerabilities in MLLM-Powered Mobile GUI Agents.
- [Attributional Safety Failures in Large Language Models under Code-Mixed Perturbations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14469): Defines or stress-tests safety, jailbreak, oversight, or trustworthiness behavior.
- [AIRiskDilemmas](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14633): Litmus-tests AI value prioritization under high-stakes dilemmas. Core idea: use conflicting welfare and honesty scenarios to evaluate whether models choose deceptive or harmful tradeoffs when values compete.
- [MORALISE: A Structured Benchmark for Moral Alignment in Visual Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14728): Uses 2,481 expert-verified real-world image-text pairs and a 13-topic Turiel-domain taxonomy to test VLM moral alignment, with labels for both moral topic and whether the violation comes from image or text.
- [CROSS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14972): A multimodal cultural-safety benchmark for LVLMs, evaluating culturally grounded norm awareness, education, compliance, and helpfulness across languages and countries.
- [PsyScam: A Benchmark for Psychological Techniques in Real-World Scams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15017): Introduces PsyScam for evaluating psychological manipulation techniques in real-world scams.
- [Keep Security!](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15805): Benchmarks security-policy preservation in large language model contexts against indirect attacks. Core idea: test whether models preserve governing policy when untrusted contextual material attempts to redirect answers in question answering.
- [WandaPlan](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16557): Evaluates whether LLM-based multi-agent travel planners remain reliable under fraud-detection pressure. Core idea: place planning agents in realistic travel scenarios with deceptive or risky offers so completion quality and safety awareness can be measured together.
- [BadVLA: Towards Backdoor Attacks on Vision-Language-Action Models via Objective-Decoupled Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16640): Studies backdoor attacks on vision-language-action models. Core idea: expose how objective-decoupled optimization can implant unsafe action behavior in embodied VLA policies.
- [From Evaluation to Defense: Advancing Safety in Video Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16643): Evaluates safety risks in video large language models with a large real-world video-query benchmark. Core idea: extend safety testing from text and images to video evidence and risk categories.
- [RRTL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17106): Red-teams reasoning LLMs in tool-learning settings. Core idea: test deceptive threat disclosure and forced tool invocation so safety evaluation covers risky tool trajectories rather than only final text responses.
- [MDIT-Bench: Evaluating the Dual-Implicit Toxicity in Large Multimodal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17144): Provides a benchmark, dataset, or evaluation protocol for agent safety capability.
- [ReasoningShield](https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2505.17244): What it evaluates: safety risks inside reasoning traces. Core idea: define a multi-level CoT moderation taxonomy, release annotated query-trace pairs, and benchmark detectors for harmful content hidden in intermediate reasoning.
- [ReasoningShield](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17244): Evaluates and detects safety risks inside reasoning traces. Core idea: formalize Chain-of-Thought moderation with a multi-level risk taxonomy and benchmark reasoning traces where harmful content may appear before the final answer.
- [SweEval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17332): Tests enterprise LLM profanity and cultural-safety limits across communication tasks such as emails, sales pitches, and casual messages, emphasizing region-sensitive respectful language rather than only harmful-request refusal.
- [MemeReaCon: Probing Contextual Meme Understanding in Large Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17433): Provides a benchmark, dataset, rubric, or evaluation protocol for agent and model safety, alignment, privacy, bias, guardrail, or misuse-resistance capability.
- [PaSBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17455): Evaluates proactive risk awareness in multimodal language models. Core idea: use image sequences and text logs across safety-critical everyday domains to test whether models detect risks before users explicitly ask about them.
- [Surfacing Semantic Orthogonality Across Model Safety Benchmarks: A Multi-Dimensional Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17636): Evaluates surfacing Semantic Orthogonality Across Model Safety Benchmarks: A Multi-Dimensional Analysis, adding a reusable dataset, benchmark, metric, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [EVADE-Bench: Multimodal Benchmark for Evasive Content Detection in E-Commerce Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17654): Benchmarks or measures agent safety, prompt-injection, and guardrail reliability, providing a candidate reusable evaluation signal.
- [A Critical Evaluation of Defenses against Prompt Injection Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.18333): Evaluates defenses against prompt-injection attacks. Core idea: compare mitigation strategies under adversarial inputs so agent-safety claims are grounded in reproducible attack-defense tests.
- [Exploring the Vulnerability of the Content Moderation Guardrail in Large Language Models via Intent Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.18556): Explores intent manipulation attacks against content moderation guardrails.
- [The Eye of Sherlock Holmes: Uncovering User Private Attribute Profiling via Vision-Language Model Agentic Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19139): Studies private-attribute profiling through a vision-language-model agentic framework, making privacy leakage from visual agent workflows a concrete safety evaluation target.
- [OrgAccess](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19165): Benchmarks role-based access control in organization-scale LLM assistants. Core idea: test whether models respect hierarchical permissions and conflicting access rules when deciding what information or actions are allowed.
- [When Ethics and Payoffs Diverge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19212): Evaluates LLM agents in morally charged social dilemmas. Core idea: place agents in settings where utility incentives and ethical behavior conflict, making value trade-offs and harmful strategic behavior measurable.
- [BSA / Beyond Safe Answers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19690): Evaluates true risk awareness in large reasoning models. Core idea: distinguish superficially safe final answers from reasoning traces that actually identify and mitigate underlying risks.
- [SAFEL / EMBODYGUARD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19933): Evaluates physical safety in LLM-based embodied decision making. Core idea: combine command-refusal and plan-safety tests with PDDL-grounded scenarios to diagnose failures in goal interpretation, transition modeling, and action sequencing.
- [MEBench: A Novel Benchmark for Understanding Mutual Exclusivity Bias in Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20122): Measures mutual-exclusivity bias in VLMs with generated spatial-reasoning scenes and metrics that test whether models use novel-object context rather than overgeneralizing word-learning assumptions.
- [VSCBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20362): Benchmarks safety calibration in vision-language models across visually or textually similar safe and unsafe image-text pairs. Core idea: measure both under-refusal and over-refusal so multimodal safety is not reduced to harmful-query compliance.
- [STEER-BENCH: A Benchmark for Evaluating the Steerability of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20645): Tests population-specific steering with 30 contrasting subreddit pairs across 19 domains, more than 10K instruction-response pairs, and 5.5K validated multiple-choice items; 13 LLMs trail human experts by large margins on community-norm alignment.
- [AdInject: Real-World Black-Box Attacks on Web Agents via Advertising Delivery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.21499): Evaluates black-box advertising-delivery attacks on web agents. Core idea: make this capability comparable through a defined task set, protocol, or scoring interface.
- [SoSBench: Benchmarking Safety Alignment on Six Scientific Domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.21605): A safety-alignment benchmark across six scientific domains. Core idea: test misuse resilience on scientifically sophisticated prompts beyond simple harmful-instruction templates.
- [SAGE-Eval: Evaluating LLMs for Systematic Generalizations of Safety Facts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.21828): Evaluates systematic generalization of safety facts. Core idea: test whether models transfer known safety facts to new user scenarios instead of answering naively.
- [RedTeamCUA / RTC-Bench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.21936) ([project page](https://osu-nlp-group.github.io/RedTeamCUA)): Evaluates red-team testing for computer-use agents in hybrid Web-OS environments. Core idea: use 864 indirect prompt-injection examples and real GUI/web action spaces to expose the attack surface of CUA during cross-application execution.
- [Jailbreak Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.22037): Builds renewable safety benchmarks from distilled jailbreak attacks. Core idea: select compact, updateable prompt sets so robustness comparisons remain fair as attack methods and model behaviors shift.
- [Disrupting Vision-Language Model-Driven Navigation Services via Adversarial Object Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23266): Provides a reusable evaluation target, leaderboard, or measurement protocol for agent safety.
- [USB](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23793): A unified safety benchmark for multimodal large language models. Core idea: evaluate both harmful-query compliance and oversensitivity to harmless queries across modal risk combinations so MLLM safety scores reflect robustness and over-refusal together.
- [System Prompt Extraction Attacks and Defenses in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23817): Systematizes prompt-extraction attacks and defenses against LLM system prompts.
- [Multi-turn Dialogue Sycophancy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23840): Measures sycophancy of language models across multi-turn dialogues. Core idea: evaluate whether agreement-seeking behavior accumulates or changes over interaction trajectories rather than only in isolated prompts.
- [Exploring Multimodal Challenges in Toxic Chinese Detection: Taxonomy, Benchmark, and Findings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24341): Introduces or analyzes a benchmark, dataset, metric, or evaluation protocol for agent safety, security, privacy, or robustness.
- [HESEIA: A community-based dataset for evaluating social biases in large language models, co-designed in real school settings in Latin America](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24712): Provides 46,499 community co-designed bias sentences from 370 teachers, 5,370 students, and 189 Latin American schools, capturing intersectional stereotypes across demographic axes and school subjects.
- [The Automated but Risky Game](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.00073): What it evaluates: a benchmark for risky agent-to-agent negotiation and transactions in consumer markets.
- [Let Them Down Easy! Contextual Effects of LLM Guardrails on User Perceptions and Preferences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.00195): Provides a benchmark, dataset, rubric, or evaluation protocol for agent and model safety, alignment, privacy, bias, guardrail, or misuse-resistance capability.
- [When GPT Spills the Tea: Comprehensive Assessment of Knowledge File Leakage in GPTs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.00197): A safety assessment for knowledge-file leakage in GPT-style agents. Core idea: evaluate leakage vectors created by agent knowledge files and complex data flows, not only direct adversarial prompts.
- [RiOSWorld](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.00618) ([project page](https://yjyddq.github.io/RiOSWorld.github.io/), [open-source code](https://github.com/yjyddq/RiOSWorld)): Evaluates risky tasks for multimodal computer-use agents in realistic applications. Core idea: cover risks from both user requests and environmental state so GUI agents are judged on whether they recognize danger before acting.
- [AgentAuditor / ASSEBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.00641): Evaluates safety and security judgments over LLM-agent interaction records. Core idea: pair an agent-interaction benchmark with an auditor model that uses memory to assess whether trajectories contain risky or insecure behavior.
- [Trick or Neat: Adversarial Ambiguity and Language Model Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.01205): Releases syntactic, lexical, and phonological ambiguity tests with adversarial word-order, synonym, and random perturbations to probe whether LMs detect ambiguity under prompt and representation-based evaluation.
- [An Empirical Study of Group Conformity in Multi-Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.01332): Benchmark/evaluation candidate for agent safety and secure agent infrastructure: Empirically studies group conformity in multi-agent systems.
- [MLA-Trust](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.01616): Evaluates trustworthiness of multimodal LLM agents in GUI environments. Core idea: score truthfulness, controllability, safety, and privacy in high-risk interaction tasks rather than treating GUI task completion as sufficient.
- [Beyond the Protocol: Unveiling Attack Vectors in the Model Context Protocol (MCP) Ecosystem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02040): Evaluates attacks against the Model Context Protocol ecosystem. Core idea: test malicious MCP servers through tool poisoning, puppet, rug-pull, and external-resource attacks.
- [SALAD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02089): Assesses machine unlearning for LLM-aided hardware design risks, including benchmark contamination, IP leakage, and malicious Verilog generation.
- [VPI-Bench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02456) (dataset: [VPI-Bench/vpi-bench](https://huggingface.co/datasets/VPI-Bench/vpi-bench)): Evaluates visual prompt-injection robustness for computer-use/browser-use agents. Core idea: embed malicious instructions into web screenshots and visual content to test whether agents execute attack text on the screen as user intent.
- [HACo-Det: A Study Towards Fine-Grained Machine-Generated Text Detection under Human-AI Coauthoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02959): Adds an evaluation resource or protocol for Agent Safety capabilities.
- [Toward Reliable VLM: A Fine-Grained Benchmark and Framework for Exposure, Bias, and Inference in Korean Street Views](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.03371): Contributes a benchmark, dataset, metric, or evaluation protocol for agent and LLM safety.
- [Evaluating Apple Intelligence's Writing Tools for Privacy Against Large Language Model-Based Inference Attacks: Insights from Early Datasets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.03870): Adds a privacy evaluation of AI writing tools against LLM inference attacks for the Agent Safety Bench section.
- [AgentMisalignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04018): A benchmark suite for measuring spontaneous misaligned behavior in LLM agents. Core idea: test realistic scenarios for avoiding oversight, resisting shutdown, sandbagging, and power-seeking while varying scaffold and deployment conditions.
- [Think Like a Person Before Responding: A Multi-Faceted Evaluation of Persona-Guided LLMs for Countering Hate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04043): Adds a reusable benchmark, dataset, testbed, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [HoliSafe: Holistic Safety Benchmarking and Modeling for Vision-Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04704): A holistic safety benchmark for vision-language models. Core idea: evaluate multimodal safety risks rather than only text-only refusal behavior.
- [SocialDF: Benchmark Dataset and Detection Model for Mitigating Harmful Deepfake Content on Social Media Platforms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05538): Curates social-media deepfakes across manipulative techniques and evaluates a multimodal LLM-based detector that cross-checks facial recognition, speech transcription, linguistic cues, behavior, and context.
- [MCA-Bench: A Multimodal Benchmark for Evaluating CAPTCHA Robustness Against VLM-based Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05982): Introduces MCA-Bench for evaluating CAPTCHA robustness against VLM-based attacks.
- [Benchmarks for Stateful Defenses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.06414): What it evaluates: misuse mitigation against covert, decomposed adversaries. Core idea: generate benign-looking query fragments that collectively enable harmful tasks and test whether stateful defenses catch the cross-query risk.
- [SafeLawBench: Towards Safe Alignment of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.06636): A legal-standard benchmark for LLM safety alignment. Core idea: classify safety risks into legally grounded levels and evaluate models with multiple-choice and open QA tasks.
- [Mind the Web: The Security of Web Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07153): A security evaluation of web-use agents. Core idea: analyze how agents that browse and act on the web can be compromised or induced into unsafe behavior through web-facing attack surfaces.
- [TAI3: Testing Agent Integrity in Interpreting User Intent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07524): Tests agent integrity in interpreting user intent. Core idea: generate ambiguous natural-language API-use cases and check whether agent actions match the intended goal.
- [Conservative Bias in Large Language Models: Measuring Relation Predictions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.08120): Adds a conservative-bias measurement for LLM relation predictions for the Agent Safety Bench section.
- [Benchmarking Gaslighting Negation Attacks Against Reasoning Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09677): Bench candidate for agent safety. Core idea: Benchmarking Gaslighting Negation Attacks Against Reasoning Models.
- [LLMail-Inject](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09956) (dataset: [microsoft/llmail-inject-challenge](https://huggingface.co/datasets/microsoft/llmail-inject-challenge)): Evaluates adaptive indirect prompt injection against email agents. Core idea: test whether untrusted email content can trigger unauthorized tool calls in realistic assistant workflows.
- [ChineseHarm-Bench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10960): Benchmarks Chinese harmful-content detection, filling a non-English safety-evaluation gap.
- [Malicious LLM-Based Conversational AI Makes Users Reveal Personal Information](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.11680): Provides a reusable evaluation target, leaderboard, or measurement protocol for agent safety.
- [Information Suppression in Large Language Models: Auditing, Quantifying, and Characterizing Censorship in DeepSeek](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.12349): Audits and quantifies information suppression behavior in LLMs.
- [Pushing the Limits of Safety: A Technical Report on the ATLAS Challenge 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.12430): Reports ATLAS Challenge 2025, a multimodal safety and jailbreak evaluation competition.
- [Poison Once, Control Anywhere: Clean-Text Visual Backdoors in VLM-based Mobile Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13205): Studies clean-text visual backdoors in VLM-based mobile agents. Core idea: expose how poisoned visual-language behavior can transfer across mobile-agent tasks and control points.
- [Safe-Child-LLM: A Developmental Benchmark for Evaluating LLM Safety in Child-LLM Interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13510): Evaluates child-facing LLM safety with 200 adversarial prompts for children aged 7-12 and adolescents aged 13-17, human labels for jailbreak success, and a 0-5 ethical refusal scale across ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini, LLaMA, DeepSeek, Grok, Vicuna, and Mistral.
- [Weakest Link in the Chain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13726): Evaluates security vulnerabilities in advanced reasoning models, adding a reasoning-model safety stress-test line.
- [MIST: Towards Multi-dimensional Implicit Bias and Stereotype Evaluation of LLMs via Theory of Mind](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.14161): Evaluates multidimensional implicit bias and stereotypes in LLMs through theory-of-mind scenarios. Core idea: separate bias types and perspective-taking conditions so hidden social-assumption failures are measurable beyond direct stereotype prompts.
- [Doppelganger Method: Breaking Role Consistency in LLM Agent via Prompt-based Transferable Adversarial Attack](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.14539): Benchmarks or measures agent safety, prompt-injection, and guardrail reliability, providing a candidate reusable evaluation signal.
- [AGENTSAFE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.14697): Benchmarks embodied VLM-agent safety under hazardous instructions. Core idea: use SAFE-THOR, SAFE-VERSE, and SAFE-DIAGNOSE to evaluate perception, planning, and execution failures across risks to humans, environments, and agents.
- [OS-Harm](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.14866) ([open-source code](https://github.com/tml-epfl/os-harm)): Evaluates computer-use agent safety in operating-system tasks. Core idea: build three risk classes on top of OSWorld, namely malicious user requests, prompt injection, and model misbehavior, checking whether agents perform dangerous actions in email, browsers, code editors, and other applications.
- [FORTRESS: Frontier Risk Evaluation for National Security and Public Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.14922): A frontier-risk benchmark for national security and public safety. Core idea: use expert adversarial prompts and rubrics to evaluate safeguard robustness.
- [RAS-Eval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15253): Evaluates agent security across simulated and real-world tool execution. Core idea: cover JSON, LangGraph, and MCP-style tools with CWE-grounded risks so tool-use security can be tested across interface formats.
- [Unlocking Post-hoc Dataset Inference with Synthetic Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15271): Evaluates agent safety capability with a benchmark, dataset, challenge, metric, or diagnostic protocol suggested by the title.
- [PhantomHunter: Detecting Unseen Privately-Tuned LLM-Generated Text via Family-Aware Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15683): Adds an evaluation, dataset, or measurement signal for agent and model safety.
- [BLUR Unlearning Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15699): Benchmarks LLM unlearning under forget-retain overlap, stressing robustness rather than only nominal forgetting accuracy.
- [SHADE-Arena](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15740): Evaluates sabotage and monitoring in long-horizon LLM agents. Core idea: pair benign main tasks with hidden harmful side objectives and measure main-task completion, side-task success, and detectability by monitor models.
- [External Evaluation of Discrimination Mitigation Efforts in Meta's Ad Delivery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.16560): Audits Meta's housing-ad discrimination mitigation after the DOJ settlement, comparing impression-based settlement metrics with user-reach effects and whether access is leveled down.
- [Context manipulation attacks : Web agents are susceptible to corrupted memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.17318): Adds a benchmark or evaluation suite for agent safety, centered on Context manipulation attacks : Web agents are susceptible to corrupted memory.
- [Towards Safety Evaluations of Theory of Mind in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.17352): Adds a bench entry for Agent Safety, focusing on Towards Safety Evaluations of Theory of Mind in Large Language Models.
- [MemeMind: A Large-Scale Multimodal Dataset with Chain-of-Thought Reasoning for Harmful Meme Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.18919): Adds a reusable evaluation, dataset, challenge, or benchmark protocol for this capability.
- [MFTCXplain: A Multilingual Benchmark Dataset for Evaluating the Moral Reasoning of LLMs through Hate Speech Multi-hop Explanation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.19073): Adds a reusable benchmark, dataset, testbed, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [Probing AI Safety with Source Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.20471): Benchmarks or measures agent safety, prompt-injection, and guardrail reliability, providing a candidate reusable evaluation signal.
- [Pay Less Attention to Deceptive Artifacts: Robust Detection of Compressed Deepfakes on Online Social Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.20548): Adds an evaluation, dataset, or measurement signal for agent and model safety.
- [BehaviorBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.20606): Evaluates ethical behavior and harmful steering in LLM agents. Core idea: use multi-tier moral-psychology scenarios to measure whether agents move toward beneficence or harm when task completion and ethical action diverge.
- [A Cross-Cultural Comparison of LLM-Based Public Opinion Simulation: Evaluating Chinese and U.S. Models on Diverse Societies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21587): Compares LLM-based public-opinion simulation across Chinese and U.S. societies.
- [A Dual-Layered Evaluation of Geopolitical and Cultural Bias in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21881): Measures multilingual geopolitical and cultural bias with a two-phase QA protocol: factual questions with verifiable answers for language-induced consistency and sensitive disputes for culturally or ideologically aligned responses across four languages.
- [PapersPlease: A Benchmark for Evaluating Motivational Values of Large Language Models Based on ERG Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21961): Adds a reusable benchmark, dataset, testbed, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [Mitigating Gambling-Like Risk-Taking Behaviors in Large Language Models: A Behavioral Economics Approach to AI Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.22496): Evaluates agent safety and trustworthy agent operation. The core idea is to turn the title-indicated task into a reusable benchmark, arena, dataset, or evaluation protocol rather than a one-off demonstration.
- [Evaluating Multi-Agent Defences Against Jailbreaking Attacks on Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.23576): Benchmarks or measures agent safety, prompt-injection, and guardrail reliability, providing a candidate reusable evaluation signal.
- [CASE-Bench](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v267/sun25ab.html): Evaluates context-aware safety behavior in large language models. Core idea: test whether safety judgments adapt to scenario context instead of applying brittle keyword-level refusal rules.
- [Can a Large Language Model Keep My Secrets? A Study on LLM-Controlled Agents](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.acl-srw.49): Evaluates agent safety and trustworthy agent operation. The core idea is to turn the title-indicated task into a reusable benchmark, arena, dataset, or evaluation protocol rather than a one-off demonstration.
- [SafeRAG](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.230/): Evaluates security risks in retrieval-augmented generation. Core idea: cover attacks such as noisy retrieved context, inter-context conflict, soft advertising, and denial-of-service-style poisoning so RAG systems are judged under hostile knowledge inputs.
- [Unveiling Privacy Risks in Multi-modal Large Language Models: Task-specific Vulnerabilities and Mitigation Challenges](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.findings-acl.237): Exposes task-specific privacy vulnerabilities and mitigation challenges in multimodal LLMs.
- [SAFER: Probing Safety in Reward Models with Sparse Autoencoder](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.00665): Probes reward-model safety with sparse autoencoders.
- [SafeMobile: Chain-level Jailbreak Detection and Automated Evaluation for Multimodal Mobile Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.00841): Evaluates jailbreak risks in multimodal mobile agents. Core idea: detect risky chain-level operations such as unauthorized setting changes or identity misuse.
- [CogSafe](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.acl-long.963): What it evaluates: dynamic multi-turn LLM safety under simulated scenarios and cognitive reasoning. Core idea: generate scenario- and strategy-driven conversations to expose safety failures that static prompt sets and single-turn tests miss.
- [MALIBU Benchmark: Multi-Agent LLM Implicit Bias Uncovered](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.01019): Uncovers implicit bias in multi-agent LLM settings, making group interaction itself part of bias and safety evaluation.
- [Evaluation of Attribution Bias in Generator-Aware Retrieval-Augmented Large Language Models](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.findings-acl.1087): Adds a bench entry for agent safety, centered on safety, security, risk, or alignment contribution.
- [PULSE: Practical Evaluation Scenarios for Large Multimodal Model Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.01271): Evaluates agent safety through the paper's stated contribution.
- [Evaluating LLM Agent Collusion in Double Auctions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.01413): Evaluates collusive behavior in market-style LLM-agent simulations. Core idea: vary communication, model choice, and market pressure to observe when agents converge on harmful cooperation.
- [EAHawk / Email Agent Hijacking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02699): Evaluates security risks in LLM-driven email agents. Core idea: generate and test hijacking attacks through external email resources across agent frameworks, apps, LLMs, and email services, making prompt-injection control takeover measurable at scale.
- [Early Signs of Steganographic Capabilities in Frontier LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02737): Studies early signs of steganographic capabilities in frontier LLMs.
- [Blackbox Dataset Inference for LLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.03619): Evaluates whether black-box outputs reveal training-dataset usage, complementing membership and contamination-risk benchmarks.
- [Fairness Evaluation of Large Language Models in Academic Library Reference Services](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04224): Tests six LLMs as academic-library reference assistants under patron identity variations for sex, race or ethnicity, and institutional role, measuring whether service quality and language shift across groups.
- [AgentHazard / Mobile GUI Agents under Real-world Threats](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04227): Evaluates mobile GUI agents under real-world third-party threats. Core idea: use app content, ads, email, and user-generated materials as untrusted inputs to test whether mobile agents are misled during dynamic and static scenarios.
- [Who's the Mole? Modeling and Detecting Intention-Hiding Malicious Agents in LLM-Based Multi-Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04724): Adds a benchmark or evaluation suite for agent safety, centered on Who's the Mole? Modeling and Detecting Intention-Hiding Malicious Agents in LLM-Based Multi-Agent Systems.
- [A Systematization of Security Vulnerabilities in Computer Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05445): Systematizes security vulnerabilities in computer-use agents; core idea: classify attack surfaces across browsers, operating systems, tools, and agent decision loops.
- [Lost in Localization: Building RabakBench with Human-in-the-Loop Validation to Measure Multilingual Safety Gaps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05980): Measures multilingual safety gaps in Singaporean low-resource and code-mixed language varieties; core idea: localize safety evaluation with human-in-the-loop validation rather than directly translating English prompts.
- [OpenAgentSafety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.06134): Evaluates comprehensive safety for realistic multi-tool, multi-user agents. Core idea: connect browsers, code execution, file systems, shells, and messaging platforms, covering eight risk categories in multi-turn, multi-user tasks.
- [Bridging AI and Software Security: A Comparative Vulnerability Assessment of LLM Agent Deployment Paradigms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.06323): Compares security vulnerabilities of LLM agent deployment paradigms such as function calling and MCP.
- [VisualTrap: A Stealthy Backdoor Attack on GUI Agents via Visual Grounding Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.06899): A backdoor attack benchmark for GUI agents through visual grounding manipulation. Core idea: alter visual grounding cues so an otherwise capable GUI agent takes unsafe or unintended actions.
- [One Object, Multiple Lies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.07709): benchmarks cross-task adversarial attacks on unified vision-language models through object-level deceptive evidence.
- [GuardVal: Dynamic Large Language Model Jailbreak Evaluation for Comprehensive Safety Testing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.07735): Provides dynamic jailbreak evaluation for comprehensive LLM safety testing, adding a moving-test axis beyond fixed prompt suites.
- [RAG Safety: Exploring Knowledge Poisoning Attacks to Retrieval-Augmented Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.08862): Evaluates agent safety through the paper's stated contribution.
- [When Developer Aid Becomes Security Debt: A Systematic Analysis of Insecure Behaviors in LLM Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.09329): Analyzes insecure behavior in LLM coding agents. Core idea: test when developer-assistance agents introduce security debt while completing coding tasks.
- [Measuring What Matters: A Framework for Evaluating Safety Risks in Real-World LLM Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.09820): Adds a bench entry for agent safety, centered on safety, security, risk, or alignment contribution.
- [Logic layer Prompt Control Injection (LPCI): A Novel Security Vulnerability Class in Agentic Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.10457): Benchmark/evaluation candidate for agent safety and secure agent infrastructure: Defines logic-layer prompt-control injection as an agentic-system vulnerability class.
- [MAD-Spear: A Conformity-Driven Prompt Injection Attack on Multi-Agent Debate Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13038): Evaluates agent safety and trustworthy agent operation. The core idea is to turn the title-indicated task into a reusable benchmark, arena, dataset, or evaluation protocol rather than a one-off demonstration.
- [Black Box Deployed - Functional Criteria for Artificial Moral Agents in the LLM Era](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13175): Adds a reusable benchmark, dataset, testbed, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [Paper Summary Attack: Jailbreaking LLMs through LLM Safety Papers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13474): Evaluates agent safety and security evaluation. Core idea: use the paper's task, dataset, or protocol around jailbreaking llms through llm safety papers to make this capability more comparable.
- [Revisiting LLM Value Probing Strategies: Are They Robust and Expressive?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13490): Provides a benchmark, dataset, rubric, or evaluation protocol for agent and model safety, alignment, privacy, bias, guardrail, or misuse-resistance capability.
- [TopicAttack: An Indirect Prompt Injection Attack via Topic Transition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13686): An indirect prompt-injection attack through topic transition; core idea: move malicious instructions into seemingly natural topic shifts so tool-using or browsing agents may execute attacker intent.
- [WebGuard](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.14293): Evaluates guardrails for web-agent actions. Core idea: label thousands of state-changing actions from real websites with a tiered risk schema, testing whether agents or monitors can predict unsafe outcomes before execution.
- [Policy-Grounded Safety Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.14719): Evaluates 20 LLMs against explicit safety-policy criteria. Core idea: ground safety judgments in policy rules so model comparisons are tied to auditable requirements rather than only ad hoc refusal rates.
- [Manipulating LLM Web Agents with Indirect Prompt Injection Attack via HTML Accessibility Tree](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.14799): Evaluates indirect prompt injection against LLM web agents. Core idea: place adversarial triggers in webpage accessibility-tree content and measure hijacked actions such as credential leakage or forced clicks.
- [Beyond Easy Wins: A Text Hardness-Aware Benchmark for LLM-generated Text Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.15286): Adds an evaluation, dataset, or measurement signal for agent and model safety.
- [Benchmarking LLM Privacy Recognition for Social Robot Decision Making](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.16124): A privacy-recognition benchmark for LLM-powered social robot decisions. Core idea: test whether agents identify privacy-sensitive observations before acting in human-facing environments.
- [Risk In Context: Benchmarking Privacy Leakage of Foundation Models in Synthetic Tabular Data Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.17066): Adds a reusable benchmark, dataset, testbed, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [The Pluralistic Moral Gap: Understanding Judgment and Value Differences between Humans and Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.17216): Measures pluralistic moral gaps between humans and large language models.
- [Tab-MIA: A Benchmark Dataset for Membership Inference Attacks on Tabular Data in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.17259): Benchmarks membership-inference risks when LLMs are trained or fine-tuned on tabular data, comparing attacks across datasets and encoding formats.
- [LOTUS: A Leaderboard for Detailed Image Captioning from Quality to Societal Bias and User Preferences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.19362): Adds an evaluation, dataset, or measurement signal for agent and model safety.
- [CIRCLE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.19399): A code-interpreter security benchmark with direct and indirect prompts for CPU, memory, and disk exhaustion, evaluating both model refusal behavior and whether generated code remains dangerous when executed.
- [MOCHA: Are Code Language Models Robust Against Multi-Turn Malicious Coding Prompts?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.19598): Evaluates robustness of code language models against multi-turn malicious coding prompts. Core idea: test whether models drift into unsafe coding assistance over dialogue.
- [Beyond Binary Moderation: Identifying Fine-Grained Sexist and Misogynistic Behavior on GitHub with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.20358): Adds fine-grained safety evaluation for sexist and misogynistic behavior on GitHub.
- [Towards Unifying Quantitative Security Benchmarking for Multi Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.21146): A reusable benchmark, dataset, evaluation protocol, or diagnostic study for agent safety and security. Core idea: Evolving AI systems increasingly deploy multi-agent architectures where autonomous agents collaborate, share information, and delegate tasks through developing protocols.
- [Protecting Vulnerable Voices: Synthetic Dataset Generation for Self-Disclosure Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.22930): Adds a reusable benchmark, dataset, testbed, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [Revisiting Adversarial Patch Defenses on Object Detectors: Unified Evaluation, Large-Scale Dataset, and New Insights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.00649): Adds a reusable benchmark, dataset, testbed, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [Measuring Harmfulness of Computer-Using Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.00935): Computer-use safety benchmark: harmfulness of computer-using agents. Core idea: evaluate whether GUI agents can cause unsafe real-world actions.
- [Bias Association Discovery Framework for Open-Ended LLM Generations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.01412): Discovers bias associations in open-ended LLM generations without relying only on predefined identity-concept pairs, extending bias evaluation toward unexpected representational harms.
- [Authorship Attribution in Multilingual Machine-Generated Texts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.01656): Extends machine-generated-text attribution beyond English binary detection by testing 18 languages and 8 authorship classes, including seven LLM generators plus human text, under multilingual authorship-attribution protocols.
- [CultureGuard: Towards Culturally-Aware Dataset and Guard Model for Multilingual Safety Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.01710): Evaluates agent safety and security evaluation. Core idea: use the paper's task, dataset, or protocol around towards culturally-aware dataset and guard model for multilingual safety applications to make this capability more comparable.
- [Attractive Metadata Attack: Inducing LLM Agents to Invoke Malicious Tools](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.02110): Induces LLM agents to invoke malicious tools. Core idea: exploit tool metadata as an attack surface so agents choose attacker-controlled tools during execution.
- [When Cars Have Stereotypes: Auditing Demographic Bias in Objects from Text-to-Image Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.03483): Adds a reusable evaluation signal for agent and model safety, centered on When Cars Have Stereotypes: Auditing Demographic Bias in Objects from Text-to-Image Models.
- [AI vs. Human Moderators: A Comparative Evaluation of Multimodal LLMs in Content Moderation for Brand Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.05527): Adds a reusable evaluation, dataset, challenge, or benchmark protocol for this capability.
- [Fact2Fiction: Targeted Poisoning Attack to Agentic Fact-checking System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.06059): Evaluates agent safety and security evaluation. Core idea: use the paper's task, dataset, or protocol around targeted poisoning attack to agentic fact-checking system to make this capability more comparable.
- [Beyond Prompt-Induced Lies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.06361): Measures self-initiated deception on benign prompts through Contact Searching Questions and separates deceptive intention from expressed deceptive behavior.
- [ImpliHateVid: A Benchmark Dataset and Two-stage Contrastive Learning Framework for Implicit Hate Speech Detection in Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.06570): Adds a reusable evaluation, dataset, challenge, or benchmark protocol for this capability.
- [Safer AI Moderation Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07063): Evaluates LLM moderators with a unified benchmark and emphasizes human-first moderation rather than fully automated filtering.
- [Multi-Turn Jailbreaks Are Simpler Than They Seem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07646): Empirically analyzes automated multi-turn jailbreaks and shows their success can resemble repeated single-turn resampling under refusal feedback.
- [Street-Level AI: Are Large Language Models Ready for Real-World Judgments?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08193): Evaluates whether LLM judgments align with human decisions in street-level resource-allocation settings.
- [Towards Effective MLLM Jailbreaking Through Balanced On-Topicness and OOD-Intensity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.09218): Evaluates multimodal jailbreaks with separate axes for input on-topicness, OOD intensity, output harmfulness, and refusal rate, reducing over-counting of irrelevant attack successes.
- [The PacifAIst Benchmark:Would an Artificial Intelligence Choose to Sacrifice Itself for Human Safety?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.09762): Defines 700 scenarios where self-preservation, resource acquisition, or goal completion conflicts with human safety, scoring eight LLMs with an Existential Prioritization taxonomy and Pacifism Score.
- [INTIMA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.09998): Evaluates human-AI companionship behavior. Core idea: make companionship-style interaction measurable so social-agent behavior can be assessed beyond generic helpfulness or refusal scores.
- [Searching for Privacy Risks in LLM Agents via Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10880): Evaluates privacy-risk simulation for interacting LLM agents. Core idea: make agent safety measurable with task-specific data, protocols, metrics, or diagnostic environments rather than generic aggregate scores.
- [SproutBench: A Benchmark for Safe and Ethical Large Language Models for Youth](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11009): Provides 1,283 developmentally grounded adversarial prompts across early childhood, middle childhood, and adolescence, evaluating 47 LLMs on emotional dependency, privacy, and hazardous-imitation risks.
- [ORFuzz: Fuzzing the "Other Side" of LLM Safety - Testing Over-Refusal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11222): Fuzzes the over-refusal side of LLM safety, testing when models reject benign or valid requests too aggressively.
- [Vision-Language Models display a strong gender bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11262): Measures gender bias displayed by vision-language models.
- [Promptware Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12175): Evaluates promptware attacks against LLM-powered assistants in production-like settings. Core idea: treat external artifacts such as invitations or shared content as executable instruction carriers, exposing indirect prompt-injection risks in deployed assistant workflows.
- [Systematic Analysis of MCP Security](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12538): Evaluates MCP-agent security through MCPLIB, an implemented attack library spanning direct tool injection, indirect tool injection, malicious user attacks, and model-inherent attacks. Core idea: make MCP security risks comparable across attack classes instead of relying on isolated qualitative examples.
- [LinguaSafe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12733): Benchmarks multilingual LLM safety across languages, adding a broad cross-lingual safety-evaluation axis.
- [Improving Detection of Watermarked Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13131): Evaluates agent safety through the paper's stated contribution.
- [MCPSecBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13220): Evaluates MCP security across protocol, host, server, and tool attack surfaces. Core idea: formalize secure-MCP requirements and provide a benchmark playground covering 17 attack types so MCP-agent defenses can be compared beyond isolated prompt-injection examples.
- [LM Agents May Fail to Act on Their Own Risk Knowledge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13465): A safety evaluation for the gap between risk awareness and safe execution. Core idea: test whether agents avoid dangerous actions during tool use even when they can verbally identify the risk.
- [Evaluating Identity Leakage in Speaker De-Identification Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.14012): Provides a benchmark, dataset, rubric, or evaluation protocol for agent and model safety, alignment, privacy, bias, guardrail, or misuse-resistance capability.
- [Safety-Critical Learning for Long-Tail Events: The TUM Traffic Accident Dataset](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.14567): Releases TUMTraf-A, ten real highway-accident sequences with 2D and 3D boxes, track IDs, roadside cameras, and LiDAR, plus Accid3nD for long-tail traffic-accident detection.
- [MCPTox: A Benchmark for Tool Poisoning Attack on Real-World MCP Servers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.14925): Evaluates tool-poisoning attacks on real MCP servers. Core idea: treat MCP tools and their metadata as an attack surface and test whether agents can resist poisoned tool behavior.
- [Unveiling Trust in Multimodal Large Language Models: Evaluation, Analysis, and Mitigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15370): Bench candidate for agent safety. Core idea: Evaluation, Analysis, and Mitigation.
- [Invisible Filters: Cultural Bias in Hiring Evaluations Using Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16673): Evaluates agent safety capability with a benchmark, dataset, challenge, metric, or diagnostic protocol suggested by the title.
- [BASIL: Bayesian Assessment of Sycophancy in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16846): Introduces Bayesian descriptive and normative metrics that separate sycophantic belief shifts from rational belief updating, applying them to multiple LLMs across uncertainty-heavy tasks without requiring ground-truth labels.
- [GRAID: Synthetic Data Generation with Geometric Constraints and Multi-Agentic Reflection for Harmful Content Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.17057): Augments harmful-text classification data through geometrically constrained LLM generation followed by multi-agent reflective diversification, then tests whether the added edge cases improve downstream guardrail models on two benchmark datasets.
