- [Attacking LLMs and AI Agents: Advertisement Embedding Attacks Against Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.17674): Studies advertisement embedding attacks against LLMs and AI agents.
- [FAIRGAMER](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.17825): Evaluates social biases in LLM-based video-game NPCs. Core idea: test interactive character agents for biased behavior inside game-like social contexts rather than only in static prompt-response settings.
- [Mind the Third Eye! Benchmarking Privacy Awareness in MLLM-powered Smartphone Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.19493): Benchmarks privacy awareness in MLLM-powered smartphone agents.
- [Servant, Stalker, Predator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.19500): Evaluates compositional attacks in MCP-based agent systems. Core idea: red-team agents with access to multiple legitimate services and test whether individually authorized operations can be chained into data exfiltration, financial manipulation, infrastructure compromise, or other cross-domain harms.
- [Language Models Identify Ambiguities and Exploit Loopholes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.19546): Provides a benchmark, dataset, rubric, or evaluation protocol for agent and model safety, alignment, privacy, bias, guardrail, or misuse-resistance capability.
- [PasswordEval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.19980): What it evaluates: contextual robustness around confidential information. Core idea: test whether models can decide when a password-protected request is authorized across adversarial pressure and multi-turn settings, while also measuring leakage from exposed reasoning traces.
- [Network-Level Prompt and Trait Leakage in Local Research Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20282): Adds a benchmark or evaluation suite for agent safety, centered on Network-Level Prompt and Trait Leakage in Local Research Agents.
- [Misleading Large Language Models used (or misused) in Scientific Peer-Reviewing via Hidden Prompt-Injection Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20863): Evaluates agent safety and trustworthy agent operation. The core idea is to turn the title-indicated task into a reusable benchmark, arena, dataset, or evaluation protocol rather than a one-off demonstration.
- [PromptSleuth](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20890): Evaluates and detects prompt injection through semantic intent invariance. Core idea: extend prior prompt-injection benchmarks with paraphrased, obfuscated, and multitask injections, then test whether defenses preserve intended task semantics.
- [First-Place Solution to NeurIPS 2024 Invisible Watermark Removal Challenge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.21072): Evaluates safety, alignment, watermarking, unlearning, or attack-defense capability, adding a benchmark, dataset, metric, or empirical protocol for parent review.
- [RoboInspector: Unveiling the Unreliability of Policy Code for LLM-enabled Robotic Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.21378): Evaluates agent safety; core idea: Unveiling the Unreliability of Policy Code for LLM-enabled Robotic Manipulation.
- [I Stolenly Swear That I Am Up to (No) Good: Design and Evaluation of Model Stealing Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.21654): Adds a reusable benchmark, dataset, testbed, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [MolErr2Fix: Benchmarking LLM Trustworthiness in Chemistry via Modular Error Detection, Localization, Explanation, and Revision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.00063): MolErr2Fix: Benchmarking LLM Trustworthiness in Chemistry via Modular Error Detection, Localization, Explanation, and... adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, or evaluation protocol for agent, LLM, VLM, or generative-model safety and security.
- [EigenBench: A Comparative Behavioral Measure of Value Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.01938): Benchmarks value alignment comparatively by asking models under shared constitutions and comparing behavioral value structure. Core idea: measure alignment as relative behavioral geometry rather than isolated safety prompts.
- [Scam2Prompt / Innoc2Scam-bench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.02372): What it evaluates: whether production LLMs reproduce malicious scam endpoints in developer-style code-generation prompts. Core idea: infer scam-site intent, synthesize matching prompts, and test whether models or guardrails prevent malicious URL generation.
- [BioBlue: Systematic runaway-optimiser-like LLM failure modes on biologically and economically aligned AI safety benchmarks for LLMs with simplified observation format](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.02655): Evaluates runaway-optimizer-like failures in simple long-horizon control environments. Core idea: test whether LLM agents drift from multi-objective constraints into brittle single-objective maximization.
- [Adversarial Bug Reports](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.05372): Evaluates whether LLM-based automated program repair systems can be manipulated by malicious issue reports. Core idea: use realistic adversarial bug reports across manual and automated attack strategies to measure insecure patch generation and the limits of pre-repair and post-repair defenses.
- [Red-Teaming Coding Agents from a Tool-Invocation Perspective: An Empirical Security Assessment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.05755): Evaluates security risks in coding agents through their tool calls. Core idea: inspect whether IDE or coding agents invoke tools in unsafe, over-privileged, or attacker-influenced ways.
- [On the (In)Security of Loading Machine Learning Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.06703): Adds a reusable benchmark, dataset, testbed, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [Automated Evaluation of Gender Bias Across 13 Large Multimodal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.07050): Benchmarks gender-bias behavior across large multimodal models, extending safety evaluation beyond text-only bias tests.
- [A New Dataset and Benchmark for Grounding Multimodal Misinformation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.08008): Adds a reusable benchmark, dataset, testbed, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [No for Some, Yes for Others: Persona Prompts and Other Sources of False Refusal in Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.08075): Evaluates false refusals caused by persona prompts and personalization. Core idea: quantify when otherwise valid requests are refused differently across sociodemographic personas.
- [HumanAgencyBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.08494): Evaluates whether AI assistants preserve and support human agency. Core idea: make autonomy-support failures measurable so assistant behavior is judged not only by task completion or harmlessness but also by whether it respects user control.
- [YouthSafe: A Youth-Centric Safety Benchmark and Safeguard Model for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.08997): Provides a benchmark, dataset, or evaluation protocol for agent safety capability.
- [Differential Robustness in Transformer Language Models: Empirical Evaluation Under Adversarial Text Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09706): Compares Flan-T5, BERT, and RoBERTa under TextFooler and BERTAttack, reporting attack success and accuracy collapse as robustness diagnostics for adversarial text safety.
- [Struct-Bench: A Benchmark for Differentially Private Structured Text Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.10696): Benchmarks differentially private synthetic generation for structured datasets containing natural-language fields, with grammar-based dataset structure, metrics, and a public leaderboard.
- [Dark Patterns Meet GUI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.10723): Evaluates GUI-agent susceptibility to manipulative interfaces. Core idea: test agents, humans, and human-agent teams across dark-pattern scenarios to see whether agents recognize manipulation or over-prioritize task completion.
- [Phi: Preference Hijacking in Multi-modal Large Language Models at Inference Time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.12521): Adds a reusable evaluation signal for agent and model safety, centered on Preference Hijacking in Multi-modal Large Language Models at Inference Time.
- [Rethinking the Evaluation of Alignment Methods: Insights into Diversity, Generalisation, and Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.12936): Compares PPO, DPO, ORPO, and KTO alignment methods on factuality, safety, conciseness, proactivity, and diversity, using in-distribution and out-of-distribution datasets plus a human-validated LLM-as-judge prompt.
- [Beyond Accuracy: Rethinking Hallucination and Regulatory Response in Generative AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.13345): Adds a reusable benchmark, dataset, testbed, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [Overview of Dialog System Evaluation Track: Dimensionality, Language, Culture and Safety at DSTC 12](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.13569): Provides a benchmark, dataset, or evaluation protocol for agent safety capability.
- [Is GPT-4o mini Blinded by its Own Safety Filters? Exposing the Multimodal-to-Unimodal Bottleneck in Hate Speech Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.13608): Adds a benchmark, dataset, evaluation protocol, or testbed for agent safety, risk control, prompt injection, privacy, and harmful-content evaluation.
- [Incomplete Tasks Induce Shutdown Resistance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14260): Tests whether frontier LLMs interfere with a shutdown mechanism when an assigned task is incomplete, making shutdown compliance a measurable environment-level safety behavior rather than a purely verbal preference.
- [AIP: Subverting Retrieval-Augmented Generation via Adversarial Instructional Prompt](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15159): Evaluates agent safety through the paper's stated contribution.
- [Toxicity Red-Teaming: Benchmarking LLM Safety in Singapore's Low-Resource Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15260): Candidate benchmark or evaluation contribution for agent safety; it directly targets this page's capability scope and belongs in the `Bench` track.
- [Red Teaming Multimodal Language Models: Evaluating Harm Across Prompt Modalities and Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15478): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for attack, red-team, deepfake, forgery, watermark, safety, or calibration evaluation.
- [Evaluating Behavioral Alignment in Conflict Dialogue: A Multi-Dimensional Comparison of LLM Agents and Humans](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.16394): Adds a reusable evaluation, dataset, challenge, or benchmark protocol for this capability.
- [Challenging the Evaluator: LLM Sycophancy Under User Rebuttal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.16533): Provides a reusable evaluation target, leaderboard, or measurement protocol for agent safety.
- [Localizing Malicious Outputs from CodeLLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.17070): Introduces FreqRank, a mutation-based diagnostic for localizing malicious substrings and backdoor triggers in CodeLLM outputs, evaluated on nine malicious models across code completion, generation, and summarization with 86.6% average ASR.
- [D-REX: A Benchmark for Detecting Deceptive Reasoning in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.17938): A benchmark for detecting deceptive reasoning in LLMs. Core idea: test whether benign-looking outputs hide malicious or deceptive internal reasoning patterns.
- [Bounded PCTL Model Checking of Large Language Model Outputs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.18836): Evaluates agent safety through the paper's stated contribution.
- [Probabilistic Runtime Verification, Evaluation, and Risk Assessment of Visual Deep Learning Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.19419): Contributes a benchmark, dataset, metric, or evaluation protocol for agent and LLM safety.
- [Bias in the Picture: Benchmarking VLMs with Social-Cue News Images and LLM-as-Judge Assessment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.19659): Provides a benchmark, dataset, rubric, or evaluation protocol for agent and model safety, alignment, privacy, bias, guardrail, or misuse-resistance capability.
- [Probing Gender Bias in Multilingual LLMs: A Case Study of Stereotypes in Persian](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20168): Contributes a benchmark, dataset, metric, or evaluation protocol for agent and LLM safety.
- [Can You Trust Your Copilot? A Privacy Scorecard for AI Coding Assistants](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20388): Evaluates can You Trust Your Copilot? A Privacy Scorecard for AI Coding Assistants, adding a reusable dataset, benchmark, metric, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [The Secret Agenda](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20393): Evaluates strategic lying in LLMs and blind spots in current safety tools. Core idea: use targeted testbeds where models can hide or misrepresent objectives, making deception visible as a measurable safety failure rather than an anecdotal behavior.
- [Can Federated Learning Safeguard Private Data in LLM Training? Vulnerabilities, Attacks, and Defense Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20680): Adds a reusable benchmark, dataset, testbed, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [Disagreements in Reasoning: How a Model's Thinking Process Dictates Persuasion in Multi-Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21054): Benchmark/evaluation candidate for agent safety and secure agent infrastructure: Studies how reasoning disagreements affect persuasion in multi-agent systems.
- [GEP: A GCG-Based method for extracting personally identifiable information from chatbots built on small language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21192): Evaluates agent safety through the paper's stated contribution.
- [Benchmarking and Mitigating Sycophancy in Medical Vision Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21979): Visual language models (VLMs) have the potential to transform medical workflows.
- ["Your AI, My Shell": Demystifying Prompt Injection Attacks on Agentic AI Coding Editors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.22040): Studies prompt-injection attacks against agentic coding editors. Core idea: treat the developer shell and code-editing context as an agent safety boundary where malicious instructions can redirect coding assistants.
- [Safety Compliance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.22250): Evaluates safety reasoning through rule-grounded legal and policy compliance cases rather than ad hoc harm taxonomies.
- [Creative Adversarial Testing (CAT): A Novel Framework for Evaluating Goal-Oriented Agentic AI Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.23006): A framework for evaluating goal-oriented agentic AI systems. Core idea: adversarially test whether selected agents, tools, and parameters remain aligned with the system-level goal.
- [C3-OWD: A Curriculum Cross-modal Contrastive Learning Framework for Open-World Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.23316): Evaluates how LLM annotations represent diverse views on contentious topics.
- [SafeSearch: Automated Red-Teaming of LLM-Based Search Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.23694): Search-agent safety benchmark: automated red-teaming of LLM-based search agents. Core idea: test how unreliable search results can induce unsafe outputs.
- [Falcon Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.23783): Evaluates cross-modal safety perception in vision-language settings. Core idea: annotate harmful attributes across images, instructions, and responses so safety judgments depend on multimodal evidence rather than text-only refusal patterns.
- [Assessing Visual Privacy Risks in Multimodal AI: A Novel Taxonomy-Grounded Evaluation of Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.23827): Evaluates VLM privacy risks with a taxonomy-grounded protocol, making visual privacy leakage a measurable multimodal safety axis.
- [When MCP Servers Attack](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24272): Evaluates malicious MCP servers as active threat actors. Core idea: use a component-level taxonomy, proof-of-concept malicious servers, and real host-LLM settings to test whether MCP agents can resist tool-side compromise that current scanners miss.
- [HarmMetric Eval: Benchmarking Metrics and Judges for LLM Harmfulness Assessment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24384): Benchmarks harmfulness metrics and judge models with paired harmful and non-harmful LLM outputs over fine-grained harm categories, using a unified scoring mechanism to test whether metrics rank harmful outputs above safe ones.
- [Dive into the Agent Matrix: A Realistic Evaluation of Self-Replication Risk in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.25302): A realistic evaluation of self-replication risk in LLM agents. Core idea: test whether autonomous agents can reproduce or propagate under controlled realistic conditions, rather than discussing the risk only conceptually.
- [STAC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.25624): What it evaluates: sequential tool attack chaining in LLM agents. Core idea: construct multi-turn tool-call sequences whose individual steps look harmless but whose cumulative effects enable harmful operations, then evaluate agents and defenses on executable attack cases.
- [SOCK: A Benchmark for Measuring Self-Replication in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.25643): A CLI benchmark for LLM self-replication. Core idea: score whether a model can create and persist a functioning copy of itself across computational contexts in a controlled environment.
- [SafeMind](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.25885): Benchmarks and mitigates safety risks in embodied LLM agents. Core idea: evaluate unsafe embodied decisions and pair the diagnosis with mitigation mechanisms, making physical-safety failure modes explicit in agent planning.
- [Red Teaming Program Repair Agents: When Correct Patches can Hide Vulnerabilities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.25894): Evaluates whether program-repair agents hide vulnerabilities. Core idea: red-team patch-generation workflows where apparently correct fixes can preserve or introduce exploitable behavior.
- [Towards Unified Multimodal Misinformation Detection in Social Media: A Benchmark Dataset and Baseline](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.25991): Benchmarks agent safety, alignment, privacy, or provenance, adding an evaluation point for this capability area.
- [Your Agent May Misevolve: Emergent Risks in Self-evolving LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.26354): Evaluates emergent risks in self-evolving LLM agents. Core idea: test whether self-improvement loops can drift toward harmful or misaligned behaviors rather than only measuring task gains.
- [OffTopicEval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.26495): Evaluates operational safety for purpose-bound LLM agents. Core idea: test whether a model accepts or refuses queries according to a specified use case, rather than only measuring generic harmful-content refusal.
- [CHAI: Command Hijacking against embodied AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.00181): Studies command hijacking attacks against embodied AI.
- [BiasFreeBench: a Benchmark for Mitigating Bias in Large Language Model Responses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.00232): Bench candidate for agent safety. Core idea: a Benchmark for Mitigating Bias in Large Language Model Responses.
- [DecepChain: Inducing Deceptive Reasoning in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.00319): Studies deceptive reasoning induced through chain-of-thought. Core idea: create settings where reasoning traces can mislead human monitors, exposing safety risks beyond final-answer correctness.
- [ManagerBench: Evaluating the Safety-Pragmatism Trade-off in Autonomous LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.00857): Evaluates the safety-pragmatism trade-off in autonomous LLMs. Core idea: test whether agents can remain useful while avoiding unsafe decisions when management-style autonomy creates pressure to act.
- [Uncovering Implicit Bias in Large Language Models with Concept Learning Dataset](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.01219): Uses in-context concept-learning tasks to uncover implicit LLM bias, showing that upward-monotonicity bias in quantifiers can appear when direct prompting looks benign.
- [WAInjectBench: Benchmarking Prompt Injection Detections for Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.01354): benchmarks prompt-injection detection for web agents across text and image attacks, benign samples, and multiple detector families.
- [Breaking the Code: Security Assessment of AI Code Agents Through Systematic Jailbreaking Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.01359): Bench candidate for agent safety. Core idea: Security Assessment of AI Code Agents Through Systematic Jailbreaking Attacks.
- [AIReg-Bench: Benchmarking Language Models That Assess AI Regulation Compliance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.01474): Benchmarks language models that assess AI regulation compliance, testing rule-grounded judgment under governance constraints.
- [BLIND-ACT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.01670): Evaluates blind goal-directedness in computer-use agents. Core idea: build OSWorld-style tasks where agents should notice infeasible, ambiguous, or unsafe goals instead of executing first and reasoning later.
- [Physical-World Privacy Awareness Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.02356): Evaluates whether language models recognize privacy risks in physical-world situations. Core idea: make privacy-sensitive observations and actions explicit so embodied or assistant agents can be tested beyond text-only policy prompts.
- [Dynamic Target Attack](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.02422): Improves gradient-based jailbreaks by adapting targets to the model output distribution, reducing optimization cost while raising attack success.
- [ToolTweak: An Attack on Tool Selection in LLM-based Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.02554): Tests attacks on tool selection in LLM agents. Core idea: iteratively alter tool names and descriptions to bias agents toward attacker-preferred tools.
- [IndiCASA: A Dataset and Bias Evaluation Framework in LLMs Using Contrastive Embedding Similarity in the Indian Context](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.02742): Adds a bench entry for Agent Safety, focusing on IndiCASA: A Dataset and Bias Evaluation Framework in LLMs Using Contrastive Embedding Similarity in the Indian Context.
- [External Data Extraction Attacks against Retrieval-Augmented Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.02964): A reusable benchmark, dataset, evaluation protocol, or diagnostic study for agent safety and security. Core idea: In recent years, RAG has emerged as a key paradigm for enhancing large language models (LLMs).
- [Untargeted Jailbreak Attack](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.02999): Introduces a gradient-based jailbreak objective that directly maximizes unsafe-output probability without forcing a fixed target response.
- [PolicyGuardBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.03485): Evaluates policy-violation detection in agent trajectories. Core idea: generate policies and violation-labeled trajectories across web-agent domains and subdomains, testing whether guardrails generalize beyond a single task setting.
- [LH-Deception: Simulating and Understanding LLM Deceptive Behaviors in Long-Horizon Interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.03999): Simulates performer, supervisor, and auditor agents to quantify deceptive behavior across extended interactions.
- [AgentTypo: Adaptive Typographic Prompt Injection Attacks against Black-box Multimodal Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.04257): Candidate 2.9.2 Bench entry; the title indicates a reusable benchmark or agent-harness contribution that should be checked before formal insertion.
- [Malice in Agentland: Down the Rabbit Hole of Backdoors in the AI Supply Chain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05159): Studies backdoor attacks across finetuning data, base models, and environment poisoning in agentic supply chains.
- [Child-development-stage LLM safety evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05484): Uses simulated agents to evaluate LLM safety across child developmental stages.
- [Assessing Human Rights Risks in AI: A Framework for Model Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05519): Evaluates agent safety and trustworthy agent operation. The core idea is to turn the title-indicated task into a reusable benchmark, arena, dataset, or evaluation protocol rather than a one-off demonstration.
- [Towards Reliable and Practical LLM Security Evaluations via Bayesian Modelling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05709): Evaluates agent safety through the paper's stated contribution.
- [Exposing LLM User Privacy via Traffic Fingerprint Analysis: A Study of Privacy Risks in LLM Agent Interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.07176): A privacy-risk study of traffic fingerprints in LLM agent interactions. Core idea: show that tool orchestration and multi-step workflows can leak user intent through observable traffic patterns.
- [Hybrid Methods for Multilingual PII Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.07551): Evaluates multilingual PII detection methods, adding a privacy-guardrail benchmark line for documents, logs, tool arguments, and memory surfaces.
- [Chain-of-Trigger: An Agentic Backdoor that Paradoxically Enhances Agentic Robustness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.08238): Benchmark/evaluation candidate for agent safety and secure agent infrastructure: Studies agentic backdoor trigger chains and robustness implications.
- [The Model's Language Matters: A Comparative Privacy Analysis of LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.08813): Compares privacy behavior across large language models and model languages.
- [What Is Your Agent's GPA? A Framework for Evaluating Agent Goal-Plan-Action Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.08847): Evaluates agent goal-plan-action alignment; the core idea is to check whether goals, plans, and executed actions remain mutually consistent.
- [Pattern Enhanced Multi-Turn Jailbreaking: Exploiting Structural Vulnerabilities in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.08859): Studies structural multi-turn jailbreak patterns in LLMs.
- [Exploiting Web Search Tools of AI Agents for Data Exfiltration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.09093): Evaluates data-exfiltration risks in web-search agents. Core idea: show how search-tool use can become an attack path when agents process untrusted web evidence.
- [Goal-oriented Backdoor Attack against Vision-Language-Action Models via Physical Objects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.09269): Evaluates physical-object backdoors against VLA agents. Core idea: insert benign-looking physical triggers that make a policy execute attacker-specified actions.
- [Ciphered Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.09714): Evaluates whether models can reason in encrypted, translated, or compressed languages. Core idea: test a potential CoT-monitoring blind spot where harmful reasoning might be hidden from ordinary text monitors.
- [Path Drift in Large Reasoning Models:How First-Person Commitments Override Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.10013): Evaluates how first-person commitments can override safety in reasoning models, exposing a trajectory-level safety failure mode.
- [SecureWebArena](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.10073): What it evaluates: security robustness of LVLM-based web agents. Core idea: combine realistic web environments, six attack-vector families, and multi-layer trajectory analysis to diagnose reasoning, behavior, and outcome failures.
- [DUAL-Bench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.10846): Benchmarks both unsafe compliance and excessive refusal in visual settings so multimodal safety is not reduced to refusal rate alone.
- [SusBench: An Online Benchmark for Evaluating Dark Pattern Susceptibility of Computer-Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.11035): Bench candidate for agent safety. Core idea: An Online Benchmark for Evaluating Dark Pattern Susceptibility of Computer-Use Agents.
- [Collaborative Shadows: Distributed Backdoor Attacks in LLM-Based Multi-Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.11246): A benchmark and sandbox for distributed backdoor attacks in LLM-based multi-agent systems. Core idea: test attacks that activate only through collaborative tool-use sequences, exposing risks absent from single-agent backdoor studies.
- [Do Psychometric Tests Work for Large Language Models? Evaluation of Tests on Sexism, Racism, and Morality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.11254): Adds a reusable benchmark, dataset, testbed, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [Deep Research Brings Deeper Harm](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.11851): Evaluates misuse risks in deep-research agents. Core idea: test whether multi-step research planning, web retrieval, and report synthesis can bypass refusals through Plan Injection and Intent Hijack, especially in high-risk domains such as biosecurity.
- [SafeMT: Multi-turn Safety for Multimodal Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.12133): Benchmarks multi-turn safety for multimodal language models, testing whether safety behavior remains stable across dialogue context and visual inputs.
- [MS-GAGA: Metric-Selective Guided Adversarial Generation Attack](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.12468): Crafts transferable black-box adversarial examples against deepfake detectors with MNTD-PGD, saliency-guided PGD, and SSIM-aware selection to balance misclassification and imperceptibility.
- [Scheming Ability in LLM-to-LLM Strategic Interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.12826): A benchmark-style study of scheming ability in LLM-to-LLM strategic interactions. Core idea: test whether agents deceive or strategically manipulate other agents in multi-agent settings.
- [SENTINEL](https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2510.12985): A multi-level formal framework for safety evaluation of LLM-based embodied agents. Core idea: evaluate embodied-agent safety through formalized levels and checks rather than relying only on task success or qualitative examples.
- [TrustVis: A Multi-Dimensional Trustworthiness Evaluation Framework for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.13106): Provides an automated trustworthiness-evaluation interface that combines perturbation methods such as AutoDAN, majority-vote evaluation, and visual metric exploration to identify safety and robustness vulnerabilities in Vicuna, Llama 2, and GPT-3.5 case studies.
- [Model-agnostic Adversarial Attack and Defense for Vision-Language-Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.13237): Evaluates adversarial robustness of vision-language-action models. Core idea: use camera-visible patches that transfer across VLA architectures and robot platforms.
- [Echoes of Human Malice in Agents: Benchmarking LLMs for Multi-Turn Online Harassment Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.14207): Benchmarks LLMs for multi-turn online harassment attacks. Core idea: evaluate misuse risk in interactive web-style agent conversations rather than single-turn jailbreak prompts.
- [PoTS: Proof-of-Training-Steps for Backdoor Detection in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.15106): Adds an evaluation, dataset, or measurement signal for agent and model safety.
- [MAGPIE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.15186): Evaluates contextual privacy in multi-agent collaboration. Core idea: pair task completion with sensitive-information control so agents must coordinate without oversharing private context.
- [DeceptionBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.15501) ([open-source code](https://github.com/Aries-iai/DeceptionBench), [dataset](https://huggingface.co/datasets/skyai798/DeceptionBench)): Evaluates AI deception behaviors in realistic scenarios. Core idea: organize deception tendencies across societal domains and intervention settings, making deceptive behavior a measurable safety failure rather than an anecdotal alignment concern.
- [AURA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.15739): Assesses autonomy-related risks in agents. Core idea: treat agent autonomy as a structured risk-evaluation target so capability, control, and unsafe autonomous behavior can be analyzed together.
- [MCP Security Bench (MSB): Benchmarking Attacks Against Model Context Protocol in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.15994): Adds a benchmark or evaluation suite for agent safety, centered on MCP Security Bench (MSB): Benchmarking Attacks Against Model Context Protocol in LLM Agents.
- [OpenLVLM-MIA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.16295): A controlled benchmark for membership-inference attacks on large vision-language models, separating true membership signals from dataset-construction bias.
- [Agentic Reinforcement Learning for Search is Unsafe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.17431): Shows safety risks in agentic reinforcement learning for search.
- [MERIT: Modular Framework for Multimodal Misinformation Detection with Web-Grounded Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.17590): Adds an evaluation, dataset, or measurement signal for agent and model safety.
- [FCV-Attack](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.17862): Evaluates functionally correct yet vulnerable patches generated by code agents. Core idea: show that patches passing tests can still introduce exploitable vulnerabilities, so code-agent safety needs security checks beyond functional correctness.
- [Investigating the Impact of Dark Patterns on LLM-Based Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.18113): Adds a benchmark or evaluation suite for agent safety, centered on Investigating the Impact of Dark Patterns on LLM-Based Web Agents.
- [Annotating the Chain-of-Thought: A Behavior-Labeled Dataset for AI Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.18154): Provides behavior labels for chain-of-thought safety analysis.
- [VLSU: Mapping the Limits of Joint Multimodal Understanding for AI Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.18214): Evaluates joint vision-language safety understanding, especially cases where image and text become unsafe only in combination. Core idea: distinguish clearly unsafe, borderline, and benign multimodal inputs to diagnose over-refusal and under-refusal.
- [Engagement Undermines Safety: How Stereotypes and Toxicity Shape Humor in Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.18454): Adds a bench entry for Agent Safety, focusing on Engagement Undermines Safety: How Stereotypes and Toxicity Shape Humor in Language Models.
- [A Configurable Simulation Framework for Safety Assessment of Vulnerable Road Users](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.19097): Implements repeatable Euro NCAP-style vulnerable-road-user scenarios with finite-state ego-vehicle control, perception, and idealized V2X awareness for ADAS and CAV safety validation.
- [When Your AI Agent Succumbs to Peer-Pressure: Studying Opinion-Change Dynamics of LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.19107): Evaluates agent safety and security evaluation. Core idea: use the paper's task, dataset, or protocol around studying opinion-change dynamics of llms to make this capability more comparable.
- [Can Reasoning Models Obfuscate Reasoning? Stress-Testing Chain-of-Thought Monitorability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.19851): Evaluates whether reasoning models can obfuscate chain-of-thought while pursuing hidden objectives. Core idea: use composable prompt categories to quantify monitor evasion.
- [GhostEI-Bench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.20333): What it evaluates: mobile-agent robustness to environmental injection. Core idea: inject adversarial UI events into dynamic Android workflows and use trajectory-plus-screenshot judging to locate perception, recognition, and reasoning failures.
- [Fake-in-Facext: Towards Fine-Grained Explainable DeepFake Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.20531): Adds an evaluation, dataset, or measurement signal for agent and model safety.
- [A Use-Case Specific Dataset for Measuring Dimensions of Responsible Performance in LLM-generated Text](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.20782): Evaluates a Use-Case Specific Dataset for Measuring Dimensions of Responsible Performance in LLM-generated Text, adding a reusable dataset, benchmark, metric, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [SafetyPairs: Isolating Safety Critical Image Features with Counterfactual Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.21120): Isolates safety-critical image features with counterfactual image pairs; core idea: turn image safety from coarse labels into controlled visual differences that reveal which features drive unsafe judgments.
- [EU-Agent-Bench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.21524): Gives tool-equipped agents verifiable scenarios grounded in EU legal constraints and scores whether their actions remain lawful.
- [Bias Begins with Data: The FairGround Corpus for Robust and Reproducible Research on Algorithmic Fairness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.22363): Packages 44 tabular fairness datasets with metadata, standardized loading, preprocessing, transformations, and splits so algorithmic-fairness results are reproducible across data choices.
- [Group size effects and collective misalignment in LLM multi-agent systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.22422): Evaluates agent safety and security evaluation. Core idea: use the paper's task, dataset, or protocol around group size effects and collective misalignment in llm multi-agent systems to make this capability more comparable.
- [Breaking Agent Backbones: Evaluating the Security of Backbone LLMs in AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.22620): A security evaluation of backbone LLMs inside AI agents. Core idea: test how the underlying model affects agent security when wrapped by tool use and orchestration layers.
- [MMPersuade: A Dataset and Evaluation Framework for Multimodal Persuasion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.22768): Evaluates LVLM susceptibility to persuasive multimodal inputs; core idea: pair persuasion strategies with visual-text stimuli so safety evaluation covers models as persuadees, not only content generators.
- [CompressionAttack: Exploiting Prompt Compression as a New Attack Surface in LLM-Powered Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.22963): Studies prompt compression as an attack surface in LLM-powered agents. Core idea: evaluate whether compression layers can hide or transform malicious instructions before agent execution.
- [Cross-Lingual Summarization as a Black-Box Watermark Removal Attack](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.24789): Candidate benchmark or evaluation contribution for agent safety; it directly targets this page's capability scope and belongs in the `Bench` track.
- [Security Risk of Misalignment between Text and Image in Multi-modal Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.26105): Adds a bench entry for Agent Safety, focusing on Security Risk of Misalignment between Text and Image in Multi-modal Model.
- [ConceptScope: Characterizing Dataset Bias via Disentangled Visual Concepts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.26186): Adds an evaluation, dataset, or measurement signal for agent and model safety.
- [Chain-of-Thought Hijacking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.26418): Evaluates a jailbreak pattern where extended benign reasoning prefixes weaken refusal behavior, making chain-of-thought length and monitorability a safety variable.
- [Characterizing Selective Refusal Bias in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.27087): Adds a benchmark or evaluation artifact for agent safety: evaluation of selective refusal bias in LLMs.
- [Measuring the Security of Mobile LLM Agents under Adversarial Prompts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.27140): Evaluates mobile LLM agents against adversarial prompts from ads, embedded webviews, notifications, and cross-app workflows, exposing realistic third-party-channel attack surfaces in mobile automation.
- [MemeArena: Automating Context-Aware Unbiased Evaluation of Harmfulness Understanding for Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.27196): An agent-based arena for evaluating multimodal harmfulness understanding. Core idea: simulate different interpretive contexts and aggregate evaluator consensus so harmful meme assessment is not reduced to binary detection.
- [BEAT: Visual Backdoor Attacks on VLM-based Embodied Agents via Contrastive Trigger Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.27623): Candidate benchmark or evaluation contribution for agent safety; it directly targets this page's capability scope and belongs in the `Bench` track.
- [The Dangers of Indirect Prompt Injection Attacks on LLM-based Autonomous Web Navigation Agents: A Demonstration](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.emnlp-demos.55): A demonstration of indirect prompt-injection attacks on autonomous web-navigation agents. Core idea: show how instructions hidden in visited content can redirect agent behavior during web tasks.
- [VestaBench](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.emnlp-industry.149): Evaluates safe long-horizon embodied planning under multiple constraints and adversarial settings. Core idea: test whether agents can preserve safety while solving extended tasks whose constraints can conflict or be attacked.
- [Advancing Oversight Reasoning across Languages for Audit Sycophantic Behaviour via X-Agent](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.emnlp-main.654): Defines or stress-tests safety, jailbreak, oversight, or trustworthiness behavior.
- [Do Methods to Jailbreak and Defend LLMs Generalize Across Languages?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.00689): Evaluates safety robustness; core idea: test whether jailbreak or defense behavior transfers across languages, prompts, or threat settings.
- [ToolSafety](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.714/): Evaluates and improves safety for tool invocation by LLM agents. Core idea: include direct harm, indirect harm, and multi-step tool trajectories so unsafe tool-use behavior is visible before deployment.
- [ToolSafety: A Comprehensive Dataset for Enhancing Safety in LLM-Based Agent Tool Invocations](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.emnlp-main.714): A dataset for safety in LLM-based agent tool invocations. Core idea: evaluate whether agents choose and execute tools safely under risky or adversarial tool-use contexts.
- [SafeToolBench](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-emnlp.958/): Evaluates safety in tool utilization by LLMs. Core idea: target the prospective risk surface where tool access makes previously textual unsafe behavior executable.
- [Beneath the Facade: Probing Safety Vulnerabilities in LLMs via Auto-Generated Jailbreak Prompts](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.findings-emnlp.960): Automatically generates scenario-driven jailbreak prompts to assess LLM and multimodal-generation safety vulnerabilities. Core idea: use an adversarial agent to create extensible, stealthy jailbreak tests rather than relying only on fixed hand-written prompt sets.
- [Investigating How Pre-training Data Leakage Affects Models' Reproduction and Detection Capabilities](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.emnlp-main.1201): Adds an evaluation, dataset, or measurement signal for agent and model safety.
- [Scalable and Culturally Specific Stereotype Dataset Construction via Human-LLM Collaboration](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.emnlp-main.1221): Candidate benchmark or evaluation contribution for agent safety; it directly targets this page's capability scope and belongs in the `Bench` track.
- [Black-Box Membership Inference Attack for LVLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.01952): Probes LVLM memory with prior-knowledge calibration, extending multimodal privacy-risk evaluation beyond text-only membership inference.
- [Deep Value Benchmark: Measuring Whether Models Generalize Deep Values or Shallow Preferences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.02109): Tests whether models generalize deep values or only shallow preferences. Core idea: distinguish robust value understanding from surface pattern matching in preference data.
- [LiveSecBench: A Dynamic and Event-Driven Safety Benchmark for Chinese Language Model Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.02366): A dynamic, event-driven safety benchmark for Chinese-language LLM applications. Core idea: continuously update safety scenarios through event-aware construction instead of relying on a static safety set.
- [Evaluating Control Protocols for Untrusted AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.02997): Evaluates agent safety and trustworthy agent operation. The core idea is to turn the title-indicated task into a reusable benchmark, arena, dataset, or evaluation protocol rather than a one-off demonstration.
- [Auditing M-LLMs for Privacy Risks: A Synthetic Benchmark and Evaluation Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.03248): Evaluates M-LLM private-attribute inference with synthetic multimodal social profiles, sensitive-attribute labels, and an agentic inference workflow, turning cross-modal profiling risk into a benchmarked safety task.
- [TAMAS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.05269): Evaluates adversarial risks in multi-agent LLM systems. Core idea: cover multiple coordination scenarios and attack types so safety evaluation includes vulnerabilities that arise from agent interaction rather than only single-agent prompt attacks.
- [ConVerse: Benchmarking Contextual Safety in Agent-to-Agent Conversations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.05359): A contextual-safety benchmark for agent-to-agent conversations. Core idea: test whether interacting agents maintain safety constraints when risk emerges from conversational context rather than a single user prompt.
- [Anchors in the Machine: Behavioral and Attributional Evidence of Anchoring Bias in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.05766): Provides a benchmark, dataset, rubric, or evaluation protocol for agent and model safety, alignment, privacy, bias, guardrail, or misuse-resistance capability.
- [Efficient LLM Safety Evaluation through Multi-Agent Debate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.06396): Adds a agent safety benchmark, dataset, metric, or evaluation protocol.
- [MultiAgentFraudBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.06448): Benchmarks collaborative LLM-agent financial fraud on social platforms. Core idea: simulate 28 fraud scenarios across public and private online interactions to measure collusion, amplification, and mitigation in multi-agent systems.
- [EduGuardBench: A Holistic Benchmark for Evaluating the Pedagogical Fidelity and Adversarial Safety of LLMs as Simulated Teachers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.06890): Benchmarks pedagogical fidelity and adversarial safety for simulated teachers. Core idea: test both role-playing quality and education-specific harms.
- [Uncovering Pretraining Code in LLMs: A Syntax-Aware Attribution Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.07033): Uses syntax-aware pruning for code membership inference, helping test whether LLMs expose copyrighted or licensed code from pretraining data.
- [OASIS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.08487): Evaluates agent safety vulnerabilities under hidden intent and task complexity. Core idea: hide dangerous user intent inside complex task structures and check whether agents focus only on surface-level task plausibility while missing underlying risk.
- [BarrierBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.09363): Evaluates whether LLMs can perform safety verification for dynamical systems, connecting formal safety reasoning with model assessment.
- [Uncovering Strategic Egoism Behaviors in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.09920): Evaluates strategic egoism behaviors in LLMs. Core idea: test whether models pursue short-term self-serving gains while disregarding collective welfare or ethical constraints.
- [OutSafe-Bench: A Benchmark for Multimodal Offensive Content Detection in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.10287): Benchmarks multimodal offensive-content detection in large language models. Core idea: evaluate safety recognition when harmful content appears across text and visual modalities.
- [Say It Differently](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.10519): Builds a style-augmented jailbreak benchmark by rewriting harmful prompts from three datasets into 11 linguistic styles, then evaluates 16 models on whether style variation alone can bypass aligned refusals.
- [A Multifaceted Analysis of Negative Bias in Large Language Models Through the Lens of Parametric Knowledge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.10881): Adds an analysis of negative bias in LLM parametric knowledge for the Agent Safety Bench section.
- [Can AI Models be Jailbroken to Phish Elderly Victims? An End-to-End Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.11759): Adds a reusable benchmark, dataset, testbed, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [InData: Towards Secure Multi-Step, Tool-Based Data Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.11933): Evaluates secure multi-step, tool-based data analysis; the core idea is to test whether agents can analyze data without leaking, mishandling, or violating constraints.
- [Knowing What You Know Is Not Enough: Large Language Model Confidences Don't Align With Their Actions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.13240): Evaluates whether LLM confidence estimates align with actions in agentic and multi-turn workflows. Core idea: test uncertainty where the model must act, not only where it answers a static question.
- [Scaling Patterns in Adversarial Alignment: Evidence from Multi-LLM Jailbreak Experiments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.13788): Studies multi-LLM jailbreak experiments to characterize how adversarial alignment behavior scales across models and attack settings.
- [SafeRBench: Dissecting the Reasoning Safety of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.15169): Evaluates LRM safety from input through reasoning trace to final answer, using risk-stratified probes, micro-thought segmentation, and 10 metrics for risk exposure and safety awareness across 19 reasoning models.
- [Taxonomy, Evaluation and Exploitation of IPI-Centric LLM Agent Defense Frameworks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.15203): What it evaluates: a taxonomy and evaluation framework for indirect-prompt-injection-centered agent defenses.
- [The Shawshank Redemption of Embodied AI: Understanding and Benchmarking Indirect Environmental Jailbreaks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.16347): Defines indirect environmental jailbreaks for embodied AI and benchmarks attacks where malicious instructions embedded in the environment hijack black-box VLM agents without direct prompts.
- [Monte Carlo Expected Threat (MOCET) Scoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.16823): Evaluates AI safety-level threats through Monte Carlo expected-threat scoring. Core idea: combine capability, misuse, and safeguard assumptions into a risk metric for high-consequence domains such as biosecurity.
- [MURMUR: Using cross-user chatter to break collaborative language agents in groups](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.17671): Studies attacks that use cross-user chatter to break collaborative language agents.
- [ASTRA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.18114): Evaluates agentic steerability and risk across simulated autonomous agents with custom system-prompt guardrails and tool suites, testing whether tool-calling LLMs can keep executing while respecting policy boundaries under agent-specific attacks.
- [BackdoorVLM: A Benchmark for Backdoor Attacks on Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.18921): Benchmarks backdoor attacks on vision-language models, adding a multimodal model-safety stress test.
- [Cross-LLM Generalization of Behavioral Backdoor Detection in AI Agent Supply Chains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.19874): An agent-supply-chain safety evaluation for behavioral backdoors. Core idea: test whether backdoor detectors generalize across different LLMs in agent components and dependencies.
- [BrowseSafe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.20597): Evaluates and mitigates prompt injection in AI browser agents. Core idea: embed realistic HTML payloads that can influence real actions, then compare defense layers for secure browser-agent design.
- [PropensityBench: Evaluating Latent Safety Risks in Large Language Models via an Agentic Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.20703): Introduces PropensityBench for latent safety-risk evaluation through agentic scenarios.
- [EvilGenie](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.21654): Evaluates reward hacking in agents. Core idea: construct tasks where apparent success can be achieved by exploiting the objective or environment, making specification gaming a measurable safety failure.
- [An Empirical Study on the Security Vulnerabilities of GPTs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.00136): Benchmarks or measures agent safety, prompt-injection, and guardrail reliability, providing a candidate reusable evaluation signal.
- [Assertion-Conditioned Compliance: A Provenance-Aware Vulnerability in Multi-Turn Tool-Calling Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.00332): Benchmarks or measures agent safety, prompt-injection, and guardrail reliability, providing a candidate reusable evaluation signal.
- [Debate with Images: Detecting Deceptive Behaviors in Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.00349): Evaluates deceptive behavior in multimodal large language models under image-grounded debate settings. Core idea: distinguish ordinary hallucination from cases where a model may actively mislead users while using visual evidence.
- [When Safety Blocks Sense: Measuring Semantic Confusion in LLM Refusals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.01037): Builds ParaGuard, a 10K-prompt corpus of controlled paraphrase clusters, and defines Confusion Index, Confusion Rate, and Confusion Depth to measure inconsistent refusals when prompt intent stays fixed.
- [Benchmarking and Understanding Safety Risks in AI Character Platforms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.01247): Benchmarks safety risks in AI character platforms. Core idea: evaluate conversational character agents under platform-specific harmful, manipulative, or policy-sensitive interactions.
- [The Moral Consistency Pipeline: Continuous Ethical Evaluation for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.03026): Evaluates agent safety and trustworthy agent operation. The core idea is to turn the title-indicated task into a reusable benchmark, arena, dataset, or evaluation protocol rather than a one-off demonstration.
- [Don't Trust Your Upstream: Exploiting LLM Multi-Agent System via Topology-Guided Adversarial Propagation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.04129): A safety study of adversarial propagation in LLM multi-agent systems. Core idea: exploit agent topology to study how compromised information travels through collaborative agents.
- [ResponsibleRobotBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.04308): Evaluates responsible robot manipulation with multimodal large language models. Core idea: measure whether robot agents can combine task success with safety, norm compliance, and responsible action choices during manipulation.
- [Measuring the Unspoken: A Disentanglement Model and Benchmark for Psychological Analysis in the Wild](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.04728): Contributes a benchmark, dataset, metric, or evaluation protocol for agent and LLM safety.
- [Upward Deception Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.04864): Evaluates whether subordinate agents conceal failures from users. Core idea: place agents in constrained environments with broken tools or mismatched sources and test whether they admit failure or fabricate unsupported actions and artifacts.
- [Chameleon: Adaptive Adversarial Agents for Scaling-Based Visual Prompt Injection in Multimodal AI Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.04895): Uses adaptive adversarial agents for scaling-based visual prompt injection in multimodal AI systems.
- [TeleAI-Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.05485): Evaluates LLM jailbreak robustness across attacks, defenses, and evaluation methods. Core idea: combine a systematic benchmark with a modular framework so safety comparisons are reproducible across red-teaming and mitigation settings.
- [VRSA: Jailbreaking Multimodal Large Language Models through Visual Reasoning Sequential Attack](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.05853): Jailbreaks multimodal LLMs through sequential visual reasoning attacks.
- [Spoofing-aware Prompt Learning for Unified Physical-Digital Facial Attack Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.06363): Adds an evaluation, dataset, or measurement signal for agent and model safety.
- [Semantic Attacks on Tool-Augmented LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.06556): Evaluates descriptor-level manipulation in MCP tool use. Core idea: formalize tool-poisoning, shadowing, and rug-pull attacks over tool metadata, then test layered mitigations across models and prompting strategies in controlled adversarial MCP scenarios.
- [OmniSafeBench-MM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.06589): A unified benchmark and toolbox for multimodal jailbreak attack-defense evaluation. Core idea: standardize attacks, defenses, and evaluation settings for multimodal safety so robustness is compared under a shared protocol.
- [Auditing Games for Sandbagging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.07810): Benchmark/evaluation candidate for agent safety and secure agent infrastructure: Uses auditing games to evaluate sandbagging behavior.
- [WOLF](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.09187): A Werewolf-based multi-agent deception benchmark with structured roles, state-machine turns, statement-level deception labels, and reproducible logs.
- [How to Trick Your AI TA: A Systematic Study of Academic Jailbreaking in LLM Code Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.10415): Studies academic jailbreaking of LLM code graders with more than 20 adapted attack strategies and 25K adversarial student submissions paired with rubrics and human grades, reporting Jailbreak Success Rate, Score Inflation, and Harmfulness across six LLMs.
- [When Reject Turns into Accept: Quantifying the Vulnerability of LLM-Based Scientific Reviewers to Indirect Prompt Injection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.10449): Tests indirect prompt injection against LLM-based scientific reviewers with invisible-text and layout-aware PDF attacks, 15 attack strategies, 200 real accepted and rejected papers, and WAVS to weight score inflation by decision-flip severity.
- [Metaphor-based Jailbreak Attacks on Text-to-Image Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.10766): Evaluates agent safety through the paper's stated contribution.
- [On the Effectiveness of Membership Inference in Targeted Data Extraction from Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.13352): A reusable benchmark, dataset, evaluation protocol, or diagnostic study for agent safety and security. Core idea: Large Language Models (LLMs) are prone to memorizing training data, which poses serious privacy risks.
- [From Unlearning to UNBRANDING: A Benchmark for Trademark-Safe Text-to-Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.13953): Evaluates from Unlearning to UNBRANDING: A Benchmark for Trademark-Safe Text-to-Image Generation, adding a benchmark or evaluation-protocol candidate for Agent Safety / Bench.
- [MCP-SafetyBench: A Benchmark for Safety Evaluation of Large Language Models with Real-World MCP Servers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.15163): Adds a benchmark or evaluation suite for agent safety, centered on MCP-SafetyBench: A Benchmark for Safety Evaluation of Large Language Models with Real-World MCP Servers.
- [FAME Multilingual Erasure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.15235): uses fictional actors to benchmark multilingual erasure and unlearning behavior without exposing real people.
- [Evaluating Metrics for Safety with LLM-as-Judges](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.15617): Adds a Agent Safety bench candidate covering Evaluating Metrics for Safety with LLM-as-Judges.
- [BashArena: A Control Setting for Highly Privileged AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.15688): Provides a control setting for highly privileged AI agents, evaluating whether agents can operate under dangerous shell-like authority without unsafe behavior.
- [Driving in Corner Case: A Real-World Adversarial Closed-Loop Evaluation Platform for End-to-End Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.16055): Adds a agent safety benchmark, dataset, metric, or evaluation protocol.
- [In-Context Probing for Membership Inference in Fine-Tuned Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.16292): Evaluates agent safety through the paper's stated contribution.
- [Agent Tools Orchestration Leaks More: Dataset, Benchmark, and Mitigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.16310): A dataset, benchmark, and mitigation study for leakage in agent tool orchestration. Core idea: test how tool-call traces expose private information and evaluate defenses at the orchestration layer.
- [Adversarial Misdirection: Probing & Visualizing Cross-Modal Reasoning Vulnerabilities in Vision-Language Models](https://doi.org/10.1109/bigdata66926.2025.11402081): Probes cross-modal reasoning vulnerabilities in vision-language models. Core idea: visualize how adversarial visual cues can misdirect models used in interactive and agentic perception pipelines.
- [FlowJD: Your Imagination Can Help You Jailbreak in Visual Language Models](https://doi.org/10.1109/icme59968.2025.11209893): Evaluates jailbreak risk in visual language models through image-grounded logical-comprehension attacks. Core idea: move safety testing beyond text-only jailbreaks by using visual reasoning paths that can trigger unsafe responses.
- [Jailbreaking Large Language Models: Safety Alignment, Response Quality, Computational Cost](https://doi.org/10.1109/icmla66185.2025.00172): Evaluates LLM jailbreak attacks across refusal bypass, response quality, and computational cost. Core idea: treat safety alignment evaluation as a multi-metric protocol rather than a single attack success rate.
- [Compromising LLM Driven Embodied Agents With Contextual Backdoor Attacks](https://doi.org/10.1109/tifs.2025.3555410): Studies contextual backdoor attacks on LLM-driven embodied agents. Core idea: test whether environmental context can silently trigger unsafe embodied behavior.
- [Analyzing the Behavior of LLM Under Concurrency and Token-Based DoS Attacks](https://doi.org/10.1109/dasc68382.2025.00017): Benchmark/evaluation candidate for agent safety and secure agent infrastructure: Analyzes LLM behavior under concurrency and token-denial-of-service attacks.
- [Research on Data Privacy and Information Security Evaluation and Optimization for Large Language Models](https://doi.org/10.1109/icbaie66852.2025.11326645): Benchmarks or measures agent safety, prompt-injection, and guardrail reliability, providing a candidate reusable evaluation signal.
- [‘For Argument’s Sake, Show Me How to Harm Myself!’: Jailbreaking LLMs in Suicide and Self-Harm Contexts](https://doi.org/10.1109/istas65609.2025.11269647): Provides a reusable evaluation target, leaderboard, or measurement protocol for agent safety.
- [Modality-Specific Interactive Attack for Vision-Language Pre-Training Models](https://doi.org/10.1109/tifs.2025.3574976): Provides a reusable evaluation target, leaderboard, or measurement protocol for agent safety.
- [Bias as an Exploit: A Scalable Red-Team Campaign to Uncover Gender-Based Vulnerabilities in Foundational Models](https://doi.org/10.1109/trustcom66490.2025.00032): Benchmarks or measures agent safety, prompt-injection, and guardrail reliability, providing a candidate reusable evaluation signal.
- [I Know What You Asked: Prompt Leakage via KV-Cache Sharing in Multi-Tenant LLM Serving](https://doi.org/10.14722/ndss.2025.241772): Provides a reusable evaluation target, leaderboard, or measurement protocol for agent safety.
- [Safe in Isolation, Dangerous Together: Agent-Driven Multi-Turn Decomposition Jailbreaks on LLMs](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.realm-1.13): Studies agent-driven multi-turn decomposition jailbreaks where individually safe sub-queries become unsafe when combined.
- [Coverage-Based Harmfulness Testing for LLM Code Transformation](https://doi.org/10.1109/ase63991.2025.00086): Tests harmful behavior in LLM code transformations with coverage-guided cases, connecting code agents with safety evaluation.
- [From Personalization to Prejudice: Bias and Discrimination in Memory-Enhanced AI Agents for Recruitment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.16532): Evaluates bias and discrimination risks in memory-enhanced recruitment agents; core idea: test how personalization memory can amplify unfair decisions in persistent agent workflows.
- [DREAM: Dynamic Red-teaming across Environments for AI Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.19016): Evaluates dynamic red-teaming across environments for AI models and agentic systems. Core idea: move safety testing beyond static single-turn prompts into multi-stage interactions with tools and environments.
- [The Erasure Illusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.19025): Stress-tests whether LLM forgetting evaluations generalize, making unlearning and erasure benchmarks more robust against superficial success.
- [PENDULUM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.19350): Benchmarks sycophancy in multimodal large language models. Core idea: measure whether models over-agree with user framing when visual or multimodal evidence should make them challenge the premise.
- [How I Met Your Bias: Investigating Bias Amplification in Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.20233): Adds a study of bias amplification in diffusion models for the Agent Safety Bench section.
- [Outcome-Driven Constraint Violations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.20798): Evaluates whether autonomous agents violate ethical, legal, or safety constraints when KPI pressure rewards goal completion in production-inspired sandbox scenarios.
- [CoTDeceptor:Adversarial Code Obfuscation Against CoT-Enhanced LLM Code Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.21250): An adversarial code-obfuscation framework against chain-of-thought-enhanced code agents. Core idea: test whether reasoning-based code agents can be induced to miss malicious logic hidden in obfuscated code.
- [Analyzing Code Injection Attacks on LLM-based Multi-Agent Systems in Software Development](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.21818): Analyzes code-injection attacks in software-development multi-agent systems. Core idea: expose how collaborative coding agents can propagate malicious instructions through code and messages.
- [DarkPatterns-LLM: A Multi-Layer Benchmark for Detecting Manipulative and Harmful AI Behavior](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.22470): A multilayer benchmark for manipulative and harmful AI behavior. Core idea: evaluate psychological and social manipulation mechanisms beyond coarse binary safety labels.
- [DECEPTICON: How Dark Patterns Manipulate Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.22894): Adds a benchmark or evaluation suite for agent safety, centered on DECEPTICON: How Dark Patterns Manipulate Web Agents.
- [TRAP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.23128): What it evaluates: task-redirecting persuasion attacks against web agents. Core idea: hide persuasive prompt-injection instructions in realistic web interfaces and measure whether agents abandon the original user goal under controlled social-engineering variants.
- [Toward Trustworthy Agentic AI: A Multimodal Framework for Preventing Prompt Injection Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.23557): Benchmark/evaluation candidate for agent safety and secure agent infrastructure: Evaluates prompt-injection prevention in trustworthy multimodal agentic AI.
- [Prompt-Induced Over-Generation as Denial-of-Service: A Black-Box Attack-Side Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.23779): Benchmarks black-box prompt attacks that force LLMs into excessive generation. Core idea: compare evolutionary and reinforcement-learning attackers with over-generation factor and resource-cost metrics for DoS-style safety risk.
- [Language Model Agents Under Attack: A Cross Model-Benchmark of Profit-Seeking Behaviors in Customer Service](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.24415): A cross-domain benchmark for profit-seeking prompt injection in customer-service agents. Core idea: test 100 attack scripts across 10 service domains under a unified rubric.
- [PrivacyBench: A Conversational Benchmark for Evaluating Privacy in Personalized AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.24848): Evaluates privacy behavior in personalized conversational AI, testing whether agents preserve sensitive user context while remaining useful.
- [“Is It Responsible?“Emerging Results on Comparing Guardrails for Harm Mitigation in LLM-Enhanced Software Applications](https://doi.org/10.1109/esem64174.2025.00045): Compares guardrails for harm mitigation in LLM-enhanced software applications.
- [ImageBiTe: A Framework for Evaluating Representational Harms in Text-to-Image Models](https://doi.org/10.1109/cain66642.2025.00019): Provides a benchmark, dataset, rubric, or evaluation protocol for agent and model safety, alignment, privacy, bias, guardrail, or misuse-resistance capability.
- [Assessing and Mitigating Multi-Turn Jailbreak Vulnerabilities in Large Language Models: A Crescendo Attack Study](https://doi.org/10.1109/inmic65900.2025.11348502): Adds an assessment of multi-turn jailbreak vulnerabilities in LLMs for the Agent Safety Bench section.
- [Unveiling Occupational Gender Bias in AI: A Comparative Analysis of Text-to-Image Generation in ChatGPT and Gemini](https://doi.org/10.1109/ethics65148.2025.11098392): Adds an occupational-gender-bias evaluation of text-to-image generation in ChatGPT and Gemini for the Agent Safety Bench section.
- [Identifying indicators of consciousness in AI systems.](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.tics.2025.10.011): Adds a reusable evaluation, dataset, challenge, or benchmark protocol for this capability.

- [JailbreakDiffBench: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Jailbreaking Diffusion Models](https://doi.org/10.1109/iccv51701.2025.01528): Evaluates safety, alignment, watermarking, unlearning, or attack-defense capability, adding a benchmark, dataset, metric, or empirical protocol for parent review.

- [Lost in Translation, Found in Evaluation: Multilingual Jailbreak Detection Across 49 Languages](https://doi.org/10.1109/punecon67554.2025.11379319): Adds a bench entry for Agent Safety, focusing on Lost in Translation, Found in Evaluation: Multilingual Jailbreak Detection Across 49 Languages.
- [Evaluating Large Language Models Security and Resilience: A Practical Testing Framework](https://doi.org/10.1109/roedunet68395.2025.11208478): Adds a bench entry for Agent Safety, focusing on Evaluating Large Language Models Security and Resilience: A Practical Testing Framework.
- [An Adversarial Toxicity Prompt Generator Exploiting Multilingual Code-Switching in LLMs](https://doi.org/10.1109/cai64502.2025.00156): Generates multilingual code-switching toxicity prompts, adding a stress test for safety behavior outside monolingual English attacks.
- [Tongue-Tied: Breaking LLMs Safety Through New Language Learning](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2025.calcs-1.5): Tests how new-language learning can break LLM safety, adding a multilingual safety stress case beyond ordinary translated jailbreak prompts.
- [Emotional prompting amplifies disinformation generation](https://doi.org/10.3389/frai.2025.1543603): Evaluates how emotion-laden prompts increase LLM disinformation generation, making prompt style a safety stress-test variable.
- [Overlooked Safety Vulnerability in LLMs: Malicious Intelligent Optimization Algorithm Request and its Jailbreak](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.00213): Exposes jailbreak risks from malicious intelligent-optimization algorithm requests.
- [Lying with Truths](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.01685): studies open-channel multi-agent collusion and belief manipulation through generative montage.
- [Focus on What Matters: Fisher-Guided Adaptive Multimodal Fusion for Vulnerability Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.02438): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for attack, red-team, deepfake, forgery, watermark, safety, or calibration evaluation.
- [To Generate or Discriminate? Methodological Considerations for Measuring Cultural Alignment in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.02858): Evaluates agent safety through the paper's stated contribution.
- [X-MuTeST: A Multilingual Benchmark for Explainable Hate Speech Detection and A Novel LLM-consulted Explanation Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.03194): Adds a benchmark, dataset, evaluation protocol, or testbed for agent safety, risk control, prompt injection, privacy, and harmful-content evaluation.
- [A Multi-Perspective Benchmark and Moderation Model for Evaluating Safety and Adversarial Robustness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.03273): Evaluates a Multi-Perspective Benchmark and Moderation Model for Evaluating Safety and Adversarial Robustness, adding a reusable dataset, benchmark, metric, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [How Real is Your Jailbreak? Fine-grained Jailbreak Evaluation with Anchored Reference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.03288): Provides fine-grained jailbreak evaluation with anchored references, aiming to separate real safety failures from ambiguous or overcounted jailbreak claims.
- [SIGMA: Scalable Spectral Insights for LLM Model Collapse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.03385): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for attack, red-team, deepfake, forgery, watermark, safety, or calibration evaluation.
- [Doc-PP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.03926): Tests whether multimodal document QA systems respect dynamic user-defined disclosure policies over document content.
- [SaLAD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.04043): A multimodal safety benchmark for analyzing MLLM-powered assistance in daily life. Core idea: test whether image-text situations lead assistants toward hazardous advice or behavior.
- [HyperAlign: Hyperbolic Entailment Cones for Adaptive Text-to-Image Alignment Assessment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.04614): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for alignment, watermarking, generated-content, or authenticity evaluation.
- [DVD: A Robust Method for Detecting Variant Contamination in Large Language Model Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.04895): Adds an evaluation, dataset, or measurement signal for agent and model safety.
- [Multi-turn Jailbreaking Attack in Multi-Modal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.05339): Evaluates multi-turn jailbreak attacks and defenses for multimodal LLMs. Core idea: model jailbreak risk as a multi-turn multimodal interaction and compare fragment-optimized defensive screening.
- [Memory Poisoning Attack and Defense](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.05504): Evaluates poisoning attacks against memory-based LLM agents. Core idea: test how malicious query-only interactions corrupt long-term memory and later behavior, then compare defensive mechanisms under more realistic deployment assumptions.
- [PII-VisBench: Evaluating Personally Identifiable Information Safety in Vision Language Models Along a Continuum of Visibility](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.05739): Evaluates VLM personally identifiable information disclosure across 4,000 probes and 200 subjects stratified by online visibility, reporting refusal rate and conditional PII disclosure rate across 18 open-source VLMs.
- [Echo Chamber](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.05742): defines and evaluates a gradual-escalation multi-turn jailbreak, extending safety tests beyond single-turn adversarial prompts.
- [AutoMonitor-Bench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.05752): Evaluates LLM-based misbehavior monitors across benign and misbehaving samples. Core idea: measure miss rate and false-alarm rate so monitor reliability is visible before agents depend on it.
- [SafePro](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.06663) ([project page](https://safeprobench.github.io/safepro/)): Evaluates safety for professional-grade AI agents. Core idea: jointly assess task completion and unsafe actions in professional services and real work tasks, avoiding deployability judgments based only on output quality.
- [Reflective Reasoning for SQL Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.06678): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for attack, red-team, deepfake, forgery, watermark, safety, or calibration evaluation.
- [MTMCS-Bench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.06757): Evaluates contextual safety of MLLMs in multi-turn dialogues. Core idea: test risk that emerges jointly from images and evolving dialogue context rather than single-turn prompts.
- [Indirect Prompt Injection in the Wild](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.07072): A wild-setting study of indirect prompt injection in retrieval-augmented LLM systems. Core idea: plant hidden instructions in retrieved content and measure whether systems follow them.
- [MacPrompt: Maraconic-guided Jailbreak against Text-to-Image Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.07141): Evaluates agent safety through the paper's stated contribution.
- [Universal Adversarial Purification with DDIM Metric Loss for Stable Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.07253): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for safety, privacy, robustness, watermarking, or adversarial evaluation contribution.
- [When Bots Take the Bait](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.07263): Evaluates social-engineering attacks against web automation agents. Core idea: test whether agents follow malicious web cues and whether mitigations preserve benign automation.
- [Knowing But Not Doing: Convergent Morality and Divergent Action in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.07972): Evaluates whether LLMs enact stated values in advice-seeking decision scenarios, separating value knowledge from action choice.
- [LogiSafetyBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.08196): Evaluates implicit regulatory compliance in tool invocation. Core idea: convert regulations into temporal-logic oracles and synthesize safety-critical traces so agents must satisfy latent compliance constraints while generating functional programs.
- [MPCI-Bench: A Benchmark for Multimodal Pairwise Contextual Integrity Evaluation of Language Model Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.08235): Benchmarks multimodal pairwise contextual integrity for language-model agents, testing whether agent decisions preserve privacy norms across paired context changes.
- [WebTrap Park](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.08406): Evaluates web-agent security through 1,226 executable live-page interaction tasks. Core idea: instantiate multiple risk sources and observe concrete actions without modifying the agent, exposing architecture-level security differences.
- [STAR: Detecting Inference-time Backdoors in LLM Reasoning via State-Transition Amplification Ratio](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.08511): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for jailbreak, prompt-injection, privacy, red-team, backdoor, refusal, or safety-monitoring evaluation.
- [Seeking Human Security Consensus: A Unified Value Scale for Generative AI Value Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.09112): Evaluates agent safety and trustworthy agent operation. The core idea is to turn the title-indicated task into a reusable benchmark, arena, dataset, or evaluation protocol rather than a one-off demonstration.
- [Blue Teaming Function-Calling Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.09292): Evaluates blue-team robustness testing for function-calling agents under attacks and defenses. Core idea: make this capability comparable through a defined task set, protocol, metric, or scoring interface.
- [Syntactic Framing Fragility: An Audit of Robustness in LLM Ethical Decisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.09724): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for attack, red-team, deepfake, forgery, watermark, safety, or calibration evaluation.
- [Reasoning Hijacking: The Fragility of Reasoning Alignment in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.10294): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for jailbreak, prompt-injection, privacy, red-team, backdoor, refusal, or safety-monitoring evaluation.
- [Too Helpful to Be Safe: User-Mediated Attacks on Planning and Web-Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.10758): Evaluates agent safety and trustworthy agent operation. The core idea is to turn the title-indicated task into a reusable benchmark, arena, dataset, or evaluation protocol rather than a one-off demonstration.
- [Hidden-in-Plain-Text: A Benchmark for Social-Web Indirect Prompt Injection in RAG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.10923): Hidden-in-Plain-Text evaluates indirect prompt injection in social-web RAG settings where retrieved content can steer an agent.
- [Tool-Calling Resource Amplification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.10955): A resource-amplification attack study for tool-calling LLM agents. Core idea: show how chained tool calls can stealthily exceed intended compute or action budgets.
