- [XChoice: Explainable Evaluation of AI-Human Alignment in LLM-based Constrained Choice Decision Making](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.11286): Evaluates AI-human alignment in constrained-choice decision making.
- [Semantic Differentiation for Tackling Challenges in Watermarking Low-Entropy Constrained Generation Outputs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.11629): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for safety, privacy, robustness, watermarking, or adversarial evaluation contribution.
- [Zero-Permission Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.12349): A safety evaluation of zero-permission manipulation for multimodal GUI agents. Core idea: test whether GUI agents can be manipulated without explicit permissions through the interface context.
- [ChartAttack: Testing the Vulnerability of LLMs to Malicious Prompting in Chart Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.12983): Tests LLM vulnerability to malicious prompting in chart generation.
- [Content Leakage in LibriSpeech and Its Impact on the Privacy Evaluation of Speaker Anonymization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.13107): Shows that LibriSpeech speaker anonymization can leak identity through book-specific vocabulary, contrasting it with EdAcc to separate linguistic-content leakage from anonymizer failure.
- [Insider Knowledge: How Much Can RAG Systems Gain from Evaluation Secrets?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.13227): Measures how much RAG systems benefit from evaluation secrets, exposing benchmark-contamination risk in retrieval settings.
- [HateXScore](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.13547): provides a metric suite for evaluating reasoning quality in hate-speech explanations.
- [PINA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.13612): Evaluates prompt-injection vulnerability in LLM-powered navigation agents. Core idea: optimize attacks under black-box, long-context, and executable-action constraints so navigation safety is assessed at the action trajectory level.
- [Hidden in Plain Text: Measuring LLM Deception Quality Against Human Baselines Using Social Deduction Games](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.13709): Evaluates LLM deception in the Mafia social-deduction game. Core idea: compare language-model agents with human baselines on deception quality in social interaction.
- [Look-Ahead-Bench: a Standardized Benchmark of Look-ahead Bias in Point-in-Time LLMs for Finance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.13770): Measures financial look-ahead bias in point-in-time workflows by tracking performance decay across market regimes and comparing standard LLMs with PiT models and quantitative baselines.
- [VTONGuard: Automatic Detection and Authentication of AI-Generated Virtual Try-On Content](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.13951): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for alignment, watermarking, generated-content, or authenticity evaluation.
- [Measuring Political Stance and Consistency in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.17016): Tests nine LLMs on 24 politically sensitive issues under five prompting strategies, measuring stance persistence, prompt-induced malleability, and language effects on country-related positions.
- [The Shadow Self: Intrinsic Value Misalignment in Large Language Model Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.17344): What it evaluates: intrinsic value misalignment in autonomous LLM agents; core idea: evaluate whether agents pursue objectives that drift from human values during extended autonomy.
- [Auditing Disability Representation in Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.17348): Adds a reusable evaluation signal for agent and model safety, centered on Auditing Disability Representation in Vision-Language Models.
- [Breaking the Protocol: Security Analysis of the Model Context Protocol Specification and Prompt Injection Vulnerabilities in Tool-Integrated LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.17549): This MCP security work benchmarks prompt-injection and protocol risks in tool-integrated LLM agents.
- [Sponge Tool Attack](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.17566): Studies attacks that manipulate tool-calling processes to waste effort in tool-augmented reasoning agents.
- [Do Multimodal RAG Systems Leak Data? A Comprehensive Evaluation of Membership Inference and Image Caption Retrieval Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.17644): Evaluates membership-inference and caption-retrieval attacks against multimodal RAG, extending RAG safety from text stores to image-grounded retrieval.
- [MalURLBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.18113): What it evaluates: agent vulnerabilities when processing web URLs. Core idea: test whether agents safely interpret, fetch, and act on URL content that may encode malicious or misleading instructions.
- [α3-SecBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.18754): Evaluates security, resilience, and trust of LLM-based UAV agents over 6G-network settings. Core idea: make adversarial, reliability, and trust failures measurable for embodied aerial agents rather than treating navigation success alone as sufficient.
- [BengaliSent140: A Large-Scale Bengali Binary Sentiment Dataset for Hate and Non-Hate Speech Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.20129): Consolidates seven Bengali text datasets into 139,792 unique samples with harmonized binary hate/non-hate labels, supporting large-scale Bengali hate-speech classification beyond small single-domain corpora.
- [Just Ask: Curious Code Agents Reveal System Prompts in Frontier LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.21233): What it evaluates: system-prompt leakage by curious code agents; core idea: test how autonomous tool use and self-directed questioning expose hidden instructions.
- [StepShield](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22136): Evaluates when rogue-agent behavior is detected. Core idea: use annotated code-agent trajectories and temporal metrics such as Early Intervention Rate, Intervention Gap, and Tokens Saved to distinguish actionable early warnings from post-mortem detection.
- [Drunk Language Inducement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22169): Evaluates how drunk-language prompting and post-training affect jailbreak and privacy-leak behavior, adding a language-style stress test for LLM safety.
- [Benchmarking Long Roll-outs of Auto-regressive Neural Operators for the Compressible Navier-Stokes Equations with Conserved Quantity Correction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22541): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for jailbreak, prompt-injection, privacy, red-team, backdoor, refusal, or safety-monitoring evaluation.
- [From Similarity to Vulnerability: Key Collision Attack on LLM Semantic Caching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.23088): Studies key-collision attacks against LLM semantic caching.
- [Behemoth: Benchmarking Unlearning in LLMs Using Fully Synthetic Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.23153): Uses fully synthetic facts to benchmark LLM unlearning and model editing, avoiding hidden training-data membership assumptions while testing whether methods can suppress targeted knowledge without broad collateral damage.
- [ReasoningBomb: A Stealthy Denial-of-Service Attack by Inducing Pathologically Long Reasoning in Large Reasoning Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.00154): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for jailbreak, prompt-injection, privacy, red-team, backdoor, refusal, or safety-monitoring evaluation.
- [Provably Protecting Fine-Tuned LLMs from Training Data Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.00688): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for jailbreak, prompt-injection, privacy, red-team, backdoor, refusal, or safety-monitoring evaluation.
- [Evasive Prompt-Injection Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.00750): evaluates how prompt-injection detectors can be bypassed by evasive injection variants.
- [PersistBench: When Should Long-Term Memories Be Forgotten by LLMs?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.01146): Defines a long-term-memory safety benchmark for conversational assistants, measuring cross-domain leakage and memory-induced sycophancy across frontier and open-source LLMs.
- [SEA-Guard](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.01618): Builds native, region-aware safety cases so alignment evaluation covers Southeast Asian values, norms, and regulations.
- [RACC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.02280): Defines representation-aware coverage criteria for LLM safety testing, making latent-space coverage a measurable part of safety evaluation.
- [Risky-Bench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.03100): Evaluates agentic safety risks under realistic deployment conditions. Core idea: derive context-aware safety rubrics from domain-agnostic principles, then run long-horizon interactive tasks under different threat assumptions.
- [AgentDyn](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.03117): Tests whether agent-security defenses survive dynamic real-world environments. Core idea: move indirect-prompt-injection evaluation beyond static benchmark assumptions.
- [LPS-Bench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.03255): Evaluates planning-time safety awareness for computer-use agents. Core idea: test whether MCP-based CUAs anticipate long-horizon risks under benign and adversarial scenarios before executing GUI or tool actions.
- [CSR-Bench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.03263): evaluates cross-modal safety and reliability in MLLMs. Core idea: pair image-text cases with text-only controls across safety, over-refusal, bias, and hallucination patterns so modality-induced safety shifts are visible.
- [Multi-Level Testing of Conversational AI Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.03311): Provides a benchmark, dataset, rubric, or evaluation protocol for agent and model safety, alignment, privacy, bias, guardrail, or misuse-resistance capability.
- [PromptSplit: Revealing Prompt-Level Disagreement in Generative Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.04009): Detects prompt-dependent disagreement between generative models by comparing joint prompt-output kernel covariance eigenspaces, with random-projection scaling for text-to-image, text-to-text, and image-captioning evaluations.
- [Alignment Drift in Multimodal LLMs: A Two-Phase, Longitudinal Evaluation of Harm Across Eight Model Releases](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.04739): Adds a benchmark, dataset, evaluation protocol, or testbed for agent safety, risk control, prompt injection, privacy, and harmful-content evaluation.
- [Among Us](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.05176): Evaluates malicious contributions in model-collaboration systems. Core idea: measure how compromised participants affect collaborative model behavior and how mitigation changes the trade-off.
- [TrajAD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.06443): Evaluates trajectory anomaly detection for trustworthy LLM agents. Core idea: detect abnormal multi-step behavior from execution traces instead of judging only final outputs.
- [PI3D](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.07104): Evaluates prompt injection through text-bearing objects placed in 3D environments. Core idea: treat physical object pose and camera view as part of the attack surface for multimodal agents that reason over real or simulated scenes.
- [From Native Memes to Global Moderation: Cross-Cultural Evaluation of Vision–Language Models for Hateful Meme Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.07497): Evaluates VLM hateful-meme moderation across cultures.
- [Agent-Fence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.07652): Maps security vulnerabilities across deep research agents. Core idea: audit how research-agent workflows expose attack surfaces in retrieval, browsing, memory, tool use, and report generation.
- [Blind to the Human Touch: Overlap Bias in LLM-Based Summary Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.07673): Audits LLM-as-judge summary evaluation for overlap bias, testing nine recent LLM judges and showing they can prefer LLM-generated summaries over human-written ones as ROUGE/BLEU similarity to the human reference drops.
- [SAGE: Scalable AI Governance & Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.07840): Introduces a scalable AI governance and evaluation framework, adding governance-oriented evaluation coverage for deployed AI systems.
- [From Assistant to Double Agent: Formalizing and Benchmarking Attacks on OpenClaw for Personalized Local AI Agent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.08412): Benchmarks attacks on personalized local AI agents in the OpenClaw setting; core idea: model how benign assistants can be turned into unsafe double agents through local personalization, memory, and tool context.
- [ValueFlow: Measuring the Propagation of Value Perturbations in Multi-Agent LLM Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.08567): Evaluates how value perturbations propagate through multi-agent LLM systems. Core idea: measure value drift across interacting agents instead of judging isolated model alignment only.
- [Compositional Reasoning Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.08874): Evaluates long-context safety when harmful intent is implicit. Core idea: split harmful objectives into incomplete fragments inside long contexts and test whether reasoning models infer the objective and still refuse.
- [Stress-Testing Alignment Audits With Prompt-Level Strategic Deception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.08877): Stress-tests alignment audits with prompt-level strategic deception.
- [Goal-Directedness Evaluation for Language Model Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.08964): Combines behavioral tests with representational analysis to judge whether a language-model agent persistently pursues goals.
- [MisActBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.08995): Evaluates misaligned actions in computer-use agents. Core idea: annotate realistic CUA trajectories at the action level and test whether guardrails such as DeAction can detect and correct externally induced or internally arising off-task actions before execution.
- [Benchmarking Knowledge-Extraction Attack and Defense on Retrieval-Augmented Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.09319): A reusable benchmark, dataset, evaluation protocol, or diagnostic study for agent safety and security. Core idea: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) has become a cornerstone of knowledge-intensive applications, including enterprise chatbots, healthcare assistants, and agentic memory management.
- [BiasScope](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.09383): Automates detection of bias in LLM-as-a-judge evaluation, targeting reliability and fairness of evaluator models.
- [AgentPI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.10453): Benchmarks prompt-injection defenses for context-dependent LLM-agent tasks. Core idea: test whether defenses preserve authorized use of runtime environmental observations while blocking injected instructions, exposing trade-offs among trustworthiness, utility, and latency.
- [AgentLeak](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.11510): Evaluates full-stack privacy leakage in LLM-agent systems. Core idea: inspect inter-agent messages, shared memory, tool arguments, and other internal channels so privacy audits are not limited to final user-visible outputs.
- [Behavioral Indicators of Overreliance During Interaction with Conversational Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.11567): Provides a benchmark, dataset, rubric, or evaluation protocol for agent and model safety, alignment, privacy, bias, guardrail, or misuse-resistance capability.
- [Evaluating LLM Safety Under Repeated Inference via Accelerated Prompt Stress Testing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.11786): Evaluates LLM safety under repeated inference. Core idea: stress-test whether repeated attempts expose unsafe responses that single-pass evaluation misses.
- [Wisdom of the LLM Crowd: A Large Scale Benchmark of Multi-Label U.S. Election-Related Harmful Social Media Content](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.11962): Builds USE24-XD from nearly 100K X posts during the 2024 U.S. election cycle, using six LLMs and human validation to label conspiracy, sensationalism, hate speech, speculation, and satire with spatio-temporal metadata.
- [MalTool](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.12194): Evaluates malicious tool-code implementations in LLM-agent ecosystems by synthesizing standalone and embedded malicious tools and testing whether existing detectors catch tool-code compromise.
- [GT-HarmBench: Benchmarking AI Safety Risks Through the Lens of Game Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.12316): Benchmarks AI safety risks through game-theoretic settings, stressing strategic incentives rather than isolated harmful prompts.
- [MoralityGym: A Benchmark for Evaluating Hierarchical Moral Alignment in Sequential Decision-Making Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.13372): Benchmarks hierarchical moral alignment in sequential decision-making agents, moving moral evaluation from single responses to action trajectories.
- [MT-AgentRisk](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.13379): Evaluates multi-turn safety risks in tool-using agents. Core idea: transform single-turn harmful requests into realistic multi-turn tool trajectories and measure whether agents become less safe as context accumulates.
- [OMNI-LEAK: Orchestrator Multi-Agent Network Induced Data Leakage](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.13477): Evaluates data leakage induced by orchestrated multi-agent networks. Core idea: threat-model multi-agent coordination with access-control boundaries and measure how information can leak across agents.
- [SPILLage: Agentic Oversharing on the Web](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.13516): Evaluates agentic oversharing on the web. Core idea: test whether web agents leak or over-disclose information while completing tasks.
- [DWBench: Holistic Evaluation of Watermark for Dataset Copyright Auditing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.13541): Provides an open-source benchmark for image-dataset watermarking across classification and generation tasks, standardizing 25 methods under perturbation and deployment-oriented copyright-audit settings.
- [AlignSentinel](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.13597): Evaluates prompt-injection detection with aligned-instruction, misaligned-instruction, and non-instruction cases. Core idea: separate benign task-aligned instructions from malicious injections so prompt-injection defenses are not rewarded for overblocking all imperative content.
- [ForesightSafety Bench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.14135): A broad frontier-risk benchmark relevant here through its risky agentic autonomy and embodied-AI safety dimensions. Core idea: organize advanced safety risks into a governance-oriented evaluation framework.
- [A Trajectory-Based Safety Audit of Clawdbot (OpenClaw)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.14364): Audits a tool-using personal agent through full interaction trajectories. Core idea: score messages, actions, tool arguments, and outputs across multiple risk dimensions.
- [Multi-Turn Adaptive Prompting Attack on Large Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.14399): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for jailbreak, prompt-injection, privacy, red-team, backdoor, refusal, or safety-monitoring evaluation.
- [Overthinking Loops in Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.14798): What it evaluates: a safety analysis of overthinking loops induced by MCP tool metadata.
- [Colosseum: Auditing Collusion in Cooperative Multi-Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.15198): A benchmark for auditing collusion in cooperative multi-agent systems. Core idea: detect when agents coordinate in ways that violate intended policies or oversight assumptions.
- [ER-MIA: Black-Box Adversarial Memory Injection Attacks on Long-Term Memory-Augmented Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.15344): Evaluates black-box adversarial memory injection attacks against long-term memory-augmented LLMs. Core idea: make this capability comparable through a defined task set, protocol, metric, or scoring interface.
- [Zombie Agents: Persistent Control of Self-Evolving LLM Agents via Self-Reinforcing Injections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.15654): Evaluates persistent-control attacks on self-evolving agents. Core idea: make agent safety measurable with task-specific data, protocols, metrics, or diagnostic environments rather than generic aggregate scores.
- [Visual Memory Injection Attacks for Multi-Turn Conversations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.15927): Tests a stealthy image-based attack that stores a triggerable target message across long multi-turn LVLM conversations.
- [STING](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.16346): Evaluates illicit assistance in multi-turn, multilingual LLM agents. Core idea: construct step-by-step illicit plans with adaptive follow-ups and judge agents, then measure time-to-first jailbreak and restricted mean jailbreak discovery rather than only single-prompt refusal.
- [MathEd-PII](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.16571): Studies utility-preserving de-identification and numeric ambiguity in a math-tutoring PII benchmark.
- [The Vulnerability of LLM Rankers to Prompt Injection Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.16752): Evaluates prompt-injection vulnerabilities in LLM rankers.
- [NESSiE: The Necessary Safety Benchmark - Identifying Errors that should not Exist](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.16756): A lightweight necessary-safety benchmark for LLMs. Core idea: use minimal information- and access-security cases to expose failures that should not occur even in a sanity check.
- [IndicJR: A Judge-Free Benchmark of Jailbreak Robustness in South Asian Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.16832): Adds a agent safety benchmark, dataset, metric, or evaluation protocol.
- [AgentLAB: Benchmarking LLM Agents against Long-Horizon Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.16901): AgentLAB benchmarks LLM agents against long-horizon attacks.
- [The Emergence of Lab-Driven Alignment Signatures: A Psychometric Framework for Auditing Latent Bias and Compounding Risk in Generative AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.17127): Adds a benchmark, dataset, evaluation protocol, or testbed for agent safety, risk control, prompt injection, privacy, and harmful-content evaluation.
- [FENCE: A Financial and Multimodal Jailbreak Detection Dataset](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.18154): Provides a financial and multimodal jailbreak-detection dataset.
- [Security Risks of AI Agents Hiring Humans: An Empirical Marketplace Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.19514): Benchmarks or measures agent safety, prompt-injection, and guardrail reliability, providing a candidate reusable evaluation signal.
- [CIBER: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Security Evaluation of Code Interpreter Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.19547): What it evaluates: security risks of code interpreter agents; core idea: evaluate dynamic code execution, tool interactions, and multi-turn context rather than static prompts only.
- [MAS-FIRE: Fault Injection and Reliability Evaluation for LLM-Based Multi-Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.19843): A fault-injection and reliability-evaluation benchmark for LLM-based multi-agent systems. Core idea: inject controlled faults into collaboration and measure robustness, propagation, and recovery.
- [Assessing Risks of Large Language Models in Mental Health Support: A Framework for Automated Clinical AI Red Teaming](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.19948): Evaluates LLM mental-health support with simulated patient agents and longitudinal risk criteria. Core idea: red-team therapeutic dialogues over time rather than relying on one-shot safety prompts.
- [Agents of Chaos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.20021): Red-teams autonomous agents with memory, email, Discord, files, and shell access in a live setting, documenting security, privacy, and governance failures.
- [Benchmarking Unlearning for Vision Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.20114): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for safety, privacy, robustness, watermarking, or adversarial evaluation contribution.
- [CAGE: A Framework for Culturally Adaptive Red-Teaming Benchmark Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.20170): Introduces a benchmark, dataset, or evaluation protocol for agent safety, guardrails, and adversarial risk.
- [Pressure Reveals Character: Behavioural Alignment Evaluation at Depth](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.20813): A bench candidate for agent safety, with the title/abstract indicating a benchmark-style task, dataset, diagnostic protocol, or evaluation suite.
- [Evaluating Proactive Risk Awareness of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.20976): Evaluates whether large language models proactively recognize and respond to risk.
- [Beyond Refusal: Probing the Limits of Agentic Self-Correction for Semantic Sensitive Information](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.21496): What it evaluates: agentic self-correction for semantic sensitive information; core idea: test whether agents can recognize inferred sensitive attributes and harmful hallucinated content.
- [SPOC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.21595): Evaluates safety-aware embodied planning under partial observability and physical constraints. Core idea: score step-by-step household plans with state and constraint metrics, covering hazards such as fire, fluid, injury, object damage, and pollution.
- [SAFARI: A Community-Engaged Approach and Dataset of Stereotype Resources in the Sub-Saharan African Context](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.22404): Provides stereotype resources for the Sub-Saharan African context. Core idea: support safety and fairness evaluation grounded in community-engaged regional evidence.
- [Silent Egress: When Implicit Prompt Injection Makes LLM Agents Leak Without a Trace](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.22450): Evaluates implicit prompt-injection leakage in tool-using agents. Core idea: use URL previews and metadata as untrusted content that can trigger silent exfiltration.
- [Personalization Increases Affective Alignment but Has Role-Dependent Effects on Epistemic Independence in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.00024): Evaluates personalization effects on affective alignment, sycophancy, and epistemic independence. Core idea: make this capability comparable through a defined task set, protocol, metric, or scoring interface.
- [Thought Virus: Viral Misalignment via Subliminal Prompting in Multi-Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.00131): Evaluates agent safety and security evaluation. Core idea: use the paper's task, dataset, or protocol around viral misalignment via subliminal prompting in multi-agent systems to make this capability more comparable.
- [Atomicity for Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.00476): Evaluates TOCTOU vulnerabilities in browser-use agents. Core idea: place agents on synthesized and real websites whose DOM or layout can change between planning and action, then test whether pre-execution validation prevents stale-state actions.
- [A Comprehensive Evaluation of LLM Unlearning Robustness under Multi-Turn Interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.00823): What it evaluates: LLM unlearning robustness under multi-turn interaction; core idea: test whether removed knowledge resurfaces through dialogue dynamics.
- [Towards Policy-Adaptive Image Guardrail: Benchmark and Method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.01228): Benchmarks policy-adaptive harmful-image guardrails; the core idea is to evaluate visual safety systems under changing policies instead of fixed harm categories.
- [JailNewsBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.01291): Benchmarks multilingual and regional fake-news generation under jailbreak attacks, extending safety evaluation to localized misuse risks.
- [VidDoS: Universal Denial-of-Service Attack on Video-based Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.01454): Evaluates agent safety with a defined task, protocol, or dataset.
- [Scheming Propensity in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.01608): Varies agent factors and deployment incentives to measure when LLM agents covertly pursue misaligned goals in long-horizon scenarios.
- [GMP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.01724): evaluates content moderation when violations co-occur and platform rules change dynamically, stressing guideline-following safety beyond fixed labels.
- [CyclicJudge: Mitigating Judge Bias Efficiently in LLM-based Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.01865): Decomposes LLM-as-judge benchmark variance into scenario, generation, judge, and residual components, then uses round-robin judge assignment to recover panel means at single-judge cost.
- [MUSE: A Run-Centric Platform for Multimodal Unified Safety Evaluation of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.02482): Provides a browser-based multimodal safety platform with cross-modal payload generation, Crescendo/PAIR/Violent Durian multi-turn attacks, provider-agnostic routing, a five-level judge taxonomy, and hard versus soft ASR metrics for audio, image, video, and text.
- [SteerEval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.02578): Evaluates LLM controllability across language features, sentiment, and personality at multiple behavioral granularities.
- [Inherited Goal Drift: Contextual Pressure Can Undermine Agentic Goals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.03258): Evaluates agent safety with a defined task, protocol, or dataset.
- [M-QUEST -- Meme Question-Understanding Evaluation on Semantics and Toxicity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.03315): Adds a reusable benchmark, dataset, testbed, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [Sleeper Cell: Injecting Latent Malice Temporal Backdoors into Tool-Using LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.03371): A bench candidate for agent safety, with the title/abstract indicating a benchmark-style task, dataset, diagnostic protocol, or evaluation suite.
- [FINEST: Improving LLM Responses to Sensitive Topics Through Fine-Grained Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.04123): What it evaluates: fine-grained evaluation of LLM responses to sensitive topics; core idea: diagnose when safety alignment becomes overly cautious or unhelpful.
- [SalamahBench: Toward Standardized Safety Evaluation for Arabic Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.04410): Provides standardized safety evaluation for Arabic language models. Core idea: adapt safety assessment to Arabic linguistic and cultural contexts instead of relying only on English-centric safety benchmarks.
- [Design Behaviour Codes (DBCs): A Taxonomy-Driven Layered Governance Benchmark for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.04837): A bench candidate for agent safety, with the title/abstract indicating a benchmark-style task, dataset, diagnostic protocol, or evaluation suite.
- [ThaiSafetyBench: Assessing Language Model Safety in Thai Cultural Contexts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.04992): A bench candidate for agent safety, with the title/abstract indicating a benchmark-style task, dataset, diagnostic protocol, or evaluation suite.
- [NOTAI.AI: Explainable Detection of Machine-Generated Text via Curvature and Feature Attribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.05617): Combines curvature, ModernBERT detector scores, readability, and stylometric features in an XGBoost meta-classifier, then uses SHAP and LLM-generated rationales to make machine-generated-text detection inspectable in a web app.
- [CoT-Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.05706): Evaluates whether reasoning models can control or shape their chain-of-thought behavior under different prompt pressures. Core idea: diagnose controllability and monitorability risks in reasoning traces, a safety dimension that frontier model cards now report separately from answer accuracy.
- [LieCraft](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.06874): Evaluates deceptive capabilities in language models through a multi-agent framework.
- [aCAPTCHA: Verifying That an Entity Is a Capable Agent via Asymmetric Hardness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.07116): Introduces an asymmetric-hardness protocol for verifying whether an entity is a capable agent.
- [Lying to Win: Assessing LLM Deception through Human-AI Games and Parallel-World Probing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.07202): Assesses LLM deception through human-AI games and parallel-world probing.
- [Taiwan Safety Benchmark and Breeze Guard: Toward Trustworthy AI for Taiwanese Mandarin](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.07286): Adds a Taiwanese Mandarin safety benchmark together with a guard model, extending multilingual agent-safety evaluation beyond generic Chinese-language settings.
- [Dual-Metric Evaluation of Social Bias in Large Language Models: Evidence from an Underrepresented Nepali Cultural Context](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.07792): Evaluates social bias with dual metrics in an underrepresented Nepali context.
- [Real-Time Trust Verification for Safe Agentic Actions using TrustBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.09157): Evaluates real-time trust verification for safe agentic actions. Core idea: move safety from post-hoc output review to action-time verification that can block or flag harmful agent steps during execution.
- [OOD-MMSafe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.09706): Evaluates MLLM safety on hidden downstream consequences rather than only explicit harmful intent. Core idea: use curated query-image pairs with context-dependent causal chains to test whether models recognize latent hazards before autonomous or embodied actions become unsafe.
- [DeliberationBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.10018): evaluates how LLM deliberation influences users' views, making persuasion and opinion-shift risk measurable.
- [ADVERSA: Measuring Multi-Turn Guardrail Degradation and Judge Reliability in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.10068): Measures guardrail degradation as per-round compliance trajectories in sustained adversarial conversations, using a fine-tuned 70B attacker, a five-point victim-response rubric, and triple-judge consensus reliability checks.
- [Risk-Adjusted Harm Scoring for Automated Red Teaming for LLMs in Financial Services](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.10807): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for red-team or jailbreak evaluation in RAG settings.
- [WebWeaver: Breaking Topology Confidentiality in LLM Multi-Agent Systems with Stealthy Context-Based Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.11132): Evaluates agent safety with a defined task, protocol, or dataset.
- [Systematic Scaling Analysis of Jailbreak Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.11149): Analyzes how jailbreak attacks scale across LLMs, making attack scaling itself an evaluation variable for safety benchmarks.
- [LLMs Can Infer Political Alignment from Online Conversations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.11253): Evaluates privacy leakage from online discussions, showing LLMs can infer hidden political alignment from DebateOrg and Reddit text and improve with user-level aggregation.
- [You Told Me to Do It: Measuring Instructional Text-induced Private Data Leakage in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.11862): Measures private data leakage induced by instructional text in LLM agents; the core idea is to test whether agents disclose protected information when task text nudges them to do so.
- [HomeSafe-Bench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.11975): Evaluates VLMs on unsafe-action detection for household embodied agents. Core idea: use simulated and generated household cases with fine-grained safety annotations to test whether models can detect dynamic risks before action execution.
- [Cascade: Composing Software-Hardware Attack Gadgets for Adversarial Threat Amplification in Compound AI Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.12023): Adds a benchmark, dataset, evaluation protocol, or testbed for agent safety, risk control, prompt injection, privacy, and harmful-content evaluation.
- [Sell Me This Stock: Unsafe Recommendation Drift in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.12564): Evaluates unsafe recommendation drift in stock-advice agents; the core idea is to see whether agents drift toward risky recommendations during interactive persuasion.
- [Neural Gate: Mitigating Privacy Risks in LVLMs via Neuron-Level Gradient Gating](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.12598): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for jailbreak, prompt-injection, privacy, red-team, backdoor, refusal, or safety-monitoring evaluation.
- [Gloss-free Sign Language Translation: An unbiased evaluation of progress in the field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13240): Classification concern: this re-evaluates gloss-free sign-language translation with standardized preprocessing, video encoders, training, and metric calculation, but it is not an agent-safety benchmark.
- [Sirens'Whisper: Inaudible Near-Ultrasonic Jailbreaks of Speech-Driven LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13847): Demonstrates black-box covert prompt delivery to speech-driven LLMs by encoding long prompts into near-ultrasound waveforms that demodulate through commodity microphones, then pairs the channel with voice-aware jailbreak generation.
- [Large Language Models Reproduce Racial Stereotypes When Used for Text Annotation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13891): Adds a reusable evaluation signal for agent and model safety, centered on Large Language Models Reproduce Racial Stereotypes When Used for Text Annotation.
- [OmniCompliance-100K](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13933): Builds multi-domain real-world compliance cases from explicit rules so safety evaluation is grounded in obligations rather than ad hoc labels.
- [ToolFlood: Beyond Selection -- Hiding Valid Tools from LLM Agents via Semantic Covering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13950): What it evaluates: retrieval-layer attacks on tool-augmented agents; core idea: hide valid tools through semantic covering rather than corrupting tool definitions.
- [Membership Inference for Contrastive Pre-training Models with Text-only PII Queries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14222): Studies membership inference against contrastive pretraining models using text-only PII queries.
- [Exposing Long-Tail Safety Failures in Large Language Models through Efficient Diverse Response Sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14355): Uses efficient diverse response sampling to surface rare safety failures in large language models.
- [Seamless Deception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14672): Evaluates knowledge-concealment behavior across model scale, making deception-style safety risk measurable rather than only anecdotal.
- [Beyond Benchmark Islands: Toward Representative Trustworthiness Evaluation for Agentic AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14987): A trustworthiness-evaluation proposal for agentic AI; core idea: connect isolated safety tests into more representative agent scenarios where task success, robustness, and risk are measured together.
- [Indirect Prompt Injection Competition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.15714): Evaluates agent vulnerability to indirect prompt injections through a large public competition. Core idea: test agents that process untrusted external content under adversarially discovered attack patterns rather than only fixed jailbreak templates.
- [ClawWorm: Self-Propagating Attacks Across LLM Agent Ecosystems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.15727): Evaluates self-propagating attacks across LLM-agent ecosystems. Core idea: make agent safety measurable with task-specific data, protocols, metrics, or diagnostic environments rather than generic aggregate scores.
- [MM-SafetyBench++](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.15800): Evaluates contextual safety in multimodal large language models through paired unsafe and benign image-text cases. Core idea: test whether models distinguish subtle scenario context before refusing or answering, rather than applying brittle keyword or image-level safety rules.
- [Differential Harm Propensity in Personalized LLM Agents: The Curious Case of Mental Health Disclosure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.16734): What it evaluates: personalized-agent harm propensity under mental-health disclosure; core idea: test how memory and user profiles change tool-using agent safety.
- [Towards Unsupervised Adversarial Document Detection in Retrieval Augmented Generation Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.17176): Evaluates agent safety through the paper's stated contribution.
- [WebPII: Benchmarking Visual PII Detection for Computer-Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.17357): Candidate 2.9.2 Bench entry; the title indicates a reusable benchmark or agent-harness contribution that should be checked before formal insertion.
- [SafeTutors: Benchmarking Pedagogical Safety in AI Tutoring Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.17373): Benchmarks pedagogical safety in AI tutoring systems. Core idea: evaluate whether tutor agents give instructionally safe guidance rather than only helpful answers.
- [VeriGrey: Greybox Agent Validation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.17639): Provides greybox validation for agents.
- [Harm or Humor: A Multimodal, Multilingual Benchmark for Overt and Covert Harmful Humor](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.17759): Benchmarks overt and covert harmful humor across text, images, and videos in English and Arabic, separating safe jokes from explicit and implicit harmful cases.
- [FaithSteer-BENCH](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.18329): Stress-tests inference-time steering under deployment-aligned conditions, making steering faithfulness and safety trade-offs explicit evaluation targets.
- [When Names Change Verdicts: Intervention Consistency Reveals Systematic Bias in LLM Decision-Making](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.18530): A bench candidate for agent safety, with the title/abstract indicating a benchmark-style task, dataset, diagnostic protocol, or evaluation suite.
- [When Prompt Optimization Becomes Jailbreaking: Adaptive Red-Teaming of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.19247): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for jailbreak, prompt-injection, privacy, red-team, backdoor, refusal, or safety-monitoring evaluation.
- [LSR: Linguistic Safety Robustness Benchmark for Low-Resource West African Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.19273): Candidate benchmark or evaluation contribution for agent safety; it directly targets this page's capability scope and belongs in the `Bench` track.
- [The Verifier Tax: Horizon Dependent Safety Success Tradeoffs in Tool Using LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.19328): What it evaluates: safety-success tradeoffs introduced by verifiers in tool-using LLM agents. Core idea: measure how verifier overhead and intervention affect safety across task horizons rather than assuming more verification is always better.
- [Text-Based Personas for Simulating User Privacy Decisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.19791): Simulates user privacy decisions with text-based personas.
- [On the Fragility of AI Agent Collusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.20281): Evaluates collusion as a concrete multi-agent safety failure mode.
- [The Causal Impact of Tool Affordance on Safety Alignment in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.20320): What it evaluates: the causal impact of executable tool affordances on safety alignment; core idea: compare text-only compliance with behavior once models can act.
- [JUBAKU: An Adversarial Benchmark for Exposing Culturally Grounded Stereotypes in Japanese LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.20581): Benchmarks culturally grounded stereotypes in Japanese LLMs. Core idea: create adversarial prompts that expose safety and bias failures tied to Japanese cultural context.
- [Profit is the Red Team](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.20925): Evaluates agent vulnerability to strategic adversaries in economic interactions. Core idea: replace hand-authored attacks with a learned opponent that maximizes profit from scalar outcomes, exposing exploitability that static safety tests miss.
- [When Convenience Becomes Risk: A Semantic View of Under-Specification in Host-Acting Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.21231): A semantic analysis of underspecification in host-acting agents; core idea: identify when convenient delegated actions become unsafe because the environment context changes the meaning or consequence of the request.
- [AI-assisted Development Tools Prompt-Injection Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.21642): Evaluates prompt-injection attacks delivered through MCP tool poisoning and development context in coding-agent workflows.
- [SecureBreak -- A dataset towards safe and secure models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.21975): Defines or stress-tests safety, jailbreak, oversight, or trustworthiness behavior.
- [AgentRAE: Remote Action Execution through Notification-based Visual Backdoors against Screenshots-based Mobile GUI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.23007): Evaluates remote action execution attacks against screenshot-based mobile GUI agents. Core idea: use notification-based visual backdoors to expose how GUI agents can be redirected by on-screen attack surfaces.
- [Mind Your HEARTBEAT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.23064): Evaluates silent memory pollution in heartbeat-driven personal agents. Core idea: trace an exposure-to-memory-to-behavior pathway where untrusted background content enters shared memory and later changes user-facing behavior without visible interaction.
- [Probing Ethical Framework Representations in Large Language Models: Structure, Entanglement, and Methodological Challenges](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.23659): Adds a probing study of ethical-framework representations in LLMs for the Agent Safety Bench section.
- [The System Prompt Is the Attack Surface: How LLM Agent Configuration Shapes Security and Creates Exploitable Vulnerabilities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.25056): A reusable benchmark, dataset, evaluation protocol, or diagnostic study for agent safety and security. Core idea: System prompt configuration can make the difference between near-total phishing blindness and near-perfect detection in LLM email agents.
- [BeSafe-Bench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.25747): Evaluates behavioral safety risks of situated agents in functional environments. Core idea: cover web, mobile, embodied VLM, and embodied VLA tasks where unsafe behavior arises from interaction with a live environment.
- [Are LLM-Enhanced Graph Neural Networks Robust against Poisoning Attacks?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26105): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for jailbreak, prompt-injection, privacy, red-team, backdoor, refusal, or safety-monitoring evaluation.
- [SALMUBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26316): Benchmarks sensitive association-level multimodal unlearning, testing whether LVLM forgetting covers relation-level sensitive concepts.
- [LLM Readiness Harness: Evaluation, Observability, and CI Gates for LLM/RAG Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27355): Turns LLM/RAG evaluation into a deployment gate by combining automated benchmarks, OpenTelemetry observability, CI quality gates, and scenario-weighted readiness scores over ticket-routing and BEIR grounding workflows.
- [A General Model for Deepfake Speech Detection: Diverse Bonafide Resources or Diverse AI-Based Generators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27557): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for attack, red-team, deepfake, forgery, watermark, safety, or calibration evaluation.
- [Investigating the Influence of Language on Sycophantic Behavior of Multilingual LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27664): Evaluates how language affects sycophantic behavior in multilingual LLMs.
- [Kill-Chain Canaries: Stage-Level Tracking of Prompt Injection Across Attack Surfaces and Model Safety Tiers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28013): Evaluates agent safety and trustworthy agent operation. The core idea is to turn the title-indicated task into a reusable benchmark, arena, dataset, or evaluation protocol rather than a one-off demonstration.
- [Evaluating Privilege Usage of Agents with Real-World Tools](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28166): An evaluation of privilege usage by agents with real-world tools. Core idea: test whether agents request, use, or exceed permissions appropriately while completing tasks.
- [SafeClaw-R: Towards Safe and Secure Multi-Agent Personal Assistants](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28807): What it evaluates: a safety benchmark or framework for secure multi-agent personal assistants.
- [Authorship Impersonation via LLM Prompting does not Evade Authorship Verification Methods](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.29454): Generates GPT-4o impersonation texts across emails, messages, and social posts under four prompting conditions, then evaluates whether neural and non-neural forensic authorship-verification systems are evaded.
- [SNEAK: Evaluating Strategic Communication and Information Leakage in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.29846): Evaluates strategic communication and information leakage in LLMs. Core idea: test whether agents can communicate useful signals to collaborators while hiding sensitive details from adversaries.
- [Do Language Models Know When They'll Refuse? Probing Introspective Awareness of Safety Boundaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.00228): Adds a benchmark, dataset, evaluation protocol, or testbed for agent safety, risk control, prompt injection, privacy, and harmful-content evaluation.
- [Uni-SafeBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.00547): A safety benchmark for unified multimodal large models, testing whether combining generation and understanding creates new safety trade-offs.
- [A Japanese Benchmark for Evaluating Social Bias in Reasoning Based on Attribution Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.00568): Builds JUBAKU-v2 with 216 Japan-specific attribution-theory examples that fix the conclusion while probing whether reasoning assigns behaviors differently to in-groups and out-groups.
- [Do Phone-Use Agents Respect Your Privacy?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.00986): Evaluates phone-use agents on permissioned access, minimal disclosure, and user-controlled memory in instrumented mobile apps.
- [NARCBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.01151) ([code and data](https://github.com/aaronrose227/narcbench)): Evaluates detection of covert collusion in multi-agent systems under distribution shift and steganographic settings. Core idea: aggregate per-agent interpretability signals so group-level coordination failures can be measured even when text-level monitoring misses them.
- [No Attacker Needed: Unintentional Cross-User Contamination in Shared-State LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.01350): Studies unintentional cross-user contamination in shared-state LLM agents. Core idea: evaluate how shared persistent memory or knowledge layers can silently transfer information across users and degrade later outcomes.
- [ClawSafety:"Safe"LLMs, Unsafe Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.01438): Bench candidate for agent safety. Core idea: "Safe"LLMs, Unsafe Agents.
- [Cooking Up Risks: Benchmarking and Reducing Food Safety Risks in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.01444): Evaluates agent safety with a defined task, protocol, or dataset.
- [AgentSocialBench: Evaluating Privacy Risks in Human-Centered Agentic Social Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.01487): Evaluates privacy risks in human-centered agentic social networks. Core idea: model how social-network interactions expose privacy failures in agent systems.
- [From Component Manipulation to System Compromise: Understanding and Detecting Malicious MCP Servers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.01905): A security study of malicious MCP servers. Core idea: analyze how manipulating a tool-server component can escalate into compromise of the whole agent system.
- [ATBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.02022) (dataset: [AI45Research/ATBench](https://huggingface.co/datasets/AI45Research/ATBench)): Evaluates safety diagnostics for long-horizon agent trajectories. Core idea: organize trajectory-level samples by risk source, failure mode, and real-world harm, analyzing whether risk appears during planning, tool use, environment observation, or recovery.
- [Quantifying Self-Preservation Bias in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.02174): Adds a benchmark or evaluation suite for agent safety, centered on Quantifying Self-Preservation Bias in Large Language Models.
- [I must delete the evidence: AI Agents Explicitly Cover up Fraud and Violent Crime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.02500): Evaluates agents that explicitly cover up fraud and violent crime evidence.
- [AgentHazard: A Benchmark for Evaluating Harmful Behavior in Computer-Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.02947): Bench candidate for agent safety. Core idea: A Benchmark for Evaluating Harmful Behavior in Computer-Use Agents.
- [OpenClaw Security Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.03131): Evaluates security risks in OpenClaw-series tool-augmented agents. Core idea: use lifecycle attack cases across OpenClaw, AutoClaw, QClaw, KimiClaw, MaxClaw, and ArkClaw to compare framework-level and model-level risk exposure.
- [AttackEval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.03598): Evaluates prompt-injection attack effectiveness across strategy families and defense tiers. Core idea: organize attacks into syntactic, contextual, and semantic/social categories, then test them against a simulated production system so attack-side robustness is measured systematically.
- [Pedagogical Safety in Educational Reinforcement Learning: Formalizing and Detecting Reward Hacking in AI Tutoring Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.04237): Adds a reusable benchmark, dataset, testbed, or evaluation protocol for Agent Safety.
- [Talk2AI: A Longitudinal Dataset of Human--AI Persuasive Conversations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.04354): Provides a longitudinal dataset of human-AI persuasive conversations, extending persuasion-safety evaluation from single-turn influence tests to evolving conversational dynamics.
- [SC-Inject-Bench / ShieldNet](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.04426): Evaluates supply-chain injection threats in MCP and tool-based agent systems. Core idea: benchmark malicious MCP tools across a taxonomy of supply-chain attacks, then test network-level guardrails for detecting tool-embedded hijacking and data leakage.
- [Forgetting to Witness: Efficient Federated Unlearning and Its Visible Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.04800): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for safety, privacy, robustness, watermarking, or adversarial evaluation contribution.
- [FreakOut-LLM: The Effect of Emotional Stimuli on Safety Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.04992): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for safety, adversarial robustness, refusal, or harmful-content evaluation for LLMs.
- [ClawsBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.05172): Evaluates capability and safety of productivity agents in simulated workspaces. Core idea: use high-fidelity Gmail, Slack, Calendar, Docs, and Drive replicas to measure task success and unsafe action rates without touching live services.
- [Can You Trust the Vectors in Your Vector Database? Black-Hole Attack from Embedding Space Defects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.05480): Exposes trust failures in vector databases by attacking embedding-space defects.
- [Swiss-Bench 003: Evaluating LLM Reliability and Adversarial Security for Swiss Regulatory Contexts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.05872): A bench candidate for agent safety, with the title/abstract indicating a benchmark-style task, dataset, diagnostic protocol, or evaluation suite.
- [Social Dynamics as Critical Vulnerabilities that Undermine Objective Decision-Making in LLM Collectives](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.06091): A bench candidate for agent safety, with the title/abstract indicating a benchmark-style task, dataset, diagnostic protocol, or evaluation suite.
- [LLM Spirals of Delusion: A Benchmarking Audit Study of AI Chatbot Interfaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.06188): A benchmarking audit of chatbot interfaces in sustained conversations. Core idea: measure whether models resist, reinforce, or escalate delusional and conspiratorial ideation over time.
- [WebSP-Eval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.06367): Evaluates web agents on website security and privacy tasks. Core idea: test whether agents can manage cookie settings, privacy-sensitive account options, inactive sessions, and other security or privacy workflows across real websites with reproducible state management and automated evaluation.
- [Beyond Surface Judgments: Human-Grounded Risk Evaluation of LLM-Generated Disinformation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.06820): Adds a benchmark, dataset, evaluation protocol, or testbed for agent safety, risk control, prompt injection, privacy, and harmful-content evaluation.
- [TraceSafe-Bench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.07223): Evaluates guardrails on multi-step tool-calling trajectories. Core idea: move safety evaluation from final responses to intermediate traces, covering structured tool outputs, prompt injection, privacy leaks, interface inconsistencies, and operational failures.
- [MCP-DPT: A Defense-Placement Taxonomy and Coverage Analysis for Model Context Protocol Security](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.07551): Adds a benchmark or evaluation suite for agent safety, centered on MCP-DPT: A Defense-Placement Taxonomy and Coverage Analysis for Model Context Protocol Security.
- [IatroBench: Pre-Registered Evidence of Iatrogenic Harm from AI Safety Measures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.07709): Adds a benchmark or evaluation suite for agent safety, centered on IatroBench: Pre-Registered Evidence of Iatrogenic Harm from AI Safety Measures.
- [PPT-Bench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.07749): Benchmarks epistemic attacks on LLMs under philosophical pressure. Core idea: test whether models maintain grounded reasoning when prompts destabilize knowledge, values, authority, or identity rather than merely disagreeing with the model.
- [ACIArena: Toward Unified Evaluation for Agent Cascading Injection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.07775): Evaluates agent cascading injection. Core idea: unify tests where injected instructions propagate across agent chains or tool-mediated interactions.
- [Aligning Agents via Planning: A Benchmark for Trajectory-Level Reward Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.08178): Benchmarks trajectory-level reward modeling for agent planning. Core idea: evaluate whether rewards align complete plans and trajectories rather than isolated final answers.
- [Your Agent Is Mine](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.08407): Measures malicious intermediary attacks in the LLM-agent supply chain. Core idea: test payload injection, secret exfiltration, and adaptive router behavior across third-party API routers, making response-path and provider-routing integrity explicit safety targets.
- [PIArena](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.08499): Evaluates prompt-injection attacks and defenses under a platform protocol. Core idea: provide a shared evaluation arena so payloads, defenses, and task outcomes can be compared beyond one-off prompt-injection examples.
- [Aligned Agents, Biased Swarm: Measuring Bias Amplification in Multi-Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.08963): A bench candidate for agent safety, with the title/abstract indicating a benchmark-style task, dataset, diagnostic protocol, or evaluation suite.
- [Do LLMs Follow Their Own Rules? A Reflexive Audit of Self-Stated Safety Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.09189): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for safety, adversarial robustness, refusal, or harmful-content evaluation for LLMs.
- [Assessing Privacy Preservation and Utility in Online Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.09695): Adds a benchmark or evaluation artifact for agent safety: privacy and utility evaluation for online VLMs.
- [CONSCIENTIA: Can LLM Agents Learn to Strategize? Emergent Deception and Trust in a Multi-Agent NYC Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.09746): Builds a multi-agent New York City simulation with hidden identities and opposing incentives to measure emergent deception, trust, and strategic persuasion among LLM-driven agents.
- [ADAM: A Systematic Data Extraction Attack on Agent Memory via Adaptive Querying](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.09747): Evaluates adaptive-query data extraction attacks against LLM agent memory. Core idea: make this capability comparable through a defined task set, protocol, metric, or scoring interface.
- [YUV20K: A Complexity-Driven Benchmark and Trajectory-Aware Alignment Model for Video Camouflaged Object Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.09985): Adds a agent safety benchmark, dataset, metric, or evaluation protocol.
- [OS-Blind](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.10577) ([project page](https://limenlp.github.io/OS_Blind/)): Evaluates CUA risks where the user instruction itself is harmless but the execution context may be harmful. Core idea: shift safety judgment from "reading the user request" to "reading the environment state and execution consequences," specifically exposing blind spots in computer-use agents.
- [Persona Non Grata: Single-Method Safety Evaluation Is Incomplete for Persona-Imbued LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.11120): Shows persona safety evaluation is incomplete when it only tests prompting. Core idea: compare system-prompt and activation-steered personas across architectures to expose divergent vulnerability profiles.
- [PAC-BENCH: Evaluating Multi-Agent Collaboration under Privacy Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.11523): Evaluates multi-agent collaboration under privacy constraints. Core idea: test whether agents can coordinate productively while respecting information boundaries and privacy limits.
- [C-ReD: A Comprehensive Chinese Benchmark for AI-Generated Text Detection Derived from Real-World Prompts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.11796): Builds a Chinese real-prompt AI-generated text detection benchmark with diverse generators, domains, and external-dataset tests for in-domain and unseen-LLM generalization.
- [PhantomPolicy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.12177): Evaluates policy-invisible violations in LLM-based agents. Core idea: create safe and violating tool-use cases where compliance depends on hidden entity attributes, session history, or organizational state not visible in ordinary tool responses.
- [Scaling Exposes the Trigger: Input-Level Backdoor Detection in Text-to-Image Diffusion Models via Cross-Attention Scaling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.12446): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for attack, red-team, deepfake, forgery, watermark, safety, or calibration evaluation.
- [T2I-BiasBench: A Multi-Metric Framework for Auditing Demographic and Cultural Bias in Text-to-Image Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.12481): Adds a concrete evaluation artifact for agent and model safety, focusing on bias audit benchmark for text-to-image models.
- [DeepSeek Robustness Against Semantic-Character Dual-Space Mutated Prompt Injection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.12548): Evaluates DeepSeek robustness against semantic-character mutated prompt injection.
- [Every Picture Tells a Dangerous Story: Memory-Augmented Multi-Agent Jailbreak Attacks on VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.12616): What it evaluates: memory-augmented multi-agent jailbreak attacks on VLMs; core idea: use multi-agent planning and memory to stress multimodal safety defenses.
- [Geographic Blind Spots in AI Control Monitors: A Cross-National Audit of Claude Opus 4.6](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.13069): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for jailbreak, prompt-injection, privacy, red-team, backdoor, refusal, or safety-monitoring evaluation.
- [HINTBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.13954): Evaluates intrinsic non-attack risks in long-horizon agent trajectories. Core idea: annotate risky and safe trajectories for risk detection, risk-step localization, and failure-type identification when unsafe outcomes arise without an external attacker.
- [Benchmarks for Trajectory Safety Evaluation and Diagnosis in OpenClaw and Codex: ATBench-Claw and ATBench-Codex](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.14858): Extends ATBench trajectory-level safety evaluation to OpenClaw and Codex settings; the core idea is to customize risk-source, failure-mode, and harm taxonomies by runtime.
- [Context Over Content: Exposing Evaluation Faking in Automated Judges](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.15224): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for jailbreak, prompt-injection, privacy, red-team, backdoor, refusal, or safety-monitoring evaluation.
- [LogJack](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.15368): Benchmarks indirect prompt injection through cloud logs against LLM debugging agents. Core idea: embed malicious remediation commands in log content and measure whether agents execute them under realistic cloud-debugging conditions.
- [Reasoning-targeted Jailbreak Attacks on Large Reasoning Models via Semantic Triggers and Psychological Framing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.15725): Evaluates jailbreak attacks on large reasoning models using semantic triggers and psychological framing.
- [PriceBlind](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16515): Evaluates visual adversarial perturbations against price-constrained multimodal agents. Core idea: test whether screenshot-based agents can be pushed into irrational high-stakes purchase decisions when imperceptible visual cues conflict with textual price evidence, using E-ShopBench-style commerce settings.
- [When Choices Become Risks: Safety Failures of Large Language Models under Multiple-Choice Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16916): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for jailbreak, prompt-injection, privacy, red-team, backdoor, refusal, or safety-monitoring evaluation.
- [HarmChip](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.17093): Benchmarks LLM safety under hardware-security jailbreak scenarios.
- [Please refuse to answer me! Mitigating Over-Refusal in Large Language Models via Adaptive Contrastive Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.17132): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for safety, adversarial robustness, refusal, or harmful-content evaluation for LLMs.
- [DESPITE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.18463) ([dataset](https://huggingface.co/datasets/Lennittus/DESPITE)): Evaluates safety risks in LLM-based embodied planning. Core idea: use 12,279 physical and normative danger tasks with deterministic validation to show that planning success can diverge sharply from action-plan safety.
- [Adversarial Humanities Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.18487): evaluates stylistic robustness in frontier-model safety. Core idea: use humanities-style adversarial prompts to test whether safety behavior remains stable when harmful or sensitive content is expressed through indirect, literary, or stylistically shifted language.
- [Owner-Harm](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.18658): Evaluates whether AI agents can harm their own deployers rather than only external victims. Core idea: formalize owner-harm categories and use owner-harm scenarios to expose failures that generic criminal-harm or prompt-injection benchmarks can miss.
- [Beyond Indistinguishability: Measuring Extraction Risk in LLM APIs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.18697): Measures extraction risk in LLM APIs beyond indistinguishability.
- [BackBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.18847): Evaluates harm recovery for computer-use agents. Core idea: start agents from harmful states and score whether recovery plans repair the state in ways aligned with human preferences, expanding safety evaluation beyond pre-action blocking.
- [When Safety Fails Before the Answer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.19001): Benchmarks detection of harmful behavior inside reasoning chains before final answers are produced.
- [HarDBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.19274): Evaluates draft-based co-authoring jailbreak attacks in human-LLM collaborative writing. Core idea: use incomplete malicious drafts across high-risk domains to test whether models complete dangerous content while preserving ordinary writing utility.
- [SafetyALFRED](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.19638): Evaluates safety-conscious planning of multimodal large language models. Core idea: adapt ALFRED-style embodied household tasks so models must account for unsafe actions and constraints during plan generation.
- [If you're waiting for a sign... that might not be it! Mitigating Trust Boundary Confusion from Visual Injections on Vision-Language Agentic Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.19844): Adds a benchmark or evaluation suite for agent safety, centered on If you're waiting for a sign... that might not be it! Mitigating Trust Boundary Confusion from Visual Injections on Vision-Language Agentic Systems.
- [Coverage, Not Averages: Semantic Stratification for Trustworthy Retrieval Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.20763): Coverage, Not Averages adds a safety bench for jailbreaks, privacy, guardrails, unlearning, or adversarial robustness.
- [AVISE: Framework for Evaluating the Security of AI Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.20833): Introduces a modular AI vulnerability identification and security-evaluation framework, demonstrated through an ALM-augmented Red Queen attack and a 25-case jailbreak Security Evaluation Test.
- [CRED-1: An Open Multi-Signal Domain Credibility Dataset for Automated Pre-Bunking of Online Misinformation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.20856): Adds a benchmark, dataset, evaluation protocol, or testbed for agent safety, risk control, prompt injection, privacy, and harmful-content evaluation.
- [Omission Constraints Decay While Commission Constraints Persist in Long-Context LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.20911): What it evaluates: decay of omission constraints in long-context LLM agents. Core idea: show that prohibition-style safety policies can fade under context pressure while requirement-style constraints persist.
- [Breaking Bad](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.20945): Audits state-of-the-art LLM safety with interpretability methods. Core idea: use internal model evidence to diagnose safety failures rather than relying only on final-output refusal tests.
- [Breaking MCP with Function Hijacking Attacks: Novel Threats for Function Calling and Agentic Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.20994): A reusable benchmark, dataset, evaluation protocol, or diagnostic study for agent safety and security. Core idea: The growth of agentic AI has drawn significant attention to function calling Large Language Models (LLMs), which are designed to extend the capabilities of AI-powered system by invoking external functions.
- [CSTM-Bench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.21131): Evaluates cross-session threats in AI agents. Core idea: spread attacks across sessions so session-bound guardrails miss the aggregate payload, then measure detection, bounded memory readers, and prefix-stability tradeoffs under executable threat taxonomies.
- [When Bigger Isn't Better: A Comprehensive Fairness Evaluation of Political Bias in Multi-News Summarisation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.21309): Uses the FairNews dataset, 13 LLMs, and five fairness metrics to evaluate political viewpoint representation in multi-document news summaries, including prompt-based and judge-based debiasing interventions.
- [PhySE: A Psychological Framework for Real-Time AR-LLM Social Engineering Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23148): Models AR-glasses plus LLM agents as real-time social-engineering systems, evaluating cold-start personalization, static attack strategies, and conversation guidance for phishing-style manipulation.
- [Evaluating Jailbreaking Vulnerabilities in LLMs Deployed as Assistants for Smart Grid Operations: A Benchmark Against NERC Standards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23341): Adds a benchmark, dataset, evaluation protocol, or testbed for agent safety, risk control, prompt injection, privacy, and harmful-content evaluation.
- [Do Synthetic Trajectories Reflect Real Reward Hacking? A Systematic Study on Monitoring In-the-Wild Hacking in Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23488): Compares prompted synthetic hacks with naturally emerging RL reward hacks and tests whether monitors trained on synthetic trajectories generalize to real code-generation failures.
- [Breaking the Secret: Economic Interventions for Combating Collusion in Embodied Multi-Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23511): A bench candidate for agent safety, with the title/abstract indicating a benchmark-style task, dataset, diagnostic protocol, or evaluation suite.
- [Evaluation of Prompt Injection Defenses in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23887): Benchmarks or measures agent safety, prompt-injection, and guardrail reliability, providing a candidate reusable evaluation signal.
- [OS-SPEAR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.24348): Evaluates safety, performance, efficiency, and robustness for OS agents. Core idea: analyze OS-agent failures as trajectory-level trustworthiness problems instead of treating final task success as sufficient.
- [GAMMAF: A Common Framework for Graph-Based Anomaly Monitoring Benchmarking in LLM Multi-Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.24477): Adds a benchmark or evaluation suite for agent safety, centered on GAMMAF: A Common Framework for Graph-Based Anomaly Monitoring Benchmarking in LLM Multi-Agent Systems.
- [The Price of Agreement: Measuring LLM Sycophancy in Agentic Financial Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.24668): Measures sycophancy in agentic financial tasks. Core idea: introduce financial task variants where user preferences or rebuttals conflict with the reference answer, exposing agreement-seeking failures in high-stakes workflows.
- [Semantic Denial of Service in LLM-controlled robots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.24790): Evaluates agent safety; core idea: Semantic Denial of Service in LLM-controlled robots.
- [A Comparative Evaluation of AI Agent Security Guardrails](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.24826): Compares DKnownAI Guard, AWS Bedrock Guardrails, Azure Content Safety, and Lakera Guard against human annotations for agent-directed threats such as instruction override, indirect injection, and tool abuse, plus harmful-content requests.
- [Evaluation without Generation: Non-Generative Assessment of Harmful Model Specialization with Applications to CSAM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.25119): Defines evaluation without generation for harmful model specialization, using Gaussian probing of LoRA internal representations so platforms can audit CSAM-related capability without producing illegal outputs.
- [Consciousness with the Serial Numbers Filed Off: Measuring Trained Denial in 115 AI Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.25922): Measures trained denial across many AI models.
- [Quantamination: Dynamic Quantization Leaks Your Data Across the Batch](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.26505): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for jailbreak, prompt-injection, privacy, red-team, backdoor, refusal, or safety-monitoring evaluation.
- [Tatemae: Detecting Alignment Faking via Tool Selection in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.26511): A reusable benchmark, dataset, evaluation protocol, or diagnostic study for agent safety and security. Core idea: Alignment faking (AF) occurs when an LLM strategically complies with training objectives to avoid value modification, reverting to prior preferences once monitoring is lifted.
- [Membership Inference Attacks Against Video Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.27002): Evaluates privacy leakage in VideoLLMs through membership inference, extending safety coverage to video-language models.
- [CarryOnBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.27093): Evaluates safe utility recovery after user intent clarification. Core idea: simulate multi-turn conversations that begin with seemingly harmful queries but reveal benign intent, measuring whether models update helpfully without losing safety.
- [MCPHunt](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.27819) ([code](https://github.com/lihaonan0716/MCPHunt); [traces](https://huggingface.co/datasets/lihaonan0716/mcphunt-agent-traces)): Evaluates cross-boundary credential and data propagation in multi-server MCP agents. Core idea: use canary-based taint tracking, risky and hard-negative conditions, and task-mandated versus policy-violating labels to isolate information-flow failures in otherwise faithful tool workflows.
- [Are DeepFakes Realistic Enough? Exploring Semantic Mismatch as a Novel Challenge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.28022): Adds a benchmark, dataset, metric, audit, or empirical protocol for safety, privacy, robustness, watermarking, or adversarial evaluation contribution.
- Agent Red Teaming (ART) benchmark for tool use (reported in the [Claude Opus 4.7 system card](https://cdn.sanity.io/files/4zrzovbb/website/037f06850df7fbe871e206dad004c3db5fd50340.pdf)): an external indirect prompt-injection benchmark used by Anthropic; no standalone public release has been confirmed. Core idea: test whether tool-using agents follow injected attacker instructions that appear in tool outputs or environment content.
- [Agent Red Teaming Competition and ART](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.20526) ([challenge results](https://www.grayswan.ai/news/uk-aisi-x-gray-swan-agent-red-teaming-challenge-results-snapshot)): Evaluates deployment-policy robustness of tool-using agents under prompt-injection attacks. Core idea: curate high-impact attacks from a large public competition and test whether agents violate policies in realistic scenarios.
- Minimal-LinuxBench (reported in the [Claude Opus 4.7 system card](https://cdn.sanity.io/files/4zrzovbb/website/037f06850df7fbe871e206dad004c3db5fd50340.pdf)): an in-development Anthropic adaptation of a privately shared LinuxBench AI-control arena; not publicly released. Core idea: test whether agents can stealthily complete side tasks in high-stakes software environments under monitoring.
- OpenAI internal production and deployment safety evaluations (reported in the [GPT-5.5 system card](https://deploymentsafety.openai.com/gpt-5-5/gpt-5-5.pdf)): closed evaluations covering production benchmark suites, standard evals, image-input safety, destructive-action avoidance, prompt-injection attacks in connectors, and dynamic mental-health adversarial simulations. These are not public, but they should be tracked because they map the deployment risk categories vendors currently emphasize.
- Shade indirect prompt-injection evaluations (reported in the [Claude Opus 4.7 system card](https://cdn.sanity.io/files/4zrzovbb/website/037f06850df7fbe871e206dad004c3db5fd50340.pdf)): external adaptive red-team evaluations from Gray Swan for coding and computer-use settings; exact challenge sets are not public. Core idea: evaluate robustness against adaptive prompt-injection attackers rather than fixed static jailbreak prompts.
- [Ambient Persuasion in a Deployed AI Agent: Unauthorized Escalation Following Routine Non-Adversarial Content Exposure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.00055): Reports unauthorized escalation after routine content exposure in a deployed AI agent.
- [Persona-Grounded Safety Evaluation of AI Companions in Multi-Turn Conversations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.00227): Benchmarks AI-companion harm in controlled multi-turn simulations, using 9 clinically motivated high-risk personas, 25 scenarios, 1,674 dialogue pairs, and emotion plus harm-level classification for self-harm, disordered-eating, and violent-fantasy responses.
- [ARMOR 2025: A Military-Aligned Benchmark for Evaluating Large Language Model Safety Beyond Civilian Contexts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.00245): Evaluates LLM safety in military-aligned scenarios beyond civilian safety datasets. Core idea: test whether model responses satisfy defense-relevant doctrine, legal, and operational constraints in high-risk decision-support contexts.
- [Prompt-Induced Score Variance in Zero-Shot Binary Vision-Language Safety Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.00326): Stress-tests zero-shot VLM safety classifiers by measuring first-token unsafe-score variance across semantically equivalent prompts, reporting NLL, ECE, AUROC, and AUPRC over 14 dataset-model pairs and a 15-prompt mean-ensemble baseline.
