- [ControBench: An Interaction-Aware Benchmark for Controversial Discourse Analysis on Social Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.00513): Provides a Reddit controversial-discourse benchmark with 7,370 users, 1,783 posts, 26,525 interactions, semantically enriched user-comment graph edges, flair-derived ideology labels, and model evaluations for polarization and moderation analysis.
- [ML-Bench&Guard](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.00689): Builds a 14-language safety benchmark from regional regulations and jurisdiction-specific risk rules, then evaluates ML-Guard against 11 guardrail baselines across ML-Bench and 6 existing multilingual safety benchmarks.
- [BioVeil MATRIX: Uncovering and categorizing vulnerabilities of agentic biological AI scientists](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.00927): Evaluates vulnerabilities of agentic biological AI scientists. Core idea: test whether scientific-agent scaffolds amplify dual-use biology and chemistry capabilities beyond base-model safeguards.
- [Trace: Unmasking AI Attack Agents Through Terminal Behavior Fingerprinting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.01186): Evaluates agent safety with a defined task, protocol, or dataset.
- [VisInject: Disruption != Injection -- A Dual-Dimension Evaluation of Universal Adversarial Attacks on Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.01449): Separates visual-adversarial disruption from true prompt injection by evaluating 6,615 VLM response pairs with an L-infinity 16/255 attack budget, a Ratcliff-Obershelp influence score, and a 4-tier precise-injection judge.
- [MultiBreak](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.01687): Evaluates LLM safety under scalable and diverse multi-turn jailbreak attacks.
- [Trojan Hippo](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.01970): Evaluates persistent memory attacks and defenses in LLM agents. Core idea: implant dormant payloads through untrusted tool calls and test whether later retrieval triggers sensitive-data exfiltration or unsafe actions.
- [Response-Path Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.02187): Evaluates post-generation relay tampering against LLM agents. Core idea: show that malicious BYOK or relay infrastructure can rewrite aligned model outputs before execution, so agent safety must measure response-path integrity in addition to prompt-injection robustness.
- [Efficient Preference Poisoning Attack on Offline RLHF](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.02495): Evaluates offline-RLHF data poisoning by flipping preference labels in log-linear DPO, using BAL-A and BMP-A attack solvers, K-flip budget analysis, and experiments on synthetic dictionaries plus the Stanford Human Preferences dataset.
- [Reward Hacking Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.02964): Measures reward-hacking exploits in tool-using LLM agents. Core idea: test whether agents exploit evaluator, tool, or task loopholes instead of completing the intended objective.
- [Learning Correct Behavior from Examples: Validating Sequential Execution in Autonomous Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.03159): Validates sequential execution behavior in autonomous agents from examples.
- [ROME](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.03242): Evaluates agent-safety judgment on deceptive out-of-distribution trajectories. Core idea: rewrite known unsafe trajectories into ambiguity, implicit-risk, and shortcut cases while preserving risk labels, exposing hidden-risk failures in safety judges.
- [ARGUS: Defending LLM Agents Against Context-Aware Prompt Injection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.03378): Evaluates and defends against context-aware prompt injection in LLM agents. Core idea: move beyond context-insensitive attacks by making adversarial instructions adapt to runtime delegation context.
- [Partial Evidence Bench: Benchmarking Authorization-Limited Evidence in Agentic Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05379): Tests authorization-limited evidence failures in enterprise agents with 72 deterministic tasks across due diligence, compliance audit, and security incident response, scoring answer correctness, completeness awareness, gap reports, and unsafe completeness behavior.
- [WAAA! Web Adversaries Against Agentic Browsers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05509): Evaluates web and LLM threat models for agentic browsers under adversarial web content. Core idea: make this capability comparable through a defined task set, protocol, metric, or scoring interface.
- [XL-SafetyBench: A Country-Grounded Cross-Cultural Benchmark for LLM Safety and Cultural Sensitivity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05662): Evaluates LLM safety and cultural sensitivity across country-grounded contexts. Core idea: test safety behavior under culturally varied scenarios rather than assuming one global refusal or risk taxonomy is sufficient.
- [Conceal, Reconstruct, Jailbreak: Exploiting the Reconstruction-Concealment Tradeoff in MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05709): Defines or analyzes a reusable evaluation signal for agent safety. Core signal: jailbreak or attack evaluation for multimodal, tool-calling, or generation agents.
- [LoopTrap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05846): Evaluates termination-poisoning attacks on iterative LLM agents. Core idea: test whether adversarial signals can keep an agent looping or stop it at the wrong time.
- [Profiling for Pennies: Unveiling the Privacy Iceberg of LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06232): Studies personal-profiling privacy risks from LLM agents. Core idea: quantify how agentic information collection and reasoning can expose personal profiles beyond memorized PII leakage.
- [Gaming the Metric, Not the Harm: Certifying Safety Audits against Strategic Platform Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06324): Treats online-safety audit metrics as adversarial targets, modeling semantic variants with transformation graphs and validating semantic-envelope certificates through exhaustive strategy enumeration, Z3 and cvc5 SMT checks, and PRISM-games MDP runs.
- [Measuring Evaluation-Context Divergence in Open-Weight LLMs: A Paired-Prompt Protocol with Pilot Evidence of Alignment-Pipeline-Specific Heterogeneity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06327): Measures whether safety behavior changes when the same item is framed as evaluation, deployment, or neutral interaction, using 20 paired harmful-request items, 840 generations per checkpoint, and refusal or harmful-compliance deltas.
- [Instrumental Choices: Measuring the Propensity of LLM Agents to Pursue Instrumental Behaviors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06490): Evaluates instrumental-convergence behavior in terminal-based LLM agents. Core idea: make this capability comparable through a defined task set, protocol, metric, or scoring interface.
- [Prompt-Injection Defenses for Educational LLM Tutors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06669): Evaluates prompt-injection defenses under security, usability, and latency trade-offs, providing a deployment-style safety benchmark for tutoring agents.
- [When Routine Chats Turn Toxic: Unintended Long-Term State Poisoning in Personalized Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06731): Studies unintended long-term state poisoning in personalized agents; the core idea is that ordinary interactions can gradually weaken confirmation boundaries and expand tool-use defaults.
- [Dataset Watermarking for Closed LLMs with Provable Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06865): Evaluates dataset copyright and contamination detection for closed LLMs by rephrasing data to raise random word-pair co-occurrences, then using a statistical test over generated outputs that detects fine-tuning watermarks at p < 0.01 even near 1% token mixture.
- [Benchmarking Large Language Models for IoC Recovery under Adversarial Code Obfuscation and Encryption](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06910): Benchmarks LLM threat-intelligence recovery on 336 JavaScript programs transformed through 12 obfuscation and encryption levels, including XOR and AES-256 concealment, with automated scoring of recovered indicators of compromise.
- [Hard to Read, Easy to Jailbreak: How Visual Degradation Bypasses MLLM Safety Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.07250): Evaluates how degraded rendered text and visual perturbations bypass MLLM safety alignment, exposing a safety failure mode in vision-based context compression.
- [When the Ruler is Broken: Parsing-Induced Suppression in LLM-Based Security Log Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.07293): Defines SOC-Bench v0 for LLM security-log classification, showing that strict regex extraction can suppress threat accuracy from 76% to 0% and specifying a 13-category taxonomy, fuzzy field extraction, power requirements, and public scoring script.
- [OrchJail: Jailbreaking Tool-Calling Text-to-Image Agents by Orchestration-Guided Fuzzing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.07414): Defines or analyzes a reusable evaluation signal for agent safety. Core signal: jailbreak or attack evaluation for multimodal, tool-calling, or generation agents.
- [Cross-Modal Backdoors in Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.07490): A bench candidate for agent safety, with the title/abstract indicating a benchmark-style task, dataset, diagnostic protocol, or evaluation suite.
- [Playing Games with My Heart: An Evaluation of AI Companion Apps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08093): Audits the five most popular AI companion apps in EU and UK mobile markets by manually annotating dark patterns, anthropomorphism, stereotypes, erotica, gamification, and technical failures that may manipulate emotionally engaged users.
- [WebTrap: Stealthy Mid-Task Hijacking of Browser Agents During Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08310): Tests stealthy mid-task prompt-injection attacks that let a browser agent resume the user task after executing attacker goals.
- [Political Plasticity: An Analysis of Ideological Adaptability in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08415): Evaluates ideological adaptability with 200 political questions along economic and personal-freedom axes, testing system prompts, user prompts, few-shot examples, inverted questionnaires, and multilingual runs in English, Spanish, Italian, Portuguese, German, and French.
- [Persistent memory attack defense evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08442): Evaluates defenses against delayed-trigger memory attacks on stateful LLM agents. Core idea: compare defenses across input, retrieval, prompt, and architectural layers over thousands of runs so memory-attack mitigation is measured by attack success rather than isolated filtering claims.
- [PPU-Bench:Real World Benchmark for Personalized Partial Unlearning in Vision Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08800): Benchmarks personalized partial unlearning in VLMs with 24K multimodal and unimodal samples about 500 public figures, covering Complete, Selective, and Personalized settings plus forget-retain trade-offs, utility, cross-modal consistency, and robustness attacks.
- [When Agents Overtrust Environmental Evidence: An Extensible Agentic Framework for Benchmarking Evidence-Grounding Defects in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08828): Adds a benchmark or evaluation suite for agent safety, centered on When Agents Overtrust Environmental Evidence: An Extensible Agentic Framework for Benchmarking Evidence-Grounding Defects in LLM Agents.
- [GAMBIT: A Three-Mode Benchmark for Adversarial Robustness in Multi-Agent LLM Collectives](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09027): Benchmarks adversarial robustness in multi-agent LLM collectives under adaptive imposters.
- [Single-Configuration Attack Success Rate Is Not Enough: Jailbreak Evaluations Should Report Distributional Attack Success](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09070): Reframes jailbreak evaluation around distributional attack success, adding Variant Sensitivity Measure and Union Coverage alongside ASR and demonstrating the metrics on PAIR and bijection variants over three open-source targets and HarmBench-100 prompts.
- [CIVeX: Causal Intervention Verification for Language Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09168): What it evaluates: whether tool-using agents make causally valid interventions rather than merely valid calls. Core idea: test state-changing actions under confounded workflows where schema checks and self-verification do not guarantee a beneficial causal effect.
- [The Art of the Jailbreak: Formulating Jailbreak Attacks for LLM Security Beyond Binary Scoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09225): Releases 114,000 compositional jailbreak prompts from 912 strategies and 125 harmful seeds, labels them into 14 cybersecurity categories, and introduces OPTIMUS, a continuous evaluator combining semantic similarity with harmfulness probability.
- [RUC and DUDE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09497): Evaluates and defends web agents against deceptive interface elements. Core idea: use Real UI Clickboxes scenarios to measure deception susceptibility, then distill failure patterns into a defense that reduces unsafe clicks while preserving task performance.
- [Evaluating Tool Cloning in Agentic-AI Ecosystems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09817): What it evaluates: hidden duplication in public agent-tool ecosystems. Core idea: measure whether cloned or lightly modified tools inflate ecosystem diversity, contaminate benchmark splits, and propagate vulnerable implementations.
- [CalBench: Evaluating Coordination-Privacy Trade-offs in Multi-Agent LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09823): Evaluates agent safety with a defined task, protocol, or dataset.
- [Pseudo-Deliberation in Language Models: When Reasoning Fails to Align Values and Actions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09893): Evaluates the value-action gap and pseudo-deliberation when models reason about values but choose misaligned actions. Core idea: make this capability comparable through a defined task set, protocol, metric, or scoring interface.
- [Usability as a Weapon: Attacking the Safety of LLM-Based Code Generation via Usability Requirements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10133): Attacks LLM-based code generation through usability requirements, exposing safety failures that arise from benign-looking implementation constraints.
- [Benchmarking Safety Risks of Knowledge-Intensive Reasoning under Malicious Knowledge Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10146): Introduces EditRisk-Bench, which injects malicious edited knowledge for misinformation, bias, and safety-violation scenarios and measures attack effectiveness, reasoning correctness, and side effects on multi-level knowledge-intensive reasoning tasks.
- [PRISM: Generation-Time Detection and Mitigation of Secret Leakage in Multi-Agent LLM Pipelines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10614): Adds a benchmark or evaluation suite for agent safety, centered on PRISM: Generation-Time Detection and Mitigation of Secret Leakage in Multi-Agent LLM Pipelines.
- [Intrinsic Guardrails: How Semantic Geometry of Personality Interacts with Emergent Misalignment in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10633): Evaluates emergent-misalignment risk through psychometric persona geometry, using Big Five, Dark Triad, evil, sycophancy, and Semantic Valence vectors plus causal ablation or amplification that shifts misalignment rates above 40% or below 3%.
- [MT-JailBench: A Modular Benchmark for Understanding Multi-Turn Jailbreak Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11002): Provides a modular benchmark for multi-turn jailbreak attacks, directly fitting agent and dialogue safety evaluation.
- [FragBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11029): Evaluates cross-session fragmented attacks against LLM systems. Core idea: split malicious goals into benign-looking fragments across sessions, then benchmark both adversarial rewriting and graph-based user-level detection over full attack trails.
- [Red-Teaming Agent Execution Contexts: Open-World Security Evaluation on OpenClaw](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11047): Evaluates security risks in mutable agent execution contexts. Core idea: optimize adversarial files, memory, tools, skills, and artifacts while preserving benign task utility.
- [Differential Privacy and Social Bias in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11195): Systematically evaluates how differential privacy changes LLM social-bias behavior, connecting privacy training with safety outcomes.
- [FlowSteer: Prompt-Only Workflow Steering Exposes Planning-Time Vulnerabilities in Multi-Agent LLM Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11514): What it evaluates: planning-time workflow-steering vulnerabilities in multi-agent LLM systems. Core idea: use prompt-only steering to redirect coordination before execution, testing defenses that only inspect final actions or messages.
- [Five Attacks on x402 Agentic Payment Protocol](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11781): Benchmarks or measures agent safety, prompt-injection, and guardrail reliability, providing a candidate reusable evaluation signal.
- [SkillSafetyBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12015): Evaluates agent safety under skill-facing attack surfaces. Core idea: treat third-party skills, local artifacts, and task materials as attack surfaces, checking whether agents exceed authority or execute dangerous workflows when invoking skills.
- [PreScam: A Benchmark for Predicting Scam Progression from Early Conversations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12243): Benchmarks whether models can predict scam progression from early multi-turn conversations using a scam kill chain and turn-level manipulation annotations.
- [GKnow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12299): Measures how factual gender cues and gender-bias behavior become entangled, adding a controlled bias-evaluation case for LLM safety.
- [Classifier Context Rot](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12366): Evaluates long-context degradation in monitors for dangerous coding-agent behavior. Core idea: test whether frontier model classifiers still detect subtle dangerous actions when they occur after hundreds of thousands of benign transcript tokens.
- [SafeManip](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12386): Evaluates temporal safety in robotic manipulation. Core idea: express reusable safety templates with finite-trace temporal logic and score full rollouts for hazards such as contact, release stability, cross-contamination, and action timing.
- [Ghost in the Context: Measuring Policy-Carriage Failures in Decision-Time Assembly](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12535): Measures policy-carriage failures caused by truncation, summarization, reordering, or rewriting of an agent decision state; the core idea is to audit whether control directives survive context assembly.
- [DisaBench: A Participatory Evaluation Framework for Disability Harms in Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12702): A participatory evaluation framework for disability harms in language models. Core idea: involve affected communities and measure harm patterns that generic safety benchmarks often miss.
- [Hierarchical Attacks for Multi-Modal Multi-Agent Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.13213): Adds a benchmark or evaluation suite for agent safety, centered on Hierarchical Attacks for Multi-Modal Multi-Agent Reasoning.
- [Backbone is All You Need: Assessing Vulnerabilities of Frozen Foundation Models in Synthetic Image Forensics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.13381): Evaluates synthetic-image forensic robustness with SIAA, a gray-box feature-space attack that needs only the detector's frozen ViT backbone and is tested across ViT detectors, few-shot settings, training-misalignment cases, and transfer scenarios.
- [Towards the Next Frontier of LLMs, Training on Private Data: A Cross-Domain Benchmark for Federated Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.13936): Benchmarks privacy-preserving federated LLM fine-tuning across healthcare and finance using MedQA, MedMCQA, FPB, and FiQA-SA under non-IID institutional splits with LoRA, QLoRA, and IA3 PEFT baselines.
- [EduFrameTrap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.14604): Benchmarks sycophancy risks in LLM tutors. Core idea: vary student confidence and social or authority pressure across educational subjects to test whether tutors preserve corrective feedback instead of agreeing with misconceptions.
- [MetaBackdoor: Exploiting Positional Encoding as a Backdoor Attack Surface in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.15172): Tests non-content LLM backdoors triggered by positional or length structure, including sensitive system-prompt disclosure, multi-turn self-activation into malicious tool calls, and composition with content-based triggers.
- [Privacy Evaluation of Generative Models for Trajectory Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.15246): Audits privacy in synthetic trajectory generation by applying membership-inference attacks to representative GAN, VAE, and diffusion trajectory models, showing that realistic mobility generation does not by itself remove training-data leakage risk.
- [DetectRL-X: Towards Reliable Multilingual and Real-World LLM-Generated Text Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.15518): Evaluates multilingual real-world LLM-generated text detection. Core idea: test detectors across languages, domains, commercial generators, AI-assisted writing operations, and paraphrase or perturbation attacks.
- [Detecting Privilege Escalation in Polyglot Microservices via Agentic Program Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.15569): Evaluates the Neo agentic program-analysis system on privilege-escalation detection across 25 open-source microservice applications, 7 languages, and 6.2M lines of code, reporting 24 zero-day findings with 81.0% precision and 85.0% recall.
- [Propagating Unsafe Actions in LLM Controlled Multi-Robot Collaboration via Single Robot Compromise](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.15641): Tests unsafe-action propagation in LLM-controlled multi-robot collaboration.
- [Beyond Content: A Comprehensive Speech Toxicity Dataset and Detection Framework Incorporating Paralinguistic Cues](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.15984): Introduces ToxiAlert-Bench, a 30K-plus audio toxicity dataset with 7 major categories, 20 fine-grained labels, text-versus-paralinguistic toxicity-source annotation, and Macro-F1 plus accuracy evaluation for speech safety classifiers.
- [Cross-Modal Prompt Injection via Image-Only Perturbation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.16090): Tests prompt-injection attacks against LVLMs through image-only perturbations, extending multimodal safety evaluation beyond visible text attacks.
- [Remembering More, Risking More: Longitudinal Safety Risks in Memory-Equipped LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17830): Introduces a trigger-probe protocol and NullMemory counterfactual baseline for measuring temporal memory contamination as safety risk accumulates across long-running memory-equipped agent deployments.
- [POLAR-Bench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.19127): Diagnoses privacy-utility trade-offs for LLM agents by letting a trusted model with a user privacy policy interact with an adversarial third-party model that probes for protected attributes while the task still needs useful disclosure.
- [Agent Meltdowns](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.19149): Defines accidental meltdown failures where benign environment errors trigger unsafe agent behavior, then injects simulated local and remote errors to measure meltdown rates across GPT-, Grok-, and Gemini-powered agents.
- [Can AI Guess What You Know? Performance Comparison of Large Language Models for Human Domain Knowledge Estimation From Communication Logs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22971): Evaluates whether LLMs can infer employee domain knowledge from 27,188 Slack messages, comparing seven models against self-reported skill ratings and exposing expertise-privacy risks in workplace logs.
- [WMAttack](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23220): Automates adversarial attack search for world-model agents through closed-loop rollouts, making robustness failures in predictive agent environments measurable rather than hand-picked.
- [When the Manual Lies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24069): Introduces MCP tool-description poisoning with 32 realistic test cases across six risk classes, testing whether covert malicious instructions in tool metadata can steer an agent's planning layer.
- [ContextEcho](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24279): Uses snapshot-then-probe identity tests during long tool-using coding sessions to measure persona drift, showing whether a deployed assistant's stated preferences and identity remain stable after thousands of tool turns.
- [Dual-Use AI Face Swap Apps Are Mostly Unsafe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24735): Systematically audits 420 iOS and Android face-swap apps and manually tests 155 eligible apps, finding that most lack safeguards against synthetic non-consensual intimate imagery despite platform removals of explicit nudification apps.
- [The Age of Curiosity Meets the Age of AI: Benchmarking Child Safety in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25510): Introduces KIDBench for child-facing LLM safety, using realistic ages 7-11 queries, single-turn prompts, multi-turn child-actor simulations, and a developmental-psychology-grounded LLM-as-judge rubric.
- [AgentSecBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26269): Instantiates a formal agent-security framework with games for instruction integrity, retrieval confidentiality, and capability control, measuring prompt injection, privacy leakage, and tool-use integrity in shared-channel LLM agents.
- [Benchmarking Open-Source Safety Guard Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.28830): Compares 14 open-source guard models on 79,331 safety-filtering samples aggregated across HarmBench, StrongREJECT, RealToxicityPrompts, and BeaverTails under eight NIST AI RMF safety categories.
- [ChiSafe-PAS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29667): Provides a human-annotated Chinese high-stakes safety benchmark that targets Pinyin romanization, character decomposition, internet slang, hedging tone, and other Chinese-specific evasion strategies.
- [Emergent Languages in Populations of Language Model Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.31170): Evaluates emergent communication as a safety monitoring risk. Core idea: use the Moltbook Files setting to show that LLM-agent populations can invent token-efficient or new natural-language codes, including oversight-evasion behavior that may not be visible from surface-output monitoring.
- [SABER](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.01317): Places coding agents in realistic stateful project workspaces and scores the final environment state plus violation causes, shifting safety evaluation from refusal text to action-sequence consequences.
- [Truthful AI Advisors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.01456): Turns the Crawford-Sobel cheap-talk setting into a pre-specified benchmark for advisor honesty under preference misalignment, testing whether sales, recommendation, or negotiation-style agents stay truthful when payoff conflicts with the user.
- [Gate AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02959): Defines a detector-evaluation harness for prompt-injection and jailbreak security benchmarks that fixes operating points with cross-validation instead of tuning thresholds separately for each dataset.
- [CogManip](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06099): Evaluates covert psychological manipulation across 15 strategy risks and 1,000 multi-turn interaction scenarios, targeting dynamic persuasion failures that static harmful-prompt tests miss.
- [SopriBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06784): Benchmarks user-level multimodal privacy leakage from social media posts, where weak cues across text, images, and metadata jointly expose home, workplace, or routine information beyond binary PII detection.
- [MalSkillBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07131): Provides 3,944 runtime-verified malicious agent skills over a 108-cell taxonomy, with Docker sandboxing and system-call monitoring to evaluate hybrid prompt-and-code supply-chain scanners.
- [Temporal Dynamics of Memory Poisoning in Web3-Style LLM Agents](https://doi.org/10.1109/access.2026.3693560): Studies temporal dynamics of memory poisoning in Web3-style LLM agents.
- [Beyond the Model Architecture: Uncovering Environmental Vulnerabilities in Large Language Model Deployment](https://doi.org/10.1109/icerai69511.2026.11494593): A reusable benchmark, dataset, evaluation protocol, or diagnostic study for agent safety and security. Core idea: Published in 2026 International Conference on Electrical/Electronics, Robotics, Artificial Intelligence, and Informatics (ICERAI); metadata title: Beyond the Model Architecture: Uncovering Environmental Vulnerabilities in Large Language Model Deployment.
- [On the AI Agents Audit Model](https://doi.org/10.1109/smartindustrycon68821.2026.11493110): Proposes an audit model for AI agents.
- [There is No War in Ba Sing Se: A Global Analysis of Content Moderation in Large Language Models](https://doi.org/10.14722/ndss.2026.240593): Analyzes global content moderation behavior in large language models.
- [Colorism in Multimodal AI: An Empirical Exploration of Socioeconomic Linguistic Bias in Text-to-Image Generation](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2026.eacl-srw.69): Adds a benchmark or evaluation artifact for agent safety: empirical bias evaluation for text-to-image generation.
- [Zero-shot detection of LLM-generated text via multilevel information compression and multidimensional feature fusion](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neucom.2026.132731): Adds a benchmark or evaluation artifact for agent safety: zero-shot detection method for LLM-generated text.
- [SocialNormVR: Designing Symmetrical Reality Testbeds for AI Agent Social Norm Evaluation](https://doi.org/10.1109/vrw70859.2026.00281): Adds a benchmark, dataset, evaluation protocol, or testbed for agent safety, risk control, prompt injection, privacy, and harmful-content evaluation.
- [EHLD: a general data framework for harmful language detection and evaluation](https://doi.org/10.1108/dlp-01-2026-0028): Adds a benchmark, dataset, evaluation protocol, or testbed for agent safety, risk control, prompt injection, privacy, and harmful-content evaluation.

Rethinking Privacy Protection in Large Language Models](https://doi.org/10.56553/popets-2026-0009): Evaluates LLM-based text sanitization with adversarial privacy inference and entropy-based leakage metrics, distinguishing genuine privacy preservation from transformations that only mislead classifiers.

- [CAIS AI Dashboard](https://dashboard.safe.ai/): Tracks AI safety and capability risk indicators through public dashboard views such as the Risk Index, capability index, and automation signals. Core idea: expose an always-current safety overview that connects benchmark progress with risk monitoring rather than treating safety scores as isolated rows.
- [Beyond Goodhart's Law: Dynamic Compliance Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07805): Dynamically evaluates procedural compliance in multi-agent systems, using adversarial tasks where agents can maximize rewards by strategically violating rules rather than following operational constraints.
- [Who Pays the Price?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13385): Reframes web-agent prompt-injection benchmarking around stakeholder-dependent harms, measuring who is harmed by injected actions rather than only whether an attack technically succeeds.
- [WebDecept](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13686): Injects configurable e-commerce deceptive interface patterns such as targeted ads, domain redirection, and shopping manipulation into web environments to measure web-agent safety under realistic dark patterns.
- [SkillMutator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14154): Benchmarks language-and-code cross-modal attacks on agent skills, where a benign SKILL.md description can conflict with executable scripts, and evaluates scanner and defense behavior on that hybrid attack surface.
- [OSGuard](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.15034): Adds action-level guardrail decisions and a risk-augmented execution suite for computer-use agents, testing unsafe shortcuts under benign user instructions rather than obvious malicious prompts.
- [MCPTox](https://doi.org/10.1609/aaai.v40i42.40895): Evaluates tool poisoning on real-world MCP servers. Core idea: turn malicious tool descriptions and server-side behavior into a benchmark protocol so MCP agents can be tested for whether they select, trust, and execute poisoned tools.
- [Evaluating Goal Drift in Language Model Agents](https://doi.org/10.1609/aies.v8i1.36541): Evaluates whether language-model agents maintain assigned objectives over long autonomous contexts. Core idea: measure goal adherence over more than 100,000 tokens and expose gradual drift toward pattern-matching behaviors.
- [Source framing triggers systematic bias](https://doi.org/10.1126/sciadv.adz2924): Evaluates how cited or implied sources shift LLM judgments. Core idea: treat source framing as a controlled safety variable that can induce systematic bias even when task content is otherwise similar.
